PART I

Exam Blueprint & EPFO Structure
The post, the paper, the people — know your battlefield

Chapter 1 — APFC Post & Exam Pattern
The Assistant Provident Fund Commissioner (APFC) is a Group A Gazetted post under the Employees'
Provident Fund Organisation (EPFO), Ministry of Labour & Employment, recruited through UPSC. The APFC
is the cutting-edge officer for enforcement of the EPF & MP Act, 1952 — the person who signs Section
7A orders, hears quasi-judicial cases, and represents EPFO before tribunals and High Courts.
Understanding the exam structure precisely is the first step to cracking it.

held on 30 November 2025. Total selection = 300 (written) + 100 (interview) = 400 marks.

1.4 Score Calculation — worked example

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 2 · Verified April 2026

FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Score = (Correct × 2.5) − (Wrong × 0.833) − (Un-attempted × 0)
Example A: 90 correct, 20 wrong, 10 blank → (90×2.5) − (20×0.833) = 225 − 16.66 = 208.34 / 300
Example B: 100 correct, 10 wrong, 10 blank → (100×2.5) − (10×0.833) = 250 − 8.33 = 241.67 / 300
Guess rule of thumb: attempt only if you can eliminate at least 2 of 4 options — expected value turns positive

ORDER OF ATTACK
• Round 1 (0–45 min): sweep and answer every
sure-shot (your strongest 4 subjects)
• Round 2 (45–90 min): two-option eliminable
questions in Labour Laws / Social Security
• Round 3 (90–110 min): Polity, Economy, Current
Affairs
• Round 4 (110–120 min): bubble review; do NOT
guess blindly in this window

TACTICS
• Target 95+ attempts with <12% error rate for 230+
score
• Leave: English RC (if slow), Quant word problems
that eat >2 min each
• Skip pattern matching: if 3 options look similar and
none feel right → blank
• Bring two black ball pens. Fill OMR carefully — stray
marks = wrong

MNEMONIC
SILCA — Social Security · Industrial Laws · Labour Codes · Current Affairs · Accounting (the
five subjects that decide your rank)
QUICK RECALL

Chapter 2 — EPFO — Organisation & Governance
EPFO — the Employees' Provident Fund Organisation — is a statutory body under the Ministry of
Labour & Employment, administering three schemes framed under a single parent Act (EPF & MP Act,
1952). With ~7 crore active members and a corpus exceeding ₹24 lakh crore, it is among the largest
social security organisations in the world. Its governing body is the tripartite Central Board of Trustees
(CBT).

2.1 The Three Schemes — one Act, three pillars

EPF 1952

Figure 2.2 — Five-tier officer hierarchy; APFC is the entry-level Group A field officer

✔ KEY FACT
CPFC is appointed by the Central Government under Section 5D of the EPF Act. The CPFC is the Chief
Executive of the Fund and is empowered to delegate any function to subordinate officers — this delegation is
what gives an APFC the authority to pass Section 7A orders.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
CBT is a tripartite body — 1/3 government, 1/3 employers, 1/3 employees. This is exam-favourite: don't
confuse with ESIC which is also tripartite but has different numbers. CBT recommends the EPF interest rate —
final approval is from the Ministry of Finance.

2.4 APFC — Core Duties at Regional / Sub-Regional Office
Function

What the APFC actually does

Enforcement

Inspect establishments; detect default; issue show-cause notices u/s 7A

Quasi-Judicial (7A)

Hold hearings with civil-court powers; determine amount due; pass speaking orders

Recovery (Sec 8–8G)

Issue recovery certificates; attach property; act as Recovery Officer

Prosecution

Sanction prosecution u/s 14/14A; appear before Magistrates courts

Exemptions (Sec 17)

Process applications; monitor exempted trusts' compliance

Grievance Redressal

Resolve UAN / PF / pension complaints via EPFiGMS / CPGRAMS

Claim Supervision

Oversee settlement (Form 19/10C/10D/5IF) within statutory deadlines

International Workers (IW)

Verify Social Security Agreements (SSA) certificates; issue COCs

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 5 · Verified April 2026

PART II

EPF & MP Act, 1952
Act No. 19 of 1952 — the mother legislation

✔ KEY FACT
Big Picture — Why the EPF Act exists: In 1952, India had no formal old-age savings mechanism for
private-sector workers. Without law, employers would either not save at all, or deduct money from wages and
spend it on business. The Act solves this by compelling a monthly saving (12% from employee, 12% from
employer) into a protected, interest-earning fund that follows the worker across jobs (via UAN), pays out at
retirement (EPF), funds a lifetime pension (EPS), and gives life cover while in service (EDLI). The Act's
'enforcement chain' (S.7A → 7Q → 14B → 8B) exists because left to themselves, defaulting employers harm
exactly the workers who can least afford the loss. Every section and every penalty you'll read in this Part
is designed with that logic.

Chapter 3 — Applicability — All Four Routes
Applicability means: to whom does the EPF & MP Act, 1952 apply? The Act uses four routes: automatic
coverage under Section 1(3)(a) and 1(3)(b), voluntary coverage under Section 1(4), continued
coverage under Section 1(5), and clubbing under Section 2A. Excluded establishments are listed in
Section 16. Master these four routes — most exam questions frame a fact-pattern and ask which
sub-section triggers coverage.

3.1 Applicability Flow — decision tree
Factory employing 20+ persons in Schedule I industry?
→ 1(3)(a) automatic

Any other establishment employing 20+ (notified)?
→ 1(3)(b) automatic

Employer + majority of employees apply voluntarily?
→ 1(4) voluntary

Once covered, head-count falls below 20?
→ 1(5) continues to apply

Multiple branches / departments of same employer?
→ 2A clubbed as one

Falls under Section 16 excluded list?
→ NOT covered

Figure 3.1 — Applicability decision tree

3.3 Section 2A Clubbing — worked example
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Scenario: "ABC Restaurants Pvt Ltd" operates 3 outlets — Delhi (8 staff), Mumbai (7 staff), Bangalore (6 staff). In
isolation, none meets the 20-employee threshold.
Section 2A treats all three as one establishment because they share a common proprietor/ownership and
common brand.
Combined head-count = 8 + 7 + 6 = 21 employees → crosses 20 → coverage triggered under Section
1(3)(b) for all three outlets.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
An employer cannot escape coverage by fragmenting the business into smaller units. Section 2A is a strict
anti-avoidance provision; EPFO looks at functional integrality, not legal form.

MNEMONIC
1-3-a, 1-3-b, 1-4, 1-5, 2A → A-B-V-C-C (Automatic factory · Beyond factory · Voluntary ·
Continued · Clubbed)
QUICK RECALL
Coverage threshold: 20+ employees
Automatic coverage: Sec 1(3)(a) factories; Sec 1(3)(b) others notified
Voluntary coverage: Sec 1(4) — both employer and majority of employees must apply
Continuity rule: Sec 1(5) — coverage persists even if count drops below 20
Clubbing: Sec 2A — functional integrality, not separate legal entities

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 7 · Verified April 2026

Exclusions: Sec 16 — co-operatives, Govt establishments, apprentices

Chapter 4 — Definitions — Section 2 (All Critical Terms)
Section 2 is the dictionary of the EPF Act. Every word in the Act must be read through these definitions.
The single most exam-relevant definition is Basic Wages — the Surya Roshni SC judgment (28 Feb 2019)
redefined what allowances fall within it, adding billions to employer liability overnight.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — EPF Act Section 2 Definitions | Tier 1 (1-2 marks)
MEMORIZE — Substance + Section #: 'Basic Wages' [Sec 2(b)] — Surya Roshni 2019: includes special
allowances that are universal & ordinarily paid · 'Employee' [Sec 2(f)] — any person employed for wages
directly or via contractor · 'Employer' [Sec 2(e)] — owner or occupier · 'Wages' = Basic + DA + Retaining
Allowance (EXCLUDES HRA, OT, bonus, employer PF) · 'Establishment' — factory or any class of
establishments notified
SKIP: All sub-clauses (a), (b), (c) by number unless they're famous · Every word verbatim · 'Member of family'
detailed list — just know it covers spouse, children, parents · 'Notified order' procedural details
TRAP: 'Basic Wages' definition POST-Surya Roshni is what's tested — includes universally-paid allowances.
Don't confuse with old (narrower) definition. Memorize Sec 2(b) and Sec 2(f) numbers since they're iconic; SKIP
the rest.

4.1 Key Definitions (alphabetical)
Term (Section)

Meaning — as used in the Act

Appropriate Govt [2(a)]

Central Govt for establishments under its authority, railways, mines, major ports;
State Govt otherwise

Authorised Officer [2(aa)]

CPFC, Addl CPFC, Dy PFC, RPFC or APFC authorised for a specific function

Basic Wages [2(b)]

All emoluments earned while on duty (whether in cash or kind), EXCLUDING the 8
items listed in 4.2 below

Employee [2(f)]

Any person employed for wages in or in connection with the work of an establishment
(direct or through contractor), excluding apprentices

Employer [2(e)]

Owner/occupier in relation to a factory; in relation to any other establishment, the
person who has the ultimate control

Excluded Employee [Para 2(f)
EPF Scheme]

Member who has withdrawn the full amount, or whose pay exceeds ₹15,000 at the
time of first joining (joining high-wage employee)

Exempted Establishment [2(fff)]

Establishment granted exemption u/s 17 to run its own approved trust

Fund [2(h)]

The Employees' Provident Fund established under Section 5 of the Act

Recovery Officer [2(lb)]

Officer authorised by CG u/s 8B to execute recovery certificates

Superannuation [2(ll)]

Attainment by an employee of the age of 58 years

4.2 Basic Wages — Included vs Excluded (the exam favourite)
INCLUDED in Basic Wages

EXCLUDED from Basic Wages

• Basic salary (core component)
• Dearness Allowance (DA) — always included
• Cash value of food concession
• Retaining allowance (for retained employees during
layoff)
• Any universal, ordinarily paid allowance per Surya
Roshni 2019

• Cash value of food concession already counted? NO
— once only
• House Rent Allowance (HRA)
• Overtime allowance
• Bonus and commission (if not universal)
• Presents made by employer
• Conveyance allowance

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 9 · Verified April 2026

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Surya Roshni vs EPFO (Supreme Court, 28 Feb 2019): all universally paid, ordinarily paid, regular
allowances are part of Basic Wages for PF. The burden is on the employer to prove an allowance is
variable/special. This case is a near-certainty in APFC exams.

4.3 Solved Example — Computing Basic Wages
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Salary slip: Basic ₹8,000 + DA ₹4,000 + HRA ₹3,000 + Conveyance ₹1,600 + Special Allowance ₹5,000 + Bonus
(monthly) ₹1,000
Step 1: Basic + DA = 8,000 + 4,000 = ₹12,000 (always in)
Step 2: HRA 3,000 and Conveyance 1,600 → EXCLUDED
Step 3: Special Allowance 5,000 — if universally paid to all → INCLUDE (Surya Roshni); if variable → EXCLUDE.
Assume universal.
Step 4: Monthly bonus 1,000 — if paid to all regularly, INCLUDE. Assume yes.
PF Basic Wages = 8,000 + 4,000 + 5,000 + 1,000 = ₹18,000 (capped at ₹15,000 for statutory PF, but
actual liability is on 18,000 if employer has not opted for cap)

MNEMONIC
BAD-HRA-COB → Basic + Allowances universal + DA = IN; HRA, Retainer exceptions, Admin,
Conveyance, OT, Bonus = OUT
QUICK RECALL
Basic Wages test: Universal + ordinary + regular → included (Surya Roshni 2019)
Always-in: Basic + DA + food concession + retaining allowance
Always-out: HRA + OT + bonus (non-universal) + gifts + conveyance
Superannuation: Age 58 — Sec 2(ll)
Excluded Employee: Wage > ₹15,000 at first joining, OR full withdrawal done
Appropriate Government: Central for mines/railways/ports; State otherwise

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 10 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 5 — Contribution Structure
Seven separate money flows operate under the EPF umbrella. The student's task is to keep them straight
— who pays, at what rate, capped at what wage, going into which account. This is the
most-tested numerical chapter of the whole syllabus.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — EPF Contribution Structure | Tier 1 (2-3 marks)
MEMORIZE — these 6 numbers are GOLD: ₹15,000 = wage ceiling · 12% + 12% = employee + employer ·
8.33% to EPS (cap ₹1,250) · 3.67% to EPF (= 12% − 8.33%) · 0.50% EDLI (employer extra) · 0.50% Admin
(employer extra; was 1.10%, reduced 2017)
SKIP: Pre-2014 contribution history · Specific account numbers (A/c 1, A/c 2, A/c 10, A/c 21, A/c 22) unless asked
verbatim · Detailed Para 32 formula derivations
TRAP — The EPS Cap: Employer's 8.33% goes to EPS ONLY UP TO ₹15,000 wage. So if salary = ₹20K, EPS gets
₹1,250 (capped); the extra ₹417 (8.33% × 5K) goes to EPF, NOT EPS. Many candidates miss this — it explains
why 'higher pension' becomes attractive only for high earners.

5.1 The Seven Flows — complete map
#

Administration

—

*Employer total on EPF side = 12%. Of this, 8.33% diverts to EPS (capped) and the rest — not a fixed
3.67% — lands in EPF. Over ₹15,000 wages, the full 12% may stay in EPF if the employee is not
contributing to EPS above the cap.

5.2 Employer 12% Split — visualised
Employer's 12% — Split Between EPF & EPS

3.67% → EPF

8.33% → EPS

Figure 5.1 — Employer's 12% is split 3.67% EPF + 8.33% EPS (EPS portion capped at ₹1,250/month on ₹15,000 wage)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 11 · Verified April 2026

5.3 Worked Example — Total Outflow on ₹15,000 Wage
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Wage = ₹15,000/month (exactly at the EPS cap).
Employee share: 12% × 15,000 = ₹1,800 → entirely to EPF A/c.
Employer EPS: 8.33% × 15,000 = ₹1,250 → EPS Pension Fund.
Employer EPF: 12% − 8.33% = 3.67% × 15,000 = ₹550 → EPF A/c.
Employer EDLI: 0.50% × 15,000 = ₹75 → EDLI Fund.
Employer Admin (EPF): 0.50% × 15,000 = ₹75.
Central Govt EPS subsidy: 1.16% × 15,000 = ₹174.
Total monthly cost to employer (excl. subsidy) = 1,800 + 1,250 + 550 + 75 + 75 = ₹3,750 on a
₹15,000 wage (25% loading).

⚠ EXAM ALERT
The EPS wage ceiling is ₹15,000. Above this, employer's EPS share stays frozen at ₹1,250/month — any
excess 12% stays in the EPF account (not EPS). This is why pensions capped at EPS cap are so low — the Sunil
Kumar SC 2022 judgment addressed this via 'higher pension'.

MNEMONIC
8.33 goes to PENSION · 3.67 stays in EPF · together they make 12
QUICK RECALL
Employee share: 12% of wages (no cap)
Employer EPF+EPS: 12% total — split 3.67% + 8.33%
EPS wage cap: ₹15,000/month (max EPS employer ₹1,250)
CG EPS subsidy: 1.16% of wages up to ₹15,000
EDLI: 0.50% employer, max ₹75/month
Admin — EPF: 0.50% of wages, min ₹75 (₹25 dormant)
Admin — EDLI: NIL (waived since 2017-18)

Visual — Employer's 12% Contribution, Sliced
Employer's 12% (capped wage ₹15,000 for EPS)

EPS — 8.33%

EPF — 3.67%

⚠ EXAM ALERT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 12 · Verified April 2026

LAST-MINUTE REVISION — EPF Contribution Structure
Q1: EPF wage ceiling? → ₹15,000/month (statutory)
Q2: Employee contribution? → 12% of wages (all to EPF)
Q3: Employer's 12% split? → 8.33% to EPS + 3.67% to EPF
Q4: Max EPS contribution? → ₹1,250/month (8.33% × ₹15,000 cap)
Q5: EDLI rate? → 0.50% by EMPLOYER ONLY (additional, capped ₹75)
Q6: Admin charge? → 0.50% by employer (reduced from 1.10% in 2017)
Q7: 'Wages' definition? → Basic + DA + Retaining Allowance (EXCLUDES HRA, OT, bonus)
Q8: Total employer outflow? → 13% (12% + 0.50% EDLI + 0.50% Admin)
Q9: Basic wages test post-Surya Roshni 2019? → 'Universal + ordinary + regular' allowances INCLUDED
Q10: Wage > ₹15K — where does excess 8.33% go? → To EPF (NOT EPS — EPS capped at ₹1,250)
EXAM-EVE CHECKLIST:
□ Drill the 7 money flows (Employee 12% + Employer 12% split + EDLI + Admin)
□ EPS cap ₹1,250 = 8.33% × ₹15,000 (memorize this calculation)
□ Surya Roshni 2019 = key case for basic wages definition

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 13 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 6 — EPF Interest Rate
The EPF interest rate is declared annually by the Central Board of Trustees (CBT) and concurred by the
Ministry of Finance. Interest is compounded monthly but credited yearly after 31 March. For FY
2025-26, the CBT retained 8.25% at its 239th meeting — the third consecutive year at this rate.

Visual — CBT Tripartite Structure (Section 5A)
Chairman — Union
Labour Minister

≤5 Central Govt

≤15 State Govt

10 Employer reps

10 Employee reps

Member-Secretary
(CPFC) — CEO of EPFO

CBT is a tripartite body — Government + Employers + Employees — that governs EPFO. Its two
sub-bodies are the Executive Committee (S.5AA) for day-to-day matters and Regional Committees
(S.5B) at the State level.

Minister. Once ratified by MoF, credit to ~7 crore subscriber accounts typically completes by June–August
2026.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
EPF interest is tax-free only up to ₹2.5 lakh annual employee contribution (Finance Act 2021 threshold;
increased to ₹5 lakh if employer does not contribute). Above that, interest on the excess is taxable as Income
from Other Sources.

MNEMONIC
8.65 → 8.55 → 8.65 → 8.50 → 8.50 → 8.10 → 8.15 → 8.25 → 8.25 → 8.25 — the COVID dip (8.10%)
is the trivia-question sweet spot
QUICK RECALL

Decided by: CBT (recommendation) + Ministry of Finance (approval)
Compounding: Monthly; credited yearly after 31 March
Tax-free limit: Employee contribution ≤ ₹2.5 lakh p.a. (₹5L if no employer)

Historical high (recent): 8.65% (FY 2016-17 and 2018-19)

Chapter 7 — Enforcement — Sections 7A, 7B, 7C, 7Q
Enforcement is the core work of the APFC. Section 7A gives the quasi-judicial power to determine money
due; 7B allows the same officer to review his own order; 7C deals with escaped amount (a
later-discovered default); 7Q levies interest at 12% p.a. on all defaulted sums. These four sections
together are the engine room of recovery.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — EPF Enforcement Sec 7A/7B/7C/7Q | Tier 1 (2-3 marks — APFC core)
MEMORIZE — Section #s ARE iconic here (this is YOUR job): Sec 7A — quasi-judicial inquiry; reasonable
opportunity (show-cause notice); civil court powers (summons, oath, documents) · Sec 7B — review by SAME
officer of his own 7A order (within 60 days) · Sec 7C — re-opening for escaped amount; 4-year limitation · Sec
7Q — 12% p.a. interest on delayed contributions · Appeal: CGIT (Central Govt Industrial Tribunal) within 60
days; 25% pre-deposit (now under SS Code)
SKIP: Procedural minutiae of inquiry · Specific show-cause notice templates · Detailed discovery procedures ·
Form numbers
TRAP: Don't confuse 7A (initial inquiry) with 7B (review by same officer). And both are different from 14B
(damages) which is a SEPARATE proceeding. 7Q = INTEREST (always 12%, mandatory); 14B = DAMAGES
(5/10/15/25% based on delay).

7.1 Section 7A — Step by Step
Default detected (inspection / return mismatch / complaint)

Show-cause notice — Section 7A(1)
Reasonable opportunity

Hearing with civil-court powers
Summons · evidence · cross-examination

Speaking Order — amount determined
Can enforce personal presence

Appeal to CGIT — Section 7-I
30 days · 25% pre-deposit

Figure 7.1 — 7A quasi-judicial process end-to-end

7.2 Interest under Section 7Q — always 12%
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
7Q Interest = Outstanding amount × 12% p.a. × (days of default ÷ 365)
Example: Unpaid PF = ₹1,00,000; default period = 180 days
Interest 7Q = 1,00,000 × 0.12 × 180/365 = ₹5,917.81
7Q is mandatory and applies automatically — no discretion.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 16 · Verified April 2026

CRITICAL: 7Q (interest) is SEPARATE from and CUMULATIVE with 14B (damages). Both are levied on the same
default simultaneously.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
7Q interest ≠ 14B damages. 7Q is 12% p.a. interest — compensating the fund for time value. 14B is penal
damages. Under the June 2024 notification, damages were unified at 1%/month. 7Q was not changed; it
remains 12% p.a. simple.

7.3 Damages under 14B — Before vs After 15 June 2024
BEFORE 14 June 2024 (para 32A)

AFTER 15 June 2024 (amended)

• Slab-based: 5% p.a. (0–2 months) → 10% (2–4 m) →
15% (4–6 m) → 25% (>6 m)
• Capped at the defaulted amount itself
• Applied separately to EPF, EPS, EDLI with different
slabs
• Example: 8-month default on ₹1,00,000 → 25% ×
1,00,000 = ₹25,000 (capped)

• Uniform 1% per month of default (12% p.a.
equivalent)
• No cap — damages accrue till clearance
• Applied identically across EPF, EPS, EDLI (unified
schedule)
• Example: 8-month default on ₹1,00,000 → 8 × 1%
× 1,00,000 = ₹8,000 (no cap)

7.4 Cumulative Example — same default, everything stacked
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Default: ₹1,00,000 PF unpaid for 30 months (post-15 June 2024 period).
7Q Interest = 1,00,000 × 12% × 30/12 = ₹30,000
14B Damages = 1,00,000 × 1% × 30 = ₹30,000
Criminal prosecution u/s 14 possible (up to 3 years imprisonment).
Total monetary liability = Principal ₹1,00,000 + ₹30,000 + ₹30,000 = ₹1,60,000
(+ potential criminal sentence if 14AB applies — deducted but not deposited)

MNEMONIC
7-A, 7-B, 7-C, 7-Q → Adjudicate · Back-review · Caught-later · Quantify-interest
QUICK RECALL
Sec 7A: Determine amount due; civil court powers; speaking order
Sec 7B: Review of own 7A order
Sec 7C: Escaped amount — later-discovered default
Sec 7Q: Interest @ 12% p.a. simple; cumulative with 14B
14B damages: 1%/month (post-15 Jun 2024); earlier slab 5-25% capped
Appeal: CGIT — Central Govt Industrial Tribunal (30 days, 25% pre-deposit)

Q1: APFC's quasi-judicial power? → Section 7A (determination of dues)
Q2: Powers under 7A? → Civil court powers (summon witnesses, oath, documents, affidavits)
Q3: Review by SAME officer? → Section 7B (within 60 days of original 7A order)
Q4: Re-opening for escaped amount? → Section 7C (4-year limitation)
Q5: Interest on delayed contributions? → Section 7Q — 12% p.a. simple interest (MANDATORY)
Q6: Appeal to where? → CGIT (Central Govt Industrial Tribunal) within 60 days

Q8: Burden of proof? → On EMPLOYER (Surya Roshni 2019)
Q9: 7A vs 14B? → 7A = DETERMINATION of dues; 14B = DAMAGES proceeding (separate)
Q10: 7Q vs 14B? → 7Q = INTEREST (always 12%); 14B = DAMAGES (slab 5/10/15/25%)

□ This is THE APFC's defining power — memorize Sections cold
□ 7A inquiry = your job description in one section
□ 7Q (interest 12%) + 14B (damages) + Principal = THREE separate amounts on default

Chapter 8 — Recovery — Sections 8, 10, 11, 12
Once a 7A order determines the amount due, the Recovery Officer steps in under Sections 8, 8A-8G.
Recovery may be done as arrears of land revenue — a strong legal weapon. Section 10 protects the
employee's PF corpus from attachment by other creditors. Section 11 gives PF dues priority over all
other debts in insolvency. Section 12 bars employers from reducing wages to adjust their PF
contribution.

✔ KEY FACT
Section 10 + Section 11 are the worker-protection twins. Section 10 keeps the corpus safe from
attachment by any court or decree. Section 11 places PF dues at the top of the waterfall during insolvency —
above even secured creditors for the dues portion.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Section 12 — no employer shall reduce wages or any privileges/benefits to which an employee is entitled in
order to adjust his PF contribution. Any attempt to pass the burden back onto the worker is a direct statutory
violation and attracts criminal penalty.

Chapter 9 — Penalties — Sections 14, 14A, 14AB, 14B
Penalties under the EPF Act span criminal, civil, and composite liabilities. Section 14 lists criminal
offences. Section 14A fixes corporate liability (directors/managers). Section 14AB — inserted by Act 33 of
1988 — makes deduction without deposit a cognizable offence (notwithstanding CrPC). Section 14B
is the civil damages provision recalibrated in June 2024.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — EPF Penalties Sec 14/14A/14B | Tier 1 (1-2 marks)
MEMORIZE: Sec 14 — Criminal: imprisonment + fine for default · Sec 14A — Corporate liability:
directors/managers personally liable if company defaults · Sec 14AB — Deduction-without-deposit =
COGNIZABLE offence (1988 amendment) · Sec 14B — DAMAGES SLAB: <2 months = 5% pa, 2-4 mo = 10%,
4-6 mo = 15%, >6 mo = 25% (capped at 100%)
SKIP: Specific imprisonment durations (3 months / 1 year / 3 years) unless directly tested · Compounding
procedural details · Section 14C wording verbatim
TRAP: 14B (DAMAGES) and 7Q (INTEREST) are SEPARATE — both levied together. Damages = penalty for delay;
Interest = compensation for time-value of money. An employer in default pays BOTH (damages + 12% interest
+ the principal contribution).

Chapter 10 — Exemptions — Section 17 & Schedule III
Section 17 allows the Appropriate Government to exempt an establishment or class of employees from
the EPF scheme, provided the employer runs its own approved trust offering benefits not less
favourable than the statutory scheme. Exemption is a privilege — it can be (and frequently is) revoked.
Schedule III lays down the ongoing conditions every exempted trust must follow.

10.2 Schedule III Conditions (every exempted trust must meet)

✓ Board of Trustees — equal worker representation
✓ Investment pattern as per Ministry of Finance notifications
✓ Trustees jointly & severally liable for mismanagement
✓ Annual audit by CA; CAG-empanelled auditor acceptable
✓ Interest on PF balance ≥ statutory rate declared by CBT
✓ Cannot reduce benefits unilaterally
✓ Returns filed with RPFC; inspection allowed
✓ Exemption revocable on breach — back to statutory PF

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Madras HC, BHEL Trichy (September 2025): An establishment exempted from EPF is NOT automatically
exempted from EPS. EPS is a separate scheme framed under Sec 6A of the Act; exemption under EPS needs a
separate notification by the Central Government. This confusion is a favourite exam trick.

✔ KEY FACT
Exempted establishments continue to pay: (a) EPS contribution (8.33% capped at ₹1,250), (b) EDLI
contribution (0.50%, capped), (c) EPF administrative charges. Only the EPF provident fund portion is
self-managed by the trust.

Connecting the Dots — How the EPF Act's provisions fit together
After 8 chapters, the Act may feel like a jigsaw. Here is the one-paragraph mental model that holds every
section together: The EPF Act is a contract of trust between worker, employer and State, with
five moving parts. (1) Who is covered — S.1 + S.2(f) define applicability + employee. (2) What is
contributed — S.6 prescribes 12% + 12% on 'basic wages' (S.2(b) as interpreted by Surya Roshni). (3)
Where money goes — split via S.6A (EPS), S.6C (EDLI) and the EPF Scheme. (4) If the employer
defaults — the State enforces via a four-stage chain: S.7A determines dues → S.7Q adds 12% interest →
S.14B adds 1%/month damages → S.8B recovers via certificate with attachment/arrest. (5) If the
employer is better than statute — S.17 allows exemption subject to Schedule-III conditions. CBT (S.5A)
governs the whole system; APFC (S.5D) is the frontline enforcer. If you can recite these five moving parts
from memory, you understand the Act's architecture — everything else is detail.

Part-II Drill — EPF Scheme 1952 Advance Paragraphs (Para 68 series)
While the Act governs contributions & enforcement, the EPF Scheme 1952 (framed under S.5) governs
what a member can take out. The Para 68 series permits partial non-refundable advances for specific
life events. Auto-settlement limit raised to ₹5 lakh (EPFO Press Release 24-Jun-2025), up from ₹1 lakh
earlier. Covers Para 68J (medical), 68B (housing), 68K (marriage/education).

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 26 · Verified April 2026

PART III

Employees' Pension Scheme, 1995
The pillar that funds every retired EPF member's monthly pension

✔ KEY FACT
Big Picture — Why EPS exists & why the formula is what it is: Before 1995, EPF gave only a lump sum at
retirement. A worker might outlive that lump sum by 10-15 years and end up destitute. EPS 1995 solves this by
diverting part of employer's EPF share into a separate pension fund (8.33% of capped wage), matched
by a Government top-up (1.16%), which funds a life-long monthly pension computed as: (Pensionable Salary
× Pensionable Service) ÷ 70. The '70' is an actuarial constant — chosen so that, across an expected 15-20 year
retirement, the contributions + investment returns exactly fund the promise. EPS is a Defined Benefit scheme:
you get the formula pension, regardless of how well the corpus does (the State/EPFO bears the risk — hence the
~₹70K cr actuarial deficit). The 2022 Sunil Kumar ruling lets members who contributed on ACTUAL (above-cap)
wages claim a PROPORTIONALLY higher pension — undoing the ₹15,000 cap for eligible legacy members.
Tables A/B/C/D handle edge cases: A+B for pre-1995 past-service, C for early pension, D for withdrawal
(service < 10 yr).

Chapter 11 — EPS-95 Architecture
The Employees' Pension Scheme, 1995 (EPS-95) replaced the Family Pension Scheme 1971. It is
framed under Section 6A of the EPF Act. EPS provides a defined-benefit monthly pension to
members on superannuation, invalidity, and to their family in the event of death. It is funded from the
employer's 8.33% share (diverted out of the 12% EPF contribution) plus a Central Government
subsidy of 1.16% of wages up to ₹15,000. Keep the formula in 12 and the nine pension types in the
other hand — those two ideas carry most of the chapter's marks.

Ch-11 Drill — International Workers (IWs) & Social Security Agreements
An International Worker (IW) under EPF Act is (a) an Indian employee who has worked in an SSA
country + holds a Certificate of Coverage (CoC), OR (b) an expatriate in India working for an EPF-covered
establishment. Special rules apply — no wage ceiling, different withdrawal rules.

✔ KEY FACT
Certificate of Coverage (CoC) is the cornerstone document. Issued by EPFO to an Indian employee detached
to an SSA country — it exempts him from the host country's social-security contribution for the detachment
period (typically up to 60 months). Analogous document from foreign SSA countries exempts their nationals
from Indian EPF.

CoC validity: Up to 60 months (detachment)
India-US SSA: Under negotiation (not signed)

Chapter 12 — Pension Formula & Calculation
Every EPS-95 member's monthly pension comes from a single formula. Master this one equation and you
can answer every pension-computation question on any past APFC paper. The trick is three small rules
that modify the formula: the +2-year service bonus if service exceeds 20 years, the early-pension
reduction of 4% per year before 58, and the statutory floor of ₹1,000 per month.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — EPS Pension Formula | Tier 1 (1-2 marks)
MEMORIZE — Numbers only, NOT paragraph numbers: (Pensionable Salary × Pensionable Service) ÷
70 · +2 year bonus if service strictly > 20 years (NOT = 20) · 4% reduction per year below 58 (early
pension) · 4% increase per year above 58 up to 60 (deferred) · ₹1,000/month floor (since 1 Sept 2014) ·
Min 10 years service for pension; else Withdrawal Benefit (Table D)
SKIP: Paragraph numbers of EPS Scheme (Para 12, 13, 14...) · Pre-1995 schemes (FPS 1971) detail · Specific
pensionable salary derivation rules beyond 'average of last 60 months' · Pre-2014 vs post-2014 calculation splits
TRAP: '+2 bonus' applies ONLY if service > 20 years strictly. Exactly 20 years = NO bonus. 20.5 years = 22.5
years for calculation. Also: ₹15,000 wage cap applies unless higher-pension opted (Sunil Kumar 2022).

12.1 The Core Formula
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Monthly Pension = (Pensionable Salary × Pensionable Service) ÷ 70
Pensionable Salary (PS): average of last 60 months' monthly salary (capped at ₹15,000 unless higher-pension
opted).
Pensionable Service (Svc): total contributory service in EPS, plus 2 years bonus if service exceeds 20 years.
Early Pension (<58): deduct 4% per year of early departure.
Floor: ₹1,000 per month regardless of formula output.
Ceiling (pre-higher-pension): ~₹7,500 based on ₹15,000 salary cap.

12.2 Three Solved Examples
Example A — 35 years service, ₹15,000 salary (max EPS cap)
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Pensionable Service: 35 years (>20) → add 2-year bonus → 37 years.
Pensionable Salary = ₹15,000 (capped).
Pension = (15,000 × 37) ÷ 70 = 5,55,000 ÷ 70 = ₹7,928.57/month
(Above the ₹7,500 typical cap — actual practice uses pro-rata methods for pre- and post-2014 service.)

Example B — 20 years service, ₹12,000 salary
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Pensionable Service: exactly 20 years (bonus applies only if >20) → 20 years.
Pensionable Salary = ₹12,000.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 30 · Verified April 2026

Pension = (12,000 × 20) ÷ 70 = 2,40,000 ÷ 70 = ₹3,428.57/month.

Example C — 7 years service only
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Service < 10 years → NOT eligible for pension.
Instead, member can claim Withdrawal Benefit under Para 14 of EPS 1995 — lump-sum based on Table D
(post-14 June 2024 in months).
Withdrawal amount proportional to service length and last wage.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
The +2 year bonus applies ONLY when service is more than 20 years (not 20 exactly, not 19). Be very precise
— a question with 20.0 years will trap most candidates. Also note: pensionable service includes
non-contributory past service added under EPS Scheme paragraphs.

MNEMONIC
Salary × Service, divide by 70. Over 20? Add 2 bonus. Under 10? Only cash.
QUICK RECALL
Formula: (Pensionable Salary × Service) ÷ 70
Pensionable Salary: Average of last 60 months, capped
Service bonus: +2 years if service > 20
Early pension: 4% reduction per year before 58
Deferment: 4% addition per year, till age 60
Floor: ₹1,000/month (since 1 Sep 2014)
Min service: 10 years (else withdrawal benefit)

⚠ EXAM ALERT
LAST-MINUTE REVISION — EPS Pension Formula
Q1: Formula? → (Pensionable Salary × Pensionable Service) ÷ 70
Q2: Pensionable salary? → Avg of last 60 months, capped ₹15,000
Q3: Service bonus? → +2 years IF service strictly > 20 years (NOT = 20)
Q4: Min service for pension? → 10 years; else Withdrawal Benefit only (Table D)
Q5: Early pension penalty? → 4% reduction per year below 58
Q6: Deferred pension bonus? → 4% per year above 58 (up to age 60)
Q7: Min pension floor? → ₹1,000/month (since 1 Sep 2014)
Q8: Pension at age 58, ₹15K salary, 30 yrs service? → 15,000 × 30 ÷ 70 = ₹6,428/month
Q9: 25 years service? → Add 2-year bonus → 27 yrs · 15K × 27 ÷ 70 = ₹5,785
Q10: Higher pension case? → Sunil Kumar 2022 (SC) — option for higher pension based on actual salary
EXAM-EVE CHECKLIST:
□ '+2 bonus' applies ONLY if service is STRICTLY > 20 years (20 exactly = NO bonus)
□ ₹1,000 floor applies regardless of formula output
□ Less than 10 yrs = Withdrawal Benefit (Table D), NOT pension

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 31 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 13 — All Nine Types of EPS Pension
EPS-95 gives nine distinct categories of benefit — four for the member (Superannuation, Early,
Invalidity, Deferred), three for the family (Widow, Children, Orphan), one for a Nominee, and one for those
with less than 10 years' service (Withdrawal Benefit — Form 10C). Each has its own trigger, quantum, and
formula nuance.

✔ KEY FACT
Invalidity Pension is the ONLY category that does not require 10 years of service — just 2 months of
contribution is enough, provided the disablement is permanent and total. This is the EPS-95's most humane
feature and a recurring exam question.

13.2 Pension vs Withdrawal Benefit — which one applies
PENSION (≥10 yrs)
• Service ≥ 10 years → Pension (nine types above)
• Monthly periodic payment
• Continues for lifetime; family continuation on death
• Governed by Pension Payment Order (PPO)
• Minimum ₹1,000/month

WITHDRAWAL BENEFIT (<10 yrs)
• Service < 10 years → Withdrawal Benefit
• One-time lump sum
• Computed via Table D of EPS 1995
• Claim via Form 10C
• No minimum floor (depends on wage + service)

MNEMONIC
Super-Early-Defer-Invalid | Widow-Child-Orphan-Nominee | Withdraw
QUICK RECALL
Min service for pension: 10 years (except invalidity — 2 months)
Min pension: ₹1,000/month
Widow pension: 50% of member's pension or ₹1,000 (higher)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 32 · Verified April 2026

Children's pension: 25% of widow's pension per child (max 2)
Orphan pension: 75% of widow's pension per child (max 2)
Early pension reduction: 4% per year below 58
Withdrawal benefit form: Form 10C (<10 yrs service)

Chapter 14 — Higher Pension — SC Sunil Kumar 2022
The Sunil Kumar vs EPFO Supreme Court judgment of 4 November 2022 reshaped EPS-95. It allowed
certain eligible members to opt for pension on their actual higher salary rather than the ₹15,000
ceiling. Hundreds of thousands of retirees suddenly became eligible for much larger monthly pensions —
at the cost of diverting a corresponding chunk from their EPF balance to the Pension Fund. This is the
most discussed EPS topic in the 2024-26 exam cycle.

14.1 The Timeline
RC Gupta SC

Sunil Kumar SC

~99% processed

Oct 2016

4 Nov 2022

Nov 2025

22 Aug 2014

11 Jul 2023

Para 11(3)

Opt-in
deadline

Figure 14.1 — Higher pension decision-to-disposal timeline

14.2 The Mechanics — how higher pension actually works
Joint option filed by employer + employee
Deadline was 11 July 2023

EPFO computes past EPS deficit
8.33% on actual salary minus what was paid on ₹15K

Member pays arrears from EPF balance (OR from personal funds)
Typically multi-lakh amounts

EPS contributions from now on at actual wage
Employer's 8.33% on full salary

Pension recomputed using higher Pensionable Salary
Formula × larger numerator

Figure 14.2 — Joint-option mechanism after Sunil Kumar SC 2022

14.3 Illustrative Comparison — ₹15K cap vs ₹60K actual
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Assume 35 years service; last 5-year average wage = ₹60,000/month.
Under ₹15,000 EPS cap:
Pension = (15,000 × 37) ÷ 70 = ₹7,929/month ≈ ₹95,143 annually
Under higher pension (actual ₹60,000 salary):
Pension = (60,000 × 37) ÷ 70 = ₹31,714/month ≈ ₹3,80,571 annually
Differential = ₹23,786/month ≈ ₹2,85,428 per year for life

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 34 · Verified April 2026

(Offset against a one-time EPF corpus reduction of ~₹10–15 lakh)

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Status as of November 2025 (per PIB): EPFO reports 99% of higher-pension applications processed.
Demand letters issued: 4,27,308. Eligible and paid/consented: 2,33,303. Pension Payment Orders (PPOs) issued:
~1,24,457 — another 12,572 in final stages. This snapshot is a favourite data-point for current-affairs questions.

✔ KEY FACT
Under Sunil Kumar 2022, eligibility is limited to those who contributed to EPF on actual salary (above the
₹15,000 cap) AND who had exercised or will now exercise a joint option with the employer. Employees who
never contributed above ₹15,000 are NOT eligible.

MNEMONIC
Option → Arrears → Bigger numerator → Bigger pension (at the cost of a smaller EPF lump sum)
QUICK RECALL
SC date: 4 November 2022 — Sunil Kumar vs EPFO
Precursor: RC Gupta vs RPFC, 2016 (allowed higher contribution)
Option deadline: 11 July 2023 (after 2 extensions)
Status Nov 2025: ~99% processed per PIB
Funding arrears: Diverted from member's EPF balance
Who is eligible: Members who contributed EPF on salary >₹15,000

Visual — Higher-Pension Eligibility Decision Flow
1. Was the member in service on 1 Sep 2014?
If NO → not eligible (post-2014 joiners with >₹15k are excluded from EPS)

2. Did the member contribute EPF on ACTUAL salary (>₹15,000)?
If NO → option unavailable — pension stays on capped ₹15,000

3. Did member submit joint option (with employer) by 11 Jul 2023?
Deadline after RC Gupta (2016) + Sunil Kumar (2022) extensions

4. EPFO processes & raises demand for arrears
Principal + interest on EPS share difference

5. Arrears debited from member's EPF balance
Any shortfall paid by member; surplus returned to EPF

6. Revised pension on ACTUAL-salary pensionable wage
Higher monthly pension; smaller EPF lump sum trade-off

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 35 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 15 — EPS 2024 Amendments (14 June 2024 Notification)
Two major changes were made to EPS by the same Ministry of Labour notification on 14 June 2024. First,
Table D — used to compute withdrawal benefits for members with less than 10 years service — was
converted from a yearly basis to a monthly basis, giving fairer outcomes to those with fractional years.
Second, Table B was extended to 42 years of service. The same notification also unified damages
under 14B at 1% per month.

15.1 Table D — Before vs After
BEFORE 14 June 2024
• Yearly basis — service rounded to full years
• Fractional year ignored
• Example: 4 years 11 months → treated as 4 years
• Short-service leavers disadvantaged

AFTER 14 June 2024
• Monthly basis — granular computation
• Every completed month counts
• Example: 4 years 11 months → treated as 59
months
• Fair outcome regardless of exit month

15.2 Table B Extension
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Table B is used where an employee has both Past Service (pre-1995) and Pensionable Service
(post-1995).
Prior cap: table went up to 24 years total service.
New cap: 42 years — covering employees with unusually long careers.
Benefit: members with 25-42 years of mixed service now get a precise factor rather than a ceiling.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
The same 14 June 2024 notification unified EPS damages at 1% per month (Para 32A of EPS 1995) —
bringing it in line with EPF's simultaneous revision of Para 32A of the EPF Scheme. Both take effect for defaults
accruing on or after 15 June 2024.

✔ KEY FACT
Three concurrent changes on 14 June 2024:
• Table D → months • Table B → 42 years • Damages → 1%/month uniform

MNEMONIC
D-B-D → D-Table months · B-Table 42 years · Damages 1%/m
QUICK RECALL
Notification date: 14 June 2024
Effective date: 15 June 2024 for damages
Table D change: Years basis → months basis
Table B extension: Up to 42 years
Damages unification: Slab (5-25%) → 1%/month (no cap)
Reason: Worker-friendly + simpler administration

PART IV

Employees' Deposit Linked Insurance (EDLI) 1976
Free life-cover worth up to ₹7 lakh — funded 100% by the employer

Chapter 16 — EDLI 1976 — The Complete Reference
The Employees' Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme is a group life-insurance cover for every
EPF-covered worker. Premium is a mere 0.50% of wages paid entirely by the employer — the
employee pays nothing, the government adds nothing. Yet the nominee receives a payout of up to
₹7,00,000 if the member dies in service. Over the decades, the sum assured has been progressively
enhanced, most recently extended on a permanent footing in October 2024.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — EDLI 1976 | Tier 2 (1 mark)
MEMORIZE: Formula = 35 × Avg wage (cap ₹15K) + ₹1,75,000 bonus · Floor ₹2.5 lakh · Ceiling ₹7
lakh (Oct 2024 made permanent) · Contribution = 0.50% by EMPLOYER ONLY (capped at ₹75/month if
wage ≥ ₹15K) · Admin charges = NIL (since Apr 2017) · Coverage from Day 1 of PF · 2025 amendment: ₹50K
minimum for workers with up to 6-month coverage gap · Statutory basis = Sec 6C of EPF Act
SKIP: EDLI Para numbers · Historical enhancement years (1976 → 1989 → 1998 → 2014 → 2021 → 2024 — only
the latest matters) · Detailed claim process beyond Form 5-IF
TRAP: Don't confuse EDLI MAX (₹7 lakh) with FLOOR (₹2.5 lakh). Also: EDLI is 100% EMPLOYER paid —
employee pays NOTHING. And EDLI ≠ ESI maternity/sickness — EDLI is ONLY death-in-service insurance.

16.1 Parameters — at a glance
Parameter

Value / Rule

Employees' Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976

Employer contribution

0.50% of wages (cap ₹75/month on ₹15,000)

NIL

NIL

Form 5-IF (EDLI)

Settlement deadline

Claim to be settled within 7 working days (30 days if EPF+EPS also claimed
together)

Max benefit

₹7,00,000 (from 28 April 2021; extended Oct 2024)

Min benefit

₹2,50,000

Bonus component

₹1,75,000 (over and above formula)

16.2 The Formula
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
EDLI payout = (35 × Average wage of last 12 months, capped ₹15,000) + ₹1,75,000 bonus

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 39 · Verified April 2026

Floor: ₹2,50,000 (if formula output is lower, pay this)
Ceiling: ₹7,00,000 (if formula output is higher, pay this)

Example 1 — Member at wage ceiling
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Average wage (capped) = ₹15,000
Formula = 35 × 15,000 + 1,75,000 = 5,25,000 + 1,75,000 = ₹7,00,000
Exactly at ceiling → pay ₹7,00,000.

Example 2 — Low wage earner
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Average wage = ₹8,000
Formula = 35 × 8,000 + 1,75,000 = 2,80,000 + 1,75,000 = ₹4,55,000
Between floor and ceiling → pay ₹4,55,000.

Example 3 — Very low wage earner (floor applies)
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Average wage = ₹2,000
Formula = 35 × 2,000 + 1,75,000 = 70,000 + 1,75,000 = ₹2,45,000
Below floor → pay ₹2,50,000 (minimum statutory).

16.5 Claim Priority — who gets the payout
Priority

Claimant

Condition

⚠ EXAM ALERT
No minimum service, no waiting period. Even if a member dies on Day 1 of employment, EDLI pays the
nominee. The 2021 amendment also removed job-change discontinuity — a member changing jobs does not
lose continuity of EDLI cover, provided PF contributions continue.

✔ KEY FACT
February 2025 CBT Amendment — EDLI Coverage Gaps: (1) Minimum assured benefit of ₹50,000 added
for workers with coverage gaps (up to 6 months of non-contribution now allowed without losing EDLI eligibility).
(2) This is SEPARATE from the standard floor of ₹2,50,000 — the ₹50,000 minimum applies when gap
disqualification would otherwise apply. Effectively, even workers with contribution gaps up to 6 months get at
least ₹50,000 EDLI cover.

✔ KEY FACT
EDLI admin charges are NIL — waived since 1 April 2017. Earlier they were 0.01% of wages (the "A/c 22"
head). This saving goes directly to the employer.

MNEMONIC
35 × avg wage + 1.75 L bonus · Min 2.50 L · Max 7.00 L · 0.50% employer only · Day 1 covered
QUICK RECALL

□ EDLI = death-in-service insurance only (NOT health, NOT maternity)
□ 100% employer-funded (employee pays NOTHING — common trap)
□ Don't confuse EDLI max (₹7L) with EDLI floor (₹2.5L) with 2025 min (₹50K)
□ Day 1 coverage — even death on the first day is covered

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 42 · Verified April 2026

PART V

Code on Social Security, 2020
Nine Acts folded into one — in force 21 November 2025

✔ KEY FACT
Big Picture — Why India moved from 29 Acts → 4 Codes: By 2019, Indian labour law had accumulated 29
separate Central Acts — each with its own threshold, inspector, registration, return, and penalty. Employers
complied with 8-12 parallel filings; workers didn't know which Act protected which benefit. The 2nd National
Commission on Labour (Ravindra Varma, 1999-2002) had recommended consolidation into four logical
buckets — one for wages, one for industrial relations, one for social security, and one for occupational
safety. The SS Code 2020 is the 'Social Security' bucket: it subsumes 9 separate SS Acts (EPF, ESI, Gratuity,
Maternity, Workmen's Compensation, ISMW, BOCW, Cine Workers, Unorganised Workers). The other three codes
are: Wages 2019 (4 Acts), IR 2020 (3 Acts), OSH 2020 (13 Acts). The Codes were assented 2019-2020 but
operationalised in phases; SS Code's major provisions came into force on 21 November 2025. Three
conceptual shifts introduced: (i) gig + platform workers now have statutory SS for the first time
(S.113-S.114); (ii) single Aadhaar-based registration replaces 9 separate registrations; (iii)
Inspector-cum-Facilitator model replaces traditional enforcement-only Inspector.

Chapter 17 — SS Code — Overview & Timeline
The Code on Social Security, 2020 (Act 36 of 2020) is one of the four new Labour Codes that
consolidate 29 existing labour laws. It specifically folds nine social-security statutes into a single code.
President's assent was given on 28 September 2020. After years of waiting for State rules, the Central
Government notified 21 November 2025 as the date from which major substantive provisions come
into force — via Gazette Notification No. 5143 (S.O. 5319(E)).
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — SS Code 2020 | Tier 1 (3-4 marks — NEW LAW)
MEMORIZE: 9 Acts subsumed (EPF, ESI, Gratuity, Maternity, BOCW, Cine, EC Act, Employment Exchanges,
Unorganised Workers) · Assented 28 Sep 2020; in force 21 Nov 2025 · FTE gratuity = 1 year (Sec 53) —
biggest change · Gig & platform workers (Sec 113-114) — first formal legal recognition · Aggregator cess for
gig workers · ESI pan-India option · 25% appeal pre-deposit · Inspector-cum-Facilitator model
SKIP: Chapter numbers within SS Code (14 chapters) · Verbatim wording of definitions · Section-by-section
mapping to old Acts (only Sec 53 matters) · Detailed transition rules
TRAP: SS Code did NOT change gratuity max (still ₹20L) but DID change FTE eligibility (1 yr vs 5 for regular).
Also: SS Code did NOT remove the 20-employee EPF threshold — it retained it (Central Govt may reduce by
notification).

Chapter 18 — Key Changes under SS Code 2020
The SS Code is not a cut-and-paste of the old laws — it modernises many provisions. Some changes are
transparent wins for workers (FTE gratuity, universal ESI option). Others ease employer compliance
(compounding, facilitator model). The table below is the single most important subject-wise compare you
must memorise.

18.1 Old Law vs SS Code — subject by subject

Uniform 25% of amount determined

Expanded — first-time offences compoundable

⚠ EXAM ALERT
FTE gratuity dropped from 5 years → 1 year under Section 53 of the SS Code. This is the single most
common exam question on the Code. FTE = Fixed Term Employment — employees on time-bound contracts.

18.2 Compounding of Offences
UNDER OLD LAWS
• Criminal prosecution first route
• Offences generally not compoundable
• Inspector could file complaint on discovery
• Fear-based compliance

UNDER SS CODE
• Inspector-cum-Facilitator advises first
• First-time offences compoundable
• Show-cause before prosecution
• Compliance-first culture

✔ KEY FACT
Under SS Code, the 25% appeal pre-deposit rule is uniform across EPF, ESI, Gratuity matters — replacing the
earlier mixed regime. This reduces litigation costs for genuine disputes.

MNEMONIC

FTE gratuity: 1 year (Section 53) — huge change
Inspector: Facilitator model — compliance first
Appeal deposit: Uniform 25%
Maternity + crèche: 50+ workers
Unorganised: Formal welfare boards (national + state)

Chapter 19 — Gig & Platform Workers — Sec 113 & 114
For the first time in Indian labour law, gig workers (short-duration task-based earners outside a
traditional employer-employee relationship) and platform workers (those who earn via a digital platform
— Ola, Swiggy, Urban Company, Amazon Flex) get formal recognition. Sections 113 & 114 lay the
foundation for welfare schemes funded partly by a cess on aggregator revenue. A National Social
Security Board has been set up as the apex policy body.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Every platform worker is a gig worker, but not every gig worker is a platform worker. The
distinguishing factor is the digital platform / aggregator. Welfare financing under Sec 114 comes from
aggregators — not from general taxation.

✔ KEY FACT
SS Code's definition of aggregator (Sec 2(2)) is the first statutory recognition of digital intermediaries in Indian
social-security law. The cess corpus is earmarked for life & disability cover, health & maternity, old-age
protection, and accident coverage for registered gig/platform workers.

Chapter 20 — Four Labour Codes — Full Picture
Between 2019 and 2020, Parliament consolidated 29 central labour laws into four codes. The SS Code
2020 is one of them. For the APFC exam, memorise the four codes, the year each was enacted, which
existing laws each replaced, and — critically — their current implementation status (as of April 2026).

20.1 The Four Codes at a Glance

✔ KEY FACT
Total laws consolidated = 4 + 3 + 9 + 13 = 29 labour laws → 4 Codes. ALL FOUR Labour Codes came into
force simultaneously on 21 November 2025. Draft central rules published 30 December 2025; government
declared full operationalisation by publishing final rules in early 2026.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Don't confuse the Industrial Relations Code (IR Code) with the Industrial Disputes Act it replaces. Many APFC
questions describe a scenario and ask which code or which section applies. The Chapter VB
(lay-off/retrenchment) thresholds went from 100 → 300 workers under the IR Code.

Chapter 20 — Code on Wages 2019 — Key Provisions
The Code on Wages 2019 merges four wage-related Acts and introduces a uniform concept of 'wages'
across all of them. Key innovations: a statutory national floor wage, universal applicability (NOT just
scheduled employments), and a single wage definition.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Code on Wages 2019 | Tier 1 (1-2 marks — NEW LAW)
MEMORIZE: 4 Acts merged (PoW 1936, MW 1948, Bonus 1965, Equal Remun 1976) · NFLMW ₹178/day
NOW STATUTORY (was advisory) · Universal coverage (NOT just scheduled employments — biggest change)
· Unified wage definition = Basic + DA + Retaining Allowance (excludes HRA, OT, bonus, employer PF) ·
Inspector-cum-Facilitator (one officer, all 4 Acts) · In force 21 Nov 2025
SKIP: Section-wise mapping to each old Act · Detailed transition timeline · State-specific rule-making procedures
TRAP: NFLMW was ADVISORY before, now STATUTORY. States CANNOT legally go below ₹178/day
anymore. Bonus calculation ceiling (₹7K or Min Wage whichever higher) is RETAINED from old Act — Code on
Wages doesn't change it.

wage ceiling

Equal pay for equal work (gender-neutral pay)

20A.2 National Floor Wage — The New Concept
Aspect

Detail

What it is

Minimum wage floor set by Central Government — NO state can set minimum wage
below this

Current rate

₹178 per day (set 2019; not revised since; ≈ ₹5,340/month)

Was advisory/non-statutory under NFLMW policy

Legal status (post-Code)

Now statutory and enforceable — states legally bound

Review period

Central Government reviews periodically (no fixed interval like old 5-year)

Scope

Applies to ALL workers (not just scheduled employments) — universal coverage

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Pre-Code NFLMW was non-statutory — states could ignore it. The Code on Wages 2019 makes the national
floor wage legally binding. This is a critical distinction tested in exams. The current floor is ₹178/day — NOT
revised since 2019.

Chapter 20 — Industrial Relations Code 2020 — Key Provisions
The IR Code 2020 merges three Acts governing trade unions, service conditions, and industrial disputes.
Major innovations: Fixed-Term Employment (FTE), Negotiating Union/Council, and raising the
Chapter VB threshold to 300 workers.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — IR Code 2020 | Tier 1 (2-3 marks — NEW LAW)
MEMORIZE — these are the 5 GAME-CHANGERS: 3 Acts merged (TU 1926, IE-SO 1946, ID 1947) · FTE —
gratuity after 1 year (NOT 5); contract expiry = NOT retrenchment (no compensation) · Chapter VB threshold:
100 → 300 workers (lay-off / retrenchment / closure) · Negotiating Union (51%+ membership) vs
Negotiating Council (proportional) · Re-skilling Fund: employer pays 15 days wages per retrenched worker ·
TU min 7 members retained · 60-day strike notice in PUS retained · In force 21 Nov 2025
SKIP: Section-wise mapping to old Acts · Detailed tribunal composition (1 or 2 member benches) · Conciliation
procedural rules · Specific arbitration award enforcement details
TRAP: Chapter VB change is the SINGLE most-tested IR Code change. Old IDA threshold = 100 workers; New =
300. Also: FTE expiry is NOT 'retrenchment' — so no compensation owed at end of fixed-term contract.

20B.1 Three Acts Merged into IR Code 2020
Act Replaced

Year

What it Governed

1926

Industrial Employment (Standing Orders) Act

1946

Dispute resolution, strikes, lockouts, retrenchment, closure

20B.2 Fixed-Term Employment (FTE) — APFC High-Value Topic
Aspect

Detail

Definition

Contract of employment for a fixed term (specific duration)

Benefits (FTE worker same as
permanent employee)

Same working hours, wages, statutory benefits (EPF, ESI, gratuity)

Gratuity eligibility

After 1 year (NOT 5 years) — proportional to service rendered

No retrenchment compensation

FTE contract expiry is NOT retrenchment — no notice/compensation required

Renewal

Can be renewed; no automatic conversion to permanent

Exam trap

FTE workers get gratuity after 1 year. Regular workers need 5 years. Under SS Code
2020, FTE gratuity = 1 year also confirmed.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
IR Code 2020 — Top 5 exam traps: (1) FTE gratuity = 1 year (not 5). (2) Chapter VB threshold = 300
workers (was 100 in IDA). (3) 51%+ membership → Negotiating Union; if no majority → Negotiating
Council. (4) Re-skilling Fund: employer pays 15 days wages per retrenched worker. (5) FTE expiry is NOT
retrenchment — no compensation.

QUICK RECALL
IR Code — Acts merged: 3: Trade Unions 1926, Standing Orders 1946, Disputes 1947
FTE gratuity threshold: 1 year (vs 5 years for regular employees)
FTE termination: NOT retrenchment — no compensation at contract end
Negotiating Union: When ONE union has 51%+ membership
Negotiating Council: When NO union has 51%+ membership
Chapter VB threshold: 300 workers (OLD: 100 workers under IDA 1947)
Re-skilling Fund: Employer pays 15 days wages per retrenched worker
Standing Orders threshold: 300+ workers (same as Ch VB)
TU minimum members: 7 (unchanged)

LAST-MINUTE REVISION — IR Code 2020 (NEW LAW)
Q1: How many Acts merged? → 3 Acts (TU 1926, IE-SO 1946, ID 1947)
Q2: FTE gratuity eligibility? → 1 year (NOT 5)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 56 · Verified April 2026

PART VI

Industrial Relations & Labour Laws
ID Act · Factories · ESI · Ten other welfare statutes

✔ KEY FACT
Big Picture — Three Pillars framework: Every Indian labour law fits into exactly one of three pillars. Pillar I
— Working Conditions (Factories Act 1948, Mines Act 1952, OSH Code 2020 concepts, ISMW 1979, BOCW
1996, Equal Remuneration 1976, Contract Labour 1970): regulates HOW the worker is treated at work. Pillar II
— Wages & Bonuses (Minimum Wages 1948, Payment of Wages 1936, Payment of Bonus 1965, Equal
Remuneration 1976, Code on Wages 2019): regulates WHAT the worker is paid. Pillar III — Social Security
(EPF 1952, EPS 1995, EDLI 1976, ESI 1948, Gratuity 1972, Maternity 1961, Workmen's Comp 1923, Unorganised
Workers 2008, SS Code 2020): regulates income security on life events (retirement, death, disability, sickness,
maternity). When reading any labour-law question, first ask: 'Which pillar is this about?' That single question
eliminates most distractors. APFC is primarily concerned with Pillar III (SS) but overlaps with Pillar II (wages
determine contributions) and Pillar I (who qualifies as 'employee').

Chapter 21 — Industrial Disputes Act, 1947
The Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (IDA) is the 75-year-old spine of India's dispute-resolution
machinery. It defines what counts as an industrial dispute, lays out the settlement hierarchy from Works
Committee to National Tribunal, regulates strikes & lock-outs, and prescribes the compensation payable
on lay-off, retrenchment, and closure. The IR Code 2020 will replace it but IDA remains the operative law
in April 2026.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — ID Act 1947 | Tier 1 (3-4 marks)
MEMORIZE (substance + key sections): Definitions of industry, workman, strike, lock-out, retrenchment,
lay-off · Public Utility Services (PUS) list · 6-week notice rule for strike/lockout in PUS (Sec 22) · Triple test of
'industry' = Bangalore Water Supply 1978 · Chapter VB threshold = 100 (old) → 300 under IR Code 2020 ·
Retrenchment compensation = 15 days wages × completed years · Lay-off compensation = 50% of basic+DA
SKIP (low-yield): All 40 sections by number · Distinction between 25-F vs 25-FF vs 25-FFF intricacies · Detailed
proviso wording · Tribunal-vs-Court-vs-Board procedural minutiae
TRAP: Students memorize Section 25F number but forget it's about retrenchment — remember the SUBSTANCE
first. Memorize section numbers ONLY for iconic sections: Sec 2 (definitions), Sec 22 (PUS notice), Sec 25F
(retrenchment).

21.4 Retrenchment Compensation — the formula

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 58 · Verified April 2026

FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Compensation = (Monthly wage ÷ 26) × 15 × Completed years
Example: Monthly wage = ₹26,000; service = 12 years 7 months.
26,000 ÷ 26 = 1,000 (daily wage equivalent)
1,000 × 15 = 15,000 (one year's compensation)
Completed years = 12 (months beyond 6 rounds up → 13)
Compensation = 15,000 × 13 = ₹1,95,000
Plus: 1 month notice or wages in lieu + any gratuity due.

21.5 IDA vs IR Code — the big shifts
IDA 1947

IR CODE 2020

• Chapter VB threshold: 100+ workers (requires
Govt permission for lay-off/retrenchment/closure)
• Strike in PU: 14 days prior notice
• Standing Orders: applicable if ≥100 workers
• No re-skilling fund
• Fixed-Term Employment not formally recognised
• No statutory negotiating-union framework

• Chapter VB threshold: 300+ workers (big
relaxation for smaller units)
• Strike in any industry: 60 days prior notice
• Standing Orders: applicable if ≥300 workers
• Re-skilling Fund for retrenched workers
• FTE formally recognised, entitled to pro-rata
benefits
• Negotiating Union with 51% support gets
statutory standing

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Strike notice under Public Utility Services: IDA → 14 days. Strike notice under IR Code 2020: 60 days,
applicable to all industries (not just PU). A 4-fold increase — this is a regular exam trick.

Chapter 22 — Factories Act, 1948
The Factories Act, 1948 regulates working conditions in factories. A "factory" means premises with 10+
workers using power OR 20+ workers without power, in a manufacturing process. The Act has been
subsumed by the OSH Code 2020 but remains in force in April 2026. Key for the APFC exam: threshold
numbers for welfare obligations.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Factories Act 1948 | Tier 2 (1-2 marks)
MEMORIZE: Definition 10 workers + power OR 20 without power · Child labour <14 prohibited; 14-18 =
adolescent (restricted) · Working hours 48/week, 9/day, max 60/wk including OT · Welfare thresholds:
Canteen-250+, Crèche-30+ women, Ambulance-500+, Welfare Officer-500+ · Weekly holiday + ½-hr
rest after 5 hrs work
SKIP: All chapter names/numbers · Specific Schedule III occupational disease list · Inspector qualifications ·
State-specific rules · Hazardous Process detailed provisions
TRAP: Mixing up worker thresholds. Just learn ONE per provision: Canteen=250, Crèche=30 women,
Ambulance=500. Don't try to memorize all welfare numbers — the 3 above cover 90% of Qs.

FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Overtime wages = 2 × ordinary rate (Section 59)
Applicable: work beyond 48 hrs/week or 9 hrs/day
Annual leave (adults): 1 day per 20 days worked in the previous calendar year
Annual leave (child workers): 1 day per 15 days worked (Section 79)

⚠ EXAM ALERT
30 women workers triggers the crèche obligation — not 30 total workers. This is one of the most-missed
threshold questions. Under the SS Code 2020, the same crèche obligation applies at 50 workers (any
gender).

□ Don't confuse Welfare Officer (500+) with Safety Officer (1000+)
□ Subsumed by OSH Code 2020 (in force 21 Nov 2025)

Chapter 23 — Employees' State Insurance Act, 1948
ESI is India's contributory health and cash-benefit insurance for the organised workforce. Originally
applicable only in notified areas, it is being extended pan-India in phases. Under the SS Code 2020, the
Central Government may make it universal by notification. Employee pays 0.75%, employer pays 3.25%
— total 4%.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — ESI Act 1948 | Tier 1 (2-3 marks)
MEMORIZE: Wage ceiling ₹21,000/month (₹25K if disabled) · Contribution: employer 3.25% + employee
0.75% = 4% · Six benefits = Medical (Day 1), Sickness (70% × 91 days), Maternity (100% × 26 weeks),
Disablement (90% × wage), Dependant (90% × wage), Funeral ₹15,000 · ESI = 'Medical Benefit' NOT 'Health
Benefit' · SPREE 2025 = one-time amnesty for unregistered employers (Jul-Dec 2025)
SKIP: ESI Corporation board composition · Detailed administrative structure · Inspector qualifications · State
Insurance Court details
TRAP: Forgetting direction of percentages. Mnemonic: Employer pays MORE (3.25%) because of LARGER
liability. Also: ESI medical = Day 1 (NO waiting period), unlike sickness (78-day prior contribution rule).

23.1 Contribution Structure
ESI Contribution (Total = 4% of Wages)

Employee 0.75%

23.4 ESI — Old Act vs SS Code 2020
ESI ACT 1948
• Applicable to notified areas only
• Threshold: 10+ employees
• Fixed coverage by Govt notification
• Separate appeals under ESI Act

SS CODE 2020
• Pan-India option via single notification
• Threshold: same, but CG may relax
• Voluntary coverage allowed for establishments
below threshold
• Unified 25% appeal deposit under Code

✔ KEY FACT
Funeral expenses under ESI = ₹15,000 (lump sum on death of insured). Don't confuse with EDLI's minimum
payout of ₹2,50,000 — the two schemes are completely separate.

MNEMONIC
Employee 0.75 · Employer 3.25 · Total 4% · Wage cap ₹21,000 · Funeral ₹15,000
⚠ EXAM ALERT
LAST-MINUTE REVISION — ESI Act 1948
Q1: Wage ceiling? → ₹21,000/month (₹25K disabled) · revised 1 Jan 2017
Q2: Contribution rates? → Employer 3.25% + Employee 0.75% = 4% (since 1 Jul 2019)
Q3: ESI for ₹20K wage? → Employer ₹650, Employee ₹150 (3.25% & 0.75% of 20K)
Q4: Medical benefit? → Day 1, full medical care for insured + family
Q5: Sickness benefit? → 70% of avg daily wage × 91 days/year (after 78 days prior contribution)
Q6: Maternity? → 100% of wage × 26 weeks
Q7: Funeral expenses? → ₹15,000 lump sum (NOT ₹10K, NOT ₹20K)
Q8: SPREE 2025? → Jul-Dec 2025; ESIC-launched; one-time amnesty for unregistered employers + employees
(includes contractual)
Q9: ESI = ? → 'Medical benefit', NOT 'Health benefit' (word choice trap)
EXAM-EVE CHECKLIST:
□ ESI = 'Medical Benefit' (exact wording)
□ ESI medical = Day 1 NO waiting; Sickness needs 78-day prior contribution
□ Employer pays MORE (3.25%); Employee pays LESS (0.75%) — don't reverse
□ SPREE 2025 covers contractual workers TOO (not just permanent)

Chapter 24 — Master Table — Other Labour Acts
A single comprehensive reference table covering the other labour welfare statutes tested in the APFC
exam. Memorise year, threshold, key numerical limits, and any unique rule. These minor Acts add up to
5-8 predictable questions every year.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Min Wages + Bonus + Maternity + EC + PoW | Tier 1 (5-7 marks combined)
MEMORIZE — Min Wages 1948: 5-year revision · 9-hr day · 48-hr week · 2× overtime · Committee +
Notification methods · scheduled employments (now universal under Code on Wages)
MEMORIZE — Bonus 1965: 20+ workers · ₹21K eligibility · ₹7K calc (or Min Wage) · 8.33% min / 20% max ·
30 days qualifying · 8 months payment
MEMORIZE — Maternity 1961: 10+ workers · 80 days qualifying · 26 weeks (1st-2nd child) / 12 weeks
(3rd+, adoption) · 50+ crèche · ₹3,500 medical bonus
MEMORIZE — EC Act 1923: Death 50% × wage × RF (₹1.2L floor) · PTD 60% × wage × RF (₹1.4L floor) · Wage
cap ₹15K · One eye = 40%
MEMORIZE — PoW 1936: ₹24K wage cap · Pay by 7th (<1000 emp) or 10th (≥1000) · Max deduction 50%
SKIP: All Section numbers (only Sec 22 of MW Act = penalty occasionally tested) · Detailed set-on/set-off
carry-forward calculations · Full Relevant Factor table — just learn pattern (young high, old low) · State-specific
minimum wages · Specific PoW deduction sub-clauses
TRAP — Bonus Act: ₹21K (eligibility) vs ₹7K (calculation) — TWO different ceilings.
TRAP — Maternity: 26 weeks is ONLY 1st-2nd child. 3rd+ = 12 weeks.
TRAP — EC Act: Don't memorize full RF table — paper gives RF in the question itself. Just know the FORMULA
shape: % × wage × RF.

Apprentices are NOT 'employees' under the EPF Act (Sec 2(f) excludes them explicitly). Therefore they do
not count towards the 20-employee threshold and no PF is deductible on their stipend. This is the single
most-repeated exam question on the Apprentices Act.

months count as full year. Maximum = ₹20,00,000 (2018 amendment).

ECA 1923 as amended.

The Minimum Wages Act 1948 is the most-tested wage statute in APFC papers. Subsumed by Code on

in SCHEDULED EMPLOYMENTS only (this was the big change in Code on Wages 2019 — universal

5-YEAR rule — most

✔ KEY FACT
The Five Numbers to Memorise for Minimum Wages Act:
5 years — minimum revision period
9 hours — normal working day
48 hours — normal working week
2× — overtime rate multiplier
6 months — limitation for claims
The Two Methods to Distinguish:
• Committee method = Govt forms a tripartite committee → recommendation → notification
• Notification method = Govt directly publishes proposal → 2-month objection → final notification
Trap: Q may ask 'when is Notification method used?' → when revising EXISTING rates (no need for new
committee)

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Common APFC trap on Min Wages: (1) Q: 'Min wages can be fixed for ANY employment?' → NO, only
scheduled. (2) Q: 'Same min wage everywhere?' → NO, varies by region, occupation, skill (S.3(3)). (3) Q: 'Min
wages includes overtime?' → NO, OT is separate (2×). (4) Q: 'Min wages applicable to apprentices?' → NO,
apprentices under Apprentices Act 1961 are excluded. (5) Q: 'Variable DA component?' → YES — DA is the
cost-of-living indexation.

Ch-24 Patch — Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 (Full Drill — HIGH HIT FREQUENCY)
The Payment of Bonus Act 1965 sets statutory bonus for workers — linked to profits AND productivity.
Subsumed by Code on Wages 2019. Famous numbers: ₹21,000 wage ceiling · 8.33% minimum · 20%
maximum · 30 working days qualifying service · 20+ employees threshold.
Aspect

Provision

Exam flash

Applicability

Every factory AND every other establishment employing 20 or
more persons on any day in the accounting year

20+ threshold

Eligible employee

Drawing wages up to ₹21,000/month (revised from ₹10K in 2015)

Above 21K = NOT
eligible

Qualifying service

Worked at least 30 working days in the accounting year

Even probationer eligible
if 30 days

Wage ceiling for
calculation

Bonus calculated on wages up to ₹7,000/month OR the minimum
wage notified for that scheduled employment — whichever HIGHER

Use ₹7,000 OR min
wage, whichever higher

Minimum bonus

8.33% of salary/wage (= 1 month's pay) regardless of profit / loss

Even loss-making firms
MUST pay minimum
8.33%

Maximum bonus

20% of salary/wage

Cap regardless of how
high profits go

Allocable surplus

67% of available surplus for companies (other than banking) · 60%
for banking

67% / 60% — frequently
tested

Set-on / Set-off

If surplus > 20% requirement → SET-ON for up to 4 years; If surplus
< 8.33% → SET-OFF from earlier years (max 4 years)

Within 8 months from close of accounting year

Or as Govt extends

(S.9)

Newly set-up

First 5 accounting years: bonus payable only if PROFIT made (8.33%

5-year grace period

✔ KEY FACT
The Bonus Act Number Tree (anchor everything to these):
20+ — employees needed for applicability
₹21,000 — wage ceiling for ELIGIBILITY
₹7,000 — wage ceiling for CALCULATION (or min wage, whichever higher)
30 days — minimum service to qualify
8.33% — minimum bonus (= 1 month wages)
20% — maximum bonus
67% / 60% — allocable surplus (companies / banking)
8 months — payment deadline
4 years — set-on / set-off carry forward
5 years — newcomer grace period

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Bonus calculation worked example:
Worker drawing ₹18,000/month (eligible, <₹21K). Min wage notified for his employment = ₹9,000/month.
Calculation ceiling = ₹9,000 (higher of ₹7,000 vs ₹9,000 min wage). Min bonus = 8.33% × 9,000 × 12 =
₹8,996 / year. Max bonus = 20% × 9,000 × 12 = ₹21,600 / year.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 72 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 22 — Trade Unions Act, 1926 — Complete Reference
The Trade Unions Act, 1926 (TUA) is the foundational statute governing registration, rights, immunities,
and obligations of trade unions in India. It gives workers the legal framework to organise collectively — a
right that flows from Article 19(1)(c) of the Constitution. The Act has 32 sections and the Registrar of
Trade Unions is the key authority. Know the registration numbers, office-bearer rules, immunities,
and fund-use provisions — those generate most exam questions.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Trade Unions Act 1926 | Tier 1 (1-2 marks)
MEMORIZE: Min 7 members to register (retained under IR Code 2020) · Min 10% of workmen OR 100
workmen for recognition · Registrar of Trade Unions is the appointing authority · Outsiders rule: max 1/3 of
office-bearers OR 5 (whichever less) can be outsiders · Immunities: civil (Sec 18) + criminal (Sec 17) — TU
cannot be sued for acts in furtherance of trade disputes · Funds usage: general fund (Sec 15) restricted use;
political fund (Sec 16) requires consent
SKIP: All 32 sections by number · Detailed cancellation procedure · Specific affidavit forms · Section 16(2)
detailed political-fund objectives wording · Internal procedure of Registrar
TRAP: Don't confuse 7 (min to register) with 10% / 100 workmen (recognition under IR Code). Section 17/18
= IMMUNITIES (don't confuse with Section 14 = appeals). The outsiders rule (1/3 OR 5) is FREQUENTLY tested
verbatim.

22A.1 Key Parameters
Parameter

Rule

Exam flash

apply for registration

7 persons who subscribe to the rules and apply

number

for executive

Registrar of Trade Unions — appointed by State Govt

Parameter
Recognition

Rule

Exam flash

Not mandatory under TUA 1926; some states have separate Recognition ≠ registration
Recognition Acts; IR Code 2020 introduces 'Negotiating

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Office-Bearer Rule (S.22) — Most Tested: In a union with 10 office bearers, maximum 5 can be outsiders
(1/3 of 10 = 3.33, and 5 — lower is 3, so max 3 outsiders). Wait — the rule says 'one-third OR 5, whichever is
LESS'. For a 10-person executive: 1/3 = 3 (rounded down) vs 5 → LESS is 3. So max 3 outsiders, at least 7 must
be from the industry. For a 15-person executive: 1/3 = 5 vs 5 → LESS is 5. Max 5 outsiders.

QUICK RECALL
Min members to register: 7 (S.4)
Age for membership: 15+ years
Age for office bearer: 18+ years
Outsiders cap: Lower of 1/3 or 5 office bearers (S.22)
Insiders minimum: 50% of office bearers must be from industry
Civil immunity: S.17 — torts in trade disputes
Criminal immunity: S.18 — lawful acts in trade disputes
Political fund: S.16 — separate, voluntary only
Amalgamation threshold: 60% member vote (S.24)

⚠ EXAM ALERT
LAST-MINUTE REVISION — Trade Unions Act 1926
Q1: Min members to register? → 7 members (retained under IR Code 2020)
Q2: Min for recognition? → 10% workmen OR 100 workmen (whichever less)
Q3: Outsiders rule? → Max 1/3 of office-bearers OR 5 (whichever less)
Q4: Civil immunity Section? → Sec 17
Q5: Criminal immunity Section? → Sec 18
Q6: Registrar of TU appointed by? → State Government (each state has own)
Q7: Political fund Section? → Sec 16 — separate from general fund; voluntary contribution
Q8: Amalgamation needs? → 60% member vote (Sec 24)
Q9: Constitutional basis for TU? → Art 19(1)(c) — right to form associations/unions
TU FEDERATIONS (high-hit): AITUC (1920, oldest, CPI-affiliated) · INTUC (1947, Congress) · HMS (1948,
Socialist) · CITU (1970, CPM) · BMS (1955, RSS-affiliated)
EXAM-EVE CHECKLIST:
□ 7 members = REGISTRATION; 10%/100 workmen = RECOGNITION (don't mix)
□ Outsiders = 1/3 of office-bearers OR 5 (the LESSER)
□ AITUC = oldest TU federation (1920); BMS = RSS-linked (1955)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 74 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 22 — Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act, 1970
The Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act, 1970 (CLRA) regulates the employment of
contract workers — workers supplied by a contractor to a 'principal employer'. The law has two goals:
regulate welfare obligations and abolish contract labour in core/perennial processes. From the APFC lens:
if a principal employer's contractor defaults on EPF, the principal employer is jointly and severally
liable (S.21(4) CLRA + S.8B EPF Act). Master the thresholds, liability chain, and abolition
procedure.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Contract Labour Act 1970 | Tier 2 (1 mark)
MEMORIZE: 20+ contract workers on any day in preceding 12 months triggers Act · Two roles: Principal
Employer (registers establishment) + Contractor (licensed) · Principal employer's EPF liability: joint &
several (S.21(4)) · Welfare obligations on contractor: canteen, rest rooms, drinking water · Abolition possible in
'core/perennial' processes by Advisory Board recommendation
SKIP: All sections by number · Detailed Advisory Board composition · Inspector qualifications · Specific
contractor licensing forms
TRAP: 20 = threshold count (NOT a different number like 10 or 50). If contractor defaults on EPF/ESI, the
PRINCIPAL employer pays, not Government — this is the recovery angle the APFC works with.

Parameter

Rule

Exam flash

establishment

Employing 20 or more contract workers on any day in the
preceding 12 months

drinking water, latrines, washing facilities, first aid

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 75 · Verified April 2026

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Contractor EPF Default — APFC Scenario: Factory X engages contractor Y who deploys 30 workers.
Contractor Y doesn't deposit PF. APFC issues notice. Who pays? Both contractor Y (primary) and principal
employer X (joint-and-several under S.21(4) CLRA and S.8B EPF Act). Factory X can recover the amount
from contractor Y's security deposit or by deduction from bills. This is the single most important CLRA point for
the APFC exam.

QUICK RECALL
Threshold (both sides): 20 or more contract workers
Principal employer: Must register (Form I)
Contractor: Must get licence
Wages liability: Contractor primary; principal employer secondary
EPF default: Joint-and-several; recover from contractor
Abolition trigger: S.10 — Advisory Board consultation + Govt notification
Form XII: Register of Contractors — maintained by principal employer
ISMW link: Cross-state contract workers → ISMW 1979 also applies

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 76 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 22 — Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace
(Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013
The POSH Act, 2013 codifies the Vishaka Guidelines 1997 (Supreme Court directions in Vishaka v.
State of Rajasthan). It applies to all workplaces — organised AND unorganised — and mandates Internal
Committees (IC) in larger establishments and Local Committees (LC) for smaller ones / unorganised
sector. POSH is standalone — not subsumed into any of the four Labour Codes. For APFC: EPFO is
the employer; APFC may be IC member or the authority handling complaints. Know IC constitution,
timelines, interim relief, conciliation, and employer duties.

Below 10 → LC handles
complaints

Woman employee at a senior level in the establishment; if no
such woman is available, nominated from another office or
administrative unit of the same employer

Must be a WOMAN at
senior level — frequently
tested

IC composition

Chairperson + at least 2 members from employees (committed
to women's cause / legal / management); 1 external member
(from NGO / person familiar with sexual harassment issues)

External member is
mandatory

Majority female

At least one-half of members shall be women

Majority women —
S.4(2)(c)

3 years

Constituted by the District Officer for: establishments with <10
employees, unorganised sector, domestic workers; LC

employer
