Chapter 38 — Partnership Accounts — Full Drill (HIGH HIT)
Partnership accounts appear in EVERY APFC accounting section (2-3 Qs typical). The Indian Partnership
Act 1932 governs partnerships. Key topics tested: (1) P&L Appropriation Account, (2) Capital
accounts (Fixed vs Fluctuating), (3) Goodwill treatment (admission, retirement, death), (4)
Profit-sharing ratios, (5) Revaluation Account.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Partnership Accounts | Tier 1 (1-2 marks)
MEMORIZE: Deed silent defaults (Sec 13): P/S = EQUAL · Interest on capital = NIL · Salary = NIL · Interest
on partner's LOAN = 6% p.a. (mandatory even if Deed silent) · P&L Appropriation order: Interest on Cap →
Salary → Reserves → Distribute remainder · Goodwill methods: Average Profit · Super Profit · Capitalisation ·
Annuity · Sacrificing Ratio = Old − New (admission) · Gaining Ratio = New − Old (retirement) · Revaluation
gain/loss = to OLD partners in OLD ratio
SKIP: Detailed Indian Partnership Act 1932 sections beyond Sec 13 · Specific journal entries verbatim · Annuity
Goodwill table values · Detailed dissolution procedures (Garner v. Murray rule is occasionally tested)
TRAP: Confusing partner's LOAN (6% default) with partner's CAPITAL (NIL default). Sacrificing ratio is for
ADMISSION; Gaining ratio is for RETIREMENT/DEATH. Don't mix them up — direction matters.

38B.1 Fundamentals — Partnership Deed Defaults
When the Partnership Deed is silent on a matter, the Indian Partnership Act 1932 (Section 13) provides
default rules. These defaults are the EXAM TRAP.
Aspect

If Deed is SILENT (default)

Common Deed Override

Profit-sharing ratio

EQUAL among all partners (regardless of
capital contribution)

Proportional to capital, or agreed ratio

Interest on capital

NIL (no interest payable)

5-12% typical; tested at 6% / 8%

Interest on drawings

NIL (no interest charged)

Charged at 6-12% if Deed specifies

Salary / Commission to
partner

NOT payable

Specific monthly amount or % of profit

Interest on partner's loan

6% p.a. (default — even if Deed silent)

Mandated by S.13(d) of Partnership Act

Admission of new partner

Requires UNANIMOUS consent of all existing
partners

Deed may permit majority

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Common APFC trap: Q: 'If Deed is silent, what interest on loan from partner?' → Answer = 6% p.a. (mandatory
under S.13(d)). BUT interest on CAPITAL = NIL if Deed silent. Don't confuse partner's LOAN (6% default) with
partner's CAPITAL (NIL default).

38B.2 P&L; Appropriation Account — The Structure
P&L Appropriation Account starts where the regular P&L Account ends (with the Net Profit). It shows HOW
that profit is DISTRIBUTED among partners.
Side
DEBIT (Appropriations)

Item
Interest on Capital (to partners)
Salary / Commission to partner
Transfer to Reserves
Profit distributed to partners (residual)

Why?
These REDUCE the profit
available for distribution

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 155 · Verified April 2026

Side

Item

CREDIT (Source)

Why?

Net Profit b/d (from P&L Account)
Interest on Drawings (charged to partners)

These INCREASE the
appropriable surplus

✔ KEY FACT
Order of Appropriation (memorise): (1) Interest on Capital → (2) Partner's Salary / Commission → (3)
Transfer to Reserves → (4) Distribute remaining profit in P/S ratio.
If profit is INSUFFICIENT to cover all appropriations, then interest on capital + salary are paid proportionately
(not in full). NEVER pay interest on capital out of capital — only out of profit.

38B.3 Capital Accounts — Fixed vs Fluctuating
Feature

Fixed Capital Method

Fluctuating Capital Method

Number of accounts

TWO per partner: (1) Capital A/c, (2) Current
A/c

ONE per partner: Capital A/c only

Capital balance

REMAINS FIXED (changed only by additional
capital introduced/withdrawn)

CHANGES every year (with profit/loss, salary,
interest etc.)

Where adjustments go

Interest, salary, profit share, drawings → in
Current A/c

All adjustments → in Capital A/c itself

Current A/c balance

Can be DEBIT or CREDIT

N/A (no Current A/c)

Exam preference

Most APFC questions use Fixed method (more
'textbook')

Simpler; some questions

38B.4 Goodwill — Methods of Valuation
Method

Formula

When used

Average Profit
Method

Goodwill = Average Profit × Number of years' purchase

Simplest; most common

Super Profit Method

Super Profit = Actual Profit − Normal Profit (Capital ×
Normal Rate %)
Goodwill = Super Profit × Number of years' purchase

Better when profits are above
industry norm

Capitalisation Method

Capitalised Value = Average Profit / Normal Rate of Return
Goodwill = Capitalised Value − Capital Employed

Used for comprehensive
valuation

Annuity Method

Goodwill = Super Profit × Annuity Factor

Considers time value of money

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Goodwill Worked Example: Firm's average profit = ₹50,000. Capital employed = ₹3,00,000. Normal rate of
return = 10%. Years of purchase = 3.
Average Profit Method: Goodwill = 50,000 × 3 = ₹1,50,000
Super Profit Method: Normal Profit = 3,00,000 × 10% = ₹30,000. Super Profit = 50,000 − 30,000 = ₹20,000.
Goodwill = 20,000 × 3 = ₹60,000
Capitalisation Method: Capitalised Value = 50,000 / 10% = ₹5,00,000. Goodwill = 5,00,000 − 3,00,000 =
₹2,00,000

38B.5 Admission / Retirement / Death of Partner
Event

Key Adjustments Required

Admission of new partner

(1) New P/S ratio calculated · (2) Sacrificing ratio of old partners · (3) Goodwill brought
in by new partner OR adjusted via partners' capital A/c · (4) Revaluation of
assets/liabilities · (5) Reserves distributed to old partners

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 156 · Verified April 2026

Event

Key Adjustments Required

Retirement / Death

(1) New P/S ratio · (2) Gaining ratio of continuing partners · (3) Retiring partner's share
of Goodwill credited to him · (4) Revaluation of assets/liabilities · (5) Reserves &
accumulated profits credited

Death — JLP (Joint Life
Policy)

If JLP exists, the surrender value (or matured value) is credited to all partners in their
old P/S ratio

Sacrificing Ratio

Old Share − New Share = Sacrificing Ratio (admission)

Gaining Ratio

New Share − Old Share = Gaining Ratio (retirement/death)

38B.6 Revaluation Account
Used when assets/liabilities are revalued at admission/retirement/death. Gain or loss on revaluation goes
to old partners in their old P/S ratio.
Revaluation Account Side

Items

DEBIT (loss side)

Decrease in asset value · Increase in liability · Unrecorded liabilities · Provision for
doubtful debts created

CREDIT (gain side)

Increase in asset value · Decrease in liability · Unrecorded assets · Provision written
back

Final balance

If Credit > Debit → GAIN → distribute to OLD partners in OLD ratio
If Debit > Credit → LOSS → debit OLD partners in OLD ratio

QUICK RECALL
Deed silent on P/S ratio: EQUAL among all partners
Deed silent on interest on capital: NIL
Deed silent on interest on loan: 6% p.a. (S.13(d) mandatory)
Deed silent on salary: NIL (no salary payable)
P&L; Appropriation: Starts with Net Profit; distributes among partners
Fixed Capital Method: Two A/cs: Capital (fixed) + Current (variable)
Sacrificing Ratio: Old Share − New Share (used at admission)
Gaining Ratio: New Share − Old Share (used at retirement)
Goodwill — average profit: Avg Profit × Years of purchase
Goodwill — super profit: (Actual − Normal Profit) × Years of purchase
Revaluation gain/loss: Distributed to OLD partners in OLD ratio
Reserves at admission: Credited to OLD partners in OLD ratio

⚠ EXAM ALERT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 157 · Verified April 2026

LAST-MINUTE REVISION — Partnership Accounts
Q1: Deed silent — P/S ratio? → EQUAL among all partners
Q2: Deed silent — interest on capital? → NIL
Q3: Deed silent — interest on partner's LOAN? → 6% p.a. (Sec 13(d) mandatory)
Q4: Deed silent — salary? → NIL (no salary payable)
Q5: Sacrificing Ratio formula? → Old Share − New Share (at ADMISSION)
Q6: Gaining Ratio formula? → New Share − Old Share (at RETIREMENT/DEATH)
Q7: Average Profit Goodwill? → Avg Profit × Years of purchase
Q8: Super Profit Goodwill? → (Actual Profit − Normal Profit) × Years
Q9: Capitalisation Goodwill? → (Avg Profit / Normal Rate) − Capital Employed
Q10: Revaluation gain distribution? → To OLD partners in OLD ratio
Q11: Partnership Act year? → 1932 (Indian Partnership Act)
Q12: P&L Appropriation order? → Interest on Cap → Salary → Reserves → Distribute remainder
EXAM-EVE CHECKLIST:
□ Interest on partner's LOAN = 6% even if Deed silent (Sec 13(d))
□ Sacrificing = ADMISSION; Gaining = RETIREMENT (don't reverse)
□ Revaluation gain/loss = ALWAYS to OLD partners in OLD ratio

Chapter 39 — Statistics — Central Tendency, Dispersion, Indices
Statistics questions on the APFC exam are standard: measures of central tendency (mean, median,
mode), dispersion (SD, variance, CV), index numbers (Laspeyre's, Paasche's, Fisher's), correlation
(Pearson's r), and the 68-95-99.7 rule of the normal distribution. Mostly formula-drill and
interpretation.

39.1 Measures of Central Tendency
Measure

Formula

Best used for

Arithmetic Mean

Σx ÷ n

Symmetric data; no outliers

Median

Middle value (sorted)

Skewed data; robust to outliers

Mode

Most frequent value

Categorical/qualitative data

Geometric Mean

(x₁ × x₂ × … × xₙ)^(1/n)

Rates of change (CAGR, returns)

Harmonic Mean

n ÷ Σ(1/xᵢ)

Rates involving time (speed, density)

FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Skewness relationships:
Symmetric distribution: Mean = Median = Mode
Positive (right) skew : Mode < Median < Mean
Negative (left) skew : Mean < Median < Mode
Empirical relationship (moderate skew): Mode ≈ 3 Median − 2 Mean

39.2 Measures of Dispersion
Measure

Formula

Interpretation

Range

Max − Min

Simplest; very outlier-sensitive

Mean Deviation

Σ|x−mean| ÷ n

Average absolute deviation

Variance (σ²)

Σ(x−mean)² ÷ n

Squared deviation average

Standard Deviation

√Variance

Same units as data; most used

Coefficient of Variation (CV)

(SD ÷ Mean) × 100

Unit-free; compare datasets

39.3 Index Numbers
Index

Formula

Bias

Laspeyre's (base-year weighted)

(Σ p₁ q₀) ÷ (Σ p₀ q₀) × 100

Over-estimates inflation (fixed base
basket)

Paasche's (current-year weighted)

(Σ p₁ q₁) ÷ (Σ p₀ q₁) × 100

Under-estimates inflation
(substitution)

Fisher's (Ideal)

√(Laspeyre × Paasche)

Geometric mean; satisfies time &
factor reversal

39.4 Indian Price Indices

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 159 · Verified April 2026

Index

Released by

Base year

Used for

CPI (Consumer Price Index)

NSO

series released
12-Feb-2026;
earlier
2012=100)

FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Pearson's correlation coefficient: r = Σ(x−x̄)(y−ȳ) ÷ (nσₓσᵧ)
Range: −1 ≤ r ≤ +1.
Spearman's rank correlation: rₛ = 1 − [6 Σd² ÷ n(n²−1)]
Normal distribution (68-95-99.7 rule):
±1σ → 68.27% of values
±2σ → 95.45% of values
±3σ → 99.73% of values

39.5 Six Sampling Methods
Method

Core idea

Example

Simple Random Sampling (SRS)

Each unit equal probability

Lottery ticket

Stratified Sampling

Divide into strata; sample each

Survey by age groups

Systematic Sampling

Every k-th unit from a list

Every 10th entry in register

Cluster Sampling

Sample whole clusters

Sample entire villages

Purposive (Judgment) Sampling

Expert-selected units

Key informants in NGO study

Multi-Stage Sampling

Sequential random at levels

NSSO household surveys

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Fisher's Ideal Index is the geometric mean of Laspeyre's and Paasche's. It satisfies BOTH the time-reversal
test and the factor-reversal test — hence the epithet 'ideal'.

MNEMONIC
L Over · P Under · F Ideal | 68 · 95 · 99.7 (1σ, 2σ, 3σ)
QUICK RECALL
Symmetric: Mean = Median = Mode
Positive skew: Mode < Median < Mean
Fisher's index: √(Laspeyre × Paasche)
CPI base year: 2024 = 100 (new series 12-Feb-2026; earlier 2012)
Normal 68-95-99.7: ±1σ, ±2σ, ±3σ
Pearson range: [-1, +1]

Part-IX Patch — Auditing Theory (Documentation · Vouching · Verification)
Auditing theory contributed 3-5 questions in the 2025 APFC paper (Qs 108-110). The examiners test
conceptual distinctions — especially vouching vs verification and the nature of audit documentation.
Sources: Standards on Auditing (SA) by ICAI; Companies Act 2013 S.143.
Concept

One-line definition

Key distinction / exam flash

Audit

Independent examination of financial information to
express an opinion.

Mandatory under Companies Act,
GST Act, Income Tax Act.

Auditor

Qualified CA appointed u/s 139 to conduct the audit.

Tenure: 5 years for individual; 10
yrs for firm (rotation).

Audit
documentation (SA
230)

Record of audit procedures performed, evidence obtained
and conclusions reached (also called 'working papers').

Includes paper + electronic +
audio/video; Does NOT include
summaries of unresolved matters
(auditor's memo only).

Audit evidence (SA
500)

Information used by the auditor to arrive at conclusions on
which the opinion is based.

Must be sufficient (quantity) +
appropriate
(relevance+reliability).

Vouching

Examining documentary evidence for transactions
recorded in books of account (invoices, receipts, bank
statements).

Transaction-level check;
primary purpose: detect
errors/fraud in recording.

Verification

Confirming the existence, ownership, valuation and
classification of assets & liabilities at balance-sheet
date.

Balance-sheet-level check;
vouching proves 'it happened',
verification proves 'it exists
today'.

Valuation

Checking whether assets/liabilities are stated at
appropriate amounts.

Part of verification — SA 540
(Auditing Accounting Estimates).

Analytical
procedures (SA 520)

Evaluations of financial information through analysis of
plausible relationships among data (ratios, trends).

Mandatory during planning and at
overall review stage.

Internal control (SA
315)

Policies & procedures within the entity to ensure orderly
conduct, safeguard assets, prevent/detect fraud & error.

Auditor MUST understand it — but
not responsible for its
effectiveness.

✔ KEY FACT
Vouching vs Verification — the line that wins marks:
Vouching answers 'Did the transaction happen and is it recorded correctly?' — it operates on Profit & Loss
items (revenue, expenses).
Verification answers 'Does the asset/liability actually exist, is it owned, and is it valued correctly?' — it operates
on Balance Sheet items.

Types of Audit (quick grid)

Trigger / Frequency

Typical scope

Statutory

Required by law (Cos Act 2013 S.143)

Financial statements; true & fair view opinion

Internal

Continuous (by management)

Operations, controls, risk — not just financials

Concurrent

Transaction-level, real-time

Banks — daily high-risk transactions

Tax

If turnover > ₹1 crore (business) / ₹50L
(prof)

S.44AB of IT Act — Form 3CA/3CB + 3CD

GST

Turnover-based (annual)

Reconciliation & compliance

Cost

Mandated for specified industries
(S.148)

Cost records, cost sheet, cost reduction

Part-IX APFC Lens — EPFO Investment Pattern, Trust Accounting & Actuarial Valuation
For APFC, accounting is not a general CA-level topic — it's applied to EPFO's own balance sheet: how
the ₹25 lakh crore corpus is invested, how exempted-establishment trusts are audited, and how the EPS
deficit is actuarially measured. The questions below repeat every year.

Two historical milestones: (1) EPFO started equity investing via CPSE ETF in August 2015 (initial 5% cap).
(2) Equity cap raised to 15% in FY 2016-17 after review. Equity share crosses ₹3 lakh crore today — making
EPFO one of India's largest institutional equity investors.

C. Actuarial Valuation & EPS Deficit
Concept

Explanation

Defined Benefit (DB)
scheme

EPS is a DB scheme — member gets a pension by formula, regardless of corpus. Sponsor
(EPFO + GoI) bears actuarial risk.

Actuarial valuation

Independent actuary estimates present-value of all FUTURE pension liabilities and
compares with current corpus. Done every 2-4 years for EPS.

Actuarial deficit

If PV of future liabilities > corpus + expected future contributions, it's an actuarial deficit
/ unfunded liability.

EPS actuarial deficit

CAG report noted ~₹70,000-80,000 crore deficit; later revisions higher. Politically
sensitive. GoI's 1.16% contribution partly covers.

Member's EPF account
accounting

Opening balance + contributions + interest credited = closing balance. No
market-linked fluctuation — interest declared annually by CBT.

IndAS 19 / AS-15
Employee Benefits

Requires employer to recognise Defined Benefit obligation on its B/S — actuarially
valued. Applies to gratuity + EPS (for companies > specified size).

Investment accounting

HTM (Held To Maturity) valued at cost; AFS (Available For Sale) and HFT (Held For
Trading) at market value via 'Mark-to-Market'.

✔ KEY FACT
EPFO itself is audited by CAG (statutory audit under S.5A(6) of EPF Act). Annual accounts placed before
Parliament. The exempted-trust audit is by independent CA, but EPFO has supervisory & inspection powers
under S.13 and can revoke exemption for non-compliance.

MNEMONIC

Part-IX Add — Corporate Social Responsibility (S.135, Cos Act 2013)
CSR becomes relevant in APFC context because large EPF-covered employers are also CSR-eligible
companies. Understanding CSR helps the APFC verify employer welfare claims & Schedule-VII alignment
(education, skill development, livelihood enhancement).
Aspect

Detail

Section

S.135 of Companies Act 2013 (in force 1 Apr 2014); rules 2014

Applicability thresholds

Any company (incl. foreign) meeting ANY of: (a) Net worth ≥ ₹500 crore, (b) Turnover ≥
₹1,000 crore, or (c) Net profit ≥ ₹5 crore

CSR spend mandate

At least 2% of average net profits of preceding 3 financial years

Schedule VII

Lists 11 permissible CSR activities — eradicating hunger/poverty, education, gender
equality, environment, national heritage, armed forces welfare, sports, PM Relief Fund,
Swachh Bharat, R&D, rural development

CSR Committee

3+ directors (one independent for listed companies)

Unspent amount

Unspent (non-ongoing) must be transferred to specified funds in Schedule VII (e.g., PM
Relief Fund) within 6 months

Ongoing project

Can defer unspent in 'Unspent CSR Account' for up to 3 years; must be spent or
transferred thereafter

2020 Amendment

Made CSR non-compliance a civil offence (previously 'comply or explain'); penalties up
to ₹1 crore (company) + ₹2 lakh (officer)

✔ KEY FACT
Common CSR-labour intersections: Skill development (PM Kaushal Vikas Yojana-linked), women's
empowerment programmes, worker-welfare facilities, child-labour elimination — all count under Schedule VII. An
EPF-compliant company that falls short on CSR can be flagged by CAG. APFC will often audit EPF + ESI +
Gratuity but occasionally coordinate with MCA on CSR reporting for large employers.

MNEMONIC
500 NW · 1,000 TO · 5 Profit · 2% × prev-3-yr avg — the CSR applicability + mandate
cheat-code

CSR governing law: S.135 of Companies Act 2013
Threshold: NW ₹500 cr OR TO ₹1,000 cr OR Profit ₹5 cr (any one)
Mandate: 2% of avg net profit of preceding 3 FYs
Schedule: Schedule VII (11 activities)
Non-compliance: Civil penalty since 2020 amendment
Unspent transfer: Within 6 months (non-ongoing)

Part-IX Add — Cash Flow Statement (AS-3 / Ind AS 7)
A Cash Flow Statement reconciles the opening and closing cash & cash equivalents for a period. It is
mandatory for all listed companies and large unlisted companies under AS-3 (Indian GAAP) and Ind AS 7.
Cash equivalents are investments readily convertible to cash with maturity ≤ 3 months from acquisition
date.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 164 · Verified April 2026

Activity

What it covers

Key items

Operating (CFO)

Core business cash flows

Cash from customers; cash paid to suppliers & employees;
interest paid (alt: financing); taxes paid

Investing (CFI)

Long-term asset & investment
transactions

Purchase/sale of fixed assets; purchase/sale of
investments; loans given/received; interest received;
dividends received

Financing (CFF)

Raising/repaying capital and
borrowings

Issue/redemption of equity & debt; dividends paid;
repayment of loans

INDIRECT METHOD

DIRECT METHOD

• Net Profit (after tax) as starting point
• Add back non-cash items (depreciation,
amortisation)
• Adjust for working capital changes (increase in
debtors = outflow)
• Add back investing/financing cash flows excluded
from CFO
• Result: Net Cash from Operations
• Simpler — starts from P&L; widely used in India

• Gross cash receipts from customers listed
• Gross cash paid to suppliers, employees, taxes
listed
• No working capital adjustment needed (all cash
directly)
• Gives clearer picture of actual cash movements
• Preferred by Ind AS 7 (though indirect also allowed)
• Rarely used in India due to data collection difficulty

Opening cash + cash equivalents
+ Net Change = Closing cash + cash equivalents
Closing cash must agree with Balance Sheet 'Cash & Bank Balances'.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Three common MCQ traps: (1) Depreciation added back in INDIRECT method (non-cash, not a real inflow). (2)
Dividend RECEIVED goes to Investing (Ind AS 7); dividend PAID goes to Financing. (3) Increase in Current
Liability = source of cash (inflow); decrease = use of cash (outflow).

Part-IX Add — Key Audit Standards (SA 700, SA 705) & CARO 2020
Standards on Auditing (SAs) are issued by the ICAI. Two SAs dominate the APFC MCQs on audit opinions:
SA 700 (forming the opinion) and SA 705 (modifications to that opinion). CARO 2020 is a mandatory
additional-report requirement for specified companies.
Standard

Full name

What it governs

SA 700

Forming an Opinion and Reporting on
Financial Statements

How the auditor evaluates audit evidence, concludes whether
financial statements give a true & fair view, and drafts the
unmodified (clean) opinion.

SA 701

Communicating Key Audit Matters
(KAM)

Listed companies only — auditor must highlight significant
risk areas in the audit report (post 2019 India mandate).

SA 705

Modifications to the Opinion in the
Independent Auditor's Report

Three types of MODIFIED opinions when financial statements
have material misstatements OR when auditor cannot obtain
sufficient evidence.

SA 706

Emphasis of Matter and Other Matter
Paragraphs

Unmodified opinion, but auditor adds a paragraph to highlight
significant fact (e.g., going concern uncertainty, pending
litigation).

SA 705 — Four Opinion Types (exam grid)
Opinion type

Trigger condition

Key phrase in report

Unmodified (Clean)

Financial statements give a true & fair view; no
material issues

"…give a true and fair view…"

Qualified

Material misstatement OR scope limitation, but NOT
pervasive (isolated to specific items)

"Except for the matter described… give
a true and fair view"

Adverse

Material misstatement that is pervasive — affects
overall picture of financial statements

"…do NOT give a true and fair view"

Disclaimer

Auditor cannot obtain sufficient evidence AND
possible effects are material and pervasive

"We do not express an opinion"

✔ KEY FACT
Decision tree: Material issue? → Yes → Pervasive? → No = Qualified; Yes = Adverse. Cannot get evidence? →
Yes → Pervasive? → No = Qualified; Yes = Disclaimer. No material issue → Unmodified.

CARO 2020 — Companies (Auditor's Report) Order
CARO 2020 (effective FY 2021-22) is a supplementary report that the statutory auditor must attach along
with the main audit report for specified companies. It replaced CARO 2016.

Applicable to

All companies EXCEPT: (a) Banking / NBFCs / Insurance companies, (b) Section 8
companies (charitable), (c) Small private companies (PvtLtd with paid-up ≤ ₹1 crore
AND turnover ≤ ₹10 crore)

Key clauses

21 reporting matters including: fixed assets, inventory, loans, investments, deposits,
cost records, statutory dues, loans/advances, default on dues, fraud reporting,
related-party transactions

Part-IX Add — NPO & Trust Accounting (R&P vs I&E)
Non-Profit Organisations (NPOs) — trusts, societies, clubs, EPFO itself — do not prepare a Trading Account
or P&L. Instead they prepare a Receipts & Payments (R&P) Account and an Income & Expenditure
(I&E) Account, plus a Balance Sheet. The key difference: R&P is cash-based; I&E is accrual-based.
Feature

Receipts & Payments (R&P)

Income & Expenditure (I&E)

Nature

Summary of all cash/bank transactions

Accrual-based performance statement

Basis

Cash basis — actual receipts & payments

Accrual basis — earned/incurred

Includes

Capital + revenue receipts & payments

Only revenue incomes & expenditure

Opening balance

Yes (opening cash/bank)

No opening balance

Closing balance

Yes (closing cash/bank)

Surplus / Deficit (not cash)

Equivalent to

Cash Book summary

Profit & Loss Account (commercial)

Depreciation

NOT included (no cash outflow)

Included (non-cash charge)

Capital items

Included (e.g. building purchased)

Excluded — on Balance Sheet

Result

Closing cash balance

Surplus or Deficit

Capital Fund & Balance Sheet of NPO
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Capital Fund = accumulated Surplus over years + Life membership fees + Legacies + Donations (capital
nature)
Balance Sheet of NPO:

Liabilities side: Capital Fund · Specific Funds · Current Liabilities
Assets side: Fixed Assets · Investments · Current Assets · Cash & Bank

shown separately from Capital Fund.

✔ KEY FACT
EPFO Trust Accounting context: An exempted-establishment PF Trust prepares accounts similar to an NPO —
it holds members' funds (liability) and invests them (assets). The 'Capital Fund' equivalent is the accumulated
Provident Fund corpus. Interest credited to members is an expenditure; interest earned on investments is
income. The I&E shows whether the trust earned enough to meet the statutory EPF interest rate.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Frequent MCQ: "Depreciation appears in ___" → I&E only (not R&P, because it is a non-cash item). "Life
membership fees appear in ___" → Capital Fund (not I&E income).

MNEMONIC
R&P = Cash (like a bank statement) · I&E = Accrual (like a P&L) · Capital Fund = NPO's
equivalent of Owner's Equity
QUICK RECALL
R&P; basis: Cash — all receipts & payments
I&E; basis: Accrual — revenue only
Depreciation in: I&E; only (non-cash)
Life membership: → Capital Fund (not I&E; income)
Specific fund: Designated donation — separate from Capital Fund
Surplus/Deficit: I&E; closing result
NPO Balance Sheet: Capital Fund + Liabilities = Fixed Assets + Investments + Current Assets

Chapter 39 — Accounting PYQ Drill — EO/AO 2025 Trap Questions
A. AS-1 Fundamental Assumptions — MATCHING is NOT one of them
⚠ EXAM ALERT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 168 · Verified April 2026

AS-1 (Disclosure of Accounting Policies) lists EXACTLY THREE fundamental accounting assumptions:
1. Going Concern — entity will continue in operation for foreseeable future
2. Accrual — revenues and costs recognised when earned/incurred (not when cash moves)
3. Consistency — same methods applied from one period to another
TRAP: Matching concept is NOT a fundamental assumption — it is a derivative of the Accrual concept and
is classified as an accounting concept/principle, not a fundamental assumption. EO/AO 2025 Q77: 'NOT a
fundamental assumption = Matching'. Answer = A (Matching concept).

B. Capital Account — CREDIT Balance (Common Trap)
Account

Normal
Balance

Logic

Exam Trap

Capital Account

CREDIT

Owner invests money INTO the business
— business owes it back to owner →
Liability → Credit

EO/AO 2025 Q82: 'Capital
account has debit balance' =
NOT correct. Debit = wrong.

Asset Accounts

Debit

Assets come in → Debit what comes in

Normal for all asset accounts

Liability Accounts

Credit

Obligation owed → Credit

Capital is a liability from the
business's perspective

Revenue / Income

Credit

Credit all incomes and gains

Revenue increases profit →
credit

Expense / Loss

Debit

Debit all expenses and losses

Reduces profit → debit

C. Operating Profit Formula — Net Profit Adjusted for Non-Operating Items
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
Operating Profit = Net Profit + Non-operating Expenses − Non-operating Incomes
Explanation: Net Profit already DEDUCTED non-operating expenses and ADDED non-operating incomes.
To find Operating Profit (which excludes these), we REVERSE those adjustments:
• Add BACK the non-operating expenses (they were subtracted from net profit)
• Remove (subtract) non-operating incomes (they were added to net profit)
Non-operating examples: Interest on loans, Dividend received, Profit/loss on sale of assets
Operating examples: Sales revenue, Cost of goods sold, Salaries, Factory rent
EO/AO 2025 Q83: Answer = C (Net profit + non-operating expenses − non-operating incomes)

D. Auditing — Vouching vs Verification (Objectives differ)
Audit Procedure

Primary Objective

What it does NOT do

Vouching

Verify the AUTHENTICITY of transactions —
checks that every entry in the books is supported
by proper documentary evidence (voucher)

NOT about detecting errors, NOT about
assessing management performance.
EO/AO 2025 Q89: Answer C =
authenticity

Verification

Examine assets/liabilities for: (1) Existence, (2)
Ownership, (3) Valuation, (4) Authority of
acquisition

NOT about historical accuracy of
accounts — that is general auditing.
EO/AO 2025 Q90: 'NOT objective of
verification = checking historical
accuracy' → D

insured cannot profit from claim

Fire · Marine · Motor ·

Pays a fixed sum on occurrence of an

(Life insurance)

Motor insurance: insurer

F. Inventory Costing — Administration Overheads EXCLUDED from AS-2
Included in Inventory Cost (AS-2)

EXCLUDED from Inventory Cost

Purchase price + import duties + freight

Administration / general overheads (not related to
bringing inventory to present location) — EO/AO 2025 Q84:
Answer A

Conversion costs (direct labour + manufacturing
overheads)

Selling and distribution costs

Normal wastage during production

Abnormal wastage — expensed in P&L; when incurred

Storage costs IF part of the production process (e.g.
maturation of cheese)

Storage costs for goods already completed and awaiting
sale

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Provision vs Reserve — The Fundamental Distinction:
Provision = a CHARGE against profit (comes before profit is calculated — e.g., Provision for bad debts,
Provision for depreciation). Mandatory when liability is probable and measurable.
Reserve = an APPROPRIATION of profit (comes AFTER profit — taken out of earned profit). NOT mandatory;
reduces distributable profit but IS distributable.
Capital Reserve (from non-distributable profits — e.g., profit on sale of fixed asset) vs Revenue Reserve (from
distributable profits — e.g., General Reserve).
EO/AO 2025 Q80: 'Provision is a charge against profit' = CORRECT → Answer D.

QUICK RECALL
AS-1 3 fundamental assumptions: Going Concern · Accrual · Consistency (Matching is NOT one of them)
Capital account balance: CREDIT (owner's investment = business liability)
Operating profit formula: Net profit + non-op. expenses − non-op. incomes
Vouching objective: Verify AUTHENTICITY of transactions

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 170 · Verified April 2026

Verification: NOT about: Historical accuracy of accounts
Life insurance type: Contingency (NOT indemnity)
Mitigation principle: Insured's duty to minimise the loss
Admin overheads in AS-2: EXCLUDED from inventory cost
Provision vs Reserve: Provision = charge against profit; Reserve = appropriation
Ledger = principal book: NOT trial balance — trial balance is just a check

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 171 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 39B — APFC Dec 2025 Accounting Drill (Q96–Q110)
All 15 accounting questions from the APFC Dec 2025 (CRT-IX) paper. This section covers Accounting
Concepts, Capital vs Revenue, Errors, Reserves & Provisions, Auditing, and Insurance.
Q

Topic / Key Fact

Answer / Trap

Q96

AS-2 inventory: 'Cost or NRV,
whichever lower' based on which
concept?

Convention of Conservatism. Conservatism = don't anticipate
profit, provide for all losses. Trap: sounds like Matching or Going
Concern — it's Conservatism.

Q97

Which is NOT a fundamental
accounting assumption?

Matching concept is NOT a fundamental assumption under AS-1.
The THREE fundamental assumptions are: Going Concern, Accrual,
and Consistency. Matching = accounting principle, not assumption.

Q98

Which is a revenue expenditure?

Expenses for obtaining a licence for running a cinema hall =
revenue expenditure (periodic renewal). Capital expenditure:
overhaul of 2nd-hand machinery (before use), legal fees to acquire
property, replacement that extends asset life.

Q99

Asset purchase recorded as an
expense — what type of error?

Error of Principle — capital expenditure treated as revenue. Error
of Omission = transaction not recorded at all. Error of Commission
= wrong amount, wrong account (same side). Compensating error
= two errors cancel each other.

Q100

Accounting reserves — correct
statement

'Provision' is a charge against profit = CORRECT. Reserve is an
APPROPRIATION (not a charge). Capital Reserve = created from
capital profits (not distributable). General Reserve = can be used
for ANY purpose (not specific purposes).

Q101

Which statement about accounts is
correct?

Ledger = principal book of accounts = CORRECT. Trial balance
agreement = NOT conclusive proof of accuracy. Suspense account
= TEMPORARY (not permanent). Nominal accounts ARE closed at
year-end.

Q102

Which is NOT correct about accounts?

Capital account has a debit balance = WRONG (it's CREDIT).
Also NOT correct: Cash sales recorded in sales day book (cash sales
go in Cash Book; sales day book = credit sales only). Discount
column records cash discount ✓; O/S rent = personal a/c ✓.

Q103

Operating profit formula

Operating Profit = Net Profit + non-operating expenses −
non-operating incomes. Reverse the signs vs Net Profit. Non-op
expenses (interest, loss on sale of asset) ADD back; non-op
incomes (dividend, interest received) DEDUCT.

Q104

Cost NOT included in computing
inventory cost (AS-2)

Administration overheads NOT contributing to bringing
inventory to present location and condition are EXCLUDED.
Included: purchase cost, conversion cost, storage essential to
production, normal wastage.

Q105

Journal entry: rent received in
advance ₹2,000 (adjustment)

Dr Rent Received in Advance A/c ₹2,000, Cr Rent Received A/c
₹2,000. Income received in advance = current liability. Debit it to
reduce the income; credit the income account (or P&L).

Q106

Insurance principle: duty to minimise
loss/damage to property

Mitigation (loss-minimisation principle). Insured must act as a
prudent uninsured person. NOT Subrogation (insurer takes over
claim rights after payment). NOT Utmost Good Faith (pre-contract
disclosure duty).

Q107

Which is NOT a contract of indemnity?

Life Insurance is NOT a contract of indemnity. Life = contract of
ASSURANCE (pays fixed sum on death/maturity). Indemnity
contracts: Fire, Marine, Motor, Health. Indemnity = restore financial
position; no enrichment.

Q108

Audit documentation — NOT correct
statement

'Audit file and documentation are the same' = NOT correct.
Audit file = physical/electronic folder; documentation = the content
within it (programmes, working papers, summaries, queries).
Documentation may be recorded on any media ✓.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 172 · Verified April 2026

Q

Topic / Key Fact

Answer / Trap

Q109

Primary objective of auditing

Primary objective = to express an opinion on whether financial
statements give a true and fair view. Detection of errors/fraud
= secondary objective. Evaluating internal controls = audit
procedure, not the primary objective.

Q110

Verification objective — which is NOT
an objective?

Verification objectives: check existence of assets, verify valuation,
check title/ownership, confirm proper disclosure. 'Verify authority
of acquisition' is NOT a standard objective of verification (that's
more of an audit/vouching step, not verification).

⚠ EXAM ALERT
APFC Dec 2025 Accounting Top Traps:
1. AS-2 'Cost or NRV lower' = Conservatism, not Matching — Q96.
2. Capital account = CREDIT balance (Q102 says debit = WRONG).
3. Provision = charge; Reserve = appropriation — Q100.
4. Error of Principle ≠ Error of Commission — Q99.
5. Life insurance = Assurance (NOT indemnity) — Q107.
Error Types — Quick Reference
Error of Principle: Wrong head — capital treated as revenue or vice versa. Trial balance MAY agree. Example:
machinery purchase debited to Purchases A/c.
Error of Omission: Transaction completely left out. Trial balance MAY agree (complete omission). Partial omission =
disagrees.
Error of Commission: Recorded in wrong account (same side/same type). Wrong amount, posting to wrong account.
Trial balance MAY agree.
Compensating Error: Two+ errors cancel each other. Trial balance AGREES despite errors. Hardest to detect.
Clerical Error: Arithmetic mistake, wrong side, wrong total. Trial balance will DISAGREE.

QUICK RECALL
Conservatism: NRV or Cost, whichever lower — don't anticipate profit
AS-1 assumptions (3): Going Concern, Accrual, Consistency
Matching concept: NOT an AS-1 assumption — it's a principle
Error of Principle: Capital↔Revenue wrong classification
Provision: Charge against profit (before arriving at profit)
Reserve: Appropriation of profit (after arriving at profit)
Capital Reserve: From capital profits — NOT distributable as dividend
General Reserve: For ANY purpose — not specific
Ledger: Principal book of accounts
Trial balance: NOT conclusive proof of accuracy
Audit documentation: NOT same as audit file
Primary audit objective: True and fair view — not error detection
Mitigation principle: Insured must minimize loss — not Subrogation
Life insurance: Assurance = NOT indemnity
Operating profit: Net profit + non-op exp − non-op income

Mental Model First · The Freedom Struggle as a Three-Act Play

Chapter 39 — How to Think About the Freedom Struggle
Stop trying to memorise 200 dates. Instead, see the freedom struggle as a play in three acts. Each
act has a clear theme, main actors, and a turning point. Once you have the play in your head, every date
hangs off it naturally.

1905 = the year the mood changed — moderates →
extremists

✔ KEY FACT
Memory pattern for the 7 bright lines: Look at the spacing.
1857 → 1885 (28 years of British rule before INC).
1885 → 1905 (20 years of moderate phase).
1905 → 1919 (14 years until Jallianwala Bagh).
1919 → 1920 → 1930 → 1942 (the 4 Gandhian inflection points, roughly every decade).
Trick: 1920, 1930, 1942 are the three big movements: NCM, CDM, Quit India. Note the gap: 1920 → 1930 (10
yrs apart) → 1942 (12 yrs gap).

39A.4 Cause-Effect Chain (so events feel inevitable)
Stop seeing events as isolated. Each movement was a reaction to what happened before:
Trigger (cause)

Response (effect)

Why?

1905 — Curzon partitions Bengal

1905-08 — Swadeshi & Boycott
Movement

Bengal split on religious lines; outrage → boycott
of British goods

1919 — Rowlatt Act + Jallianwala
Bagh

1920 — Non-Cooperation
Movement

Faith in British justice destroyed; Gandhi calls full
non-cooperation

1922 — Chauri Chaura violence

Gandhi suspends NCM

Gandhi: 'people not ready for non-violent mass
action yet'

1927 — Simon Commission
(all-white)

'Simon Go Back' protests; Nehru
Report 1928

Insult: no Indian on a Commission deciding
India's future

1930 — Salt tax + arbitrary tax
laws

Dandi March (12 March - 6
April); CDM

Salt = something every Indian uses; perfect
symbol for resistance

1942 — WWII; Cripps Mission fails

Quit India Movement (8 August
1942)

Allies promise no immediate freedom; Congress:
'Do or Die'

1946 — Naval mutiny + INA trials

British realise control is slipping

Combined with WWII drain → decision to leave

1947 — Mountbatten Plan

Independence + Partition

Communal tensions made unified independence
impossible

✔ KEY FACT
Why this approach works: When the exam asks 'what triggered NCM?' you don't need to memorise the
answer — you derive it from the chain: '1919 Jallianwala Bagh shocked Gandhi → 1920 NCM'. Causes and effects
are sticky; isolated dates are not.

39A.5 Self-Check Before Moving On
✔ KEY FACT
Cover the right. Answer aloud.
1. What event ended Act I (moderate era)? → 1905 Partition of Bengal
2. What event ended Act II (extremist era)? → 1919 Jallianwala Bagh
3. Which 3 movements anchor Act III (Gandhian)? → NCM 1920, CDM 1930, Quit India 1942
4. Who said 'Swaraj is my birthright'? → Bal Gangadhar Tilak (Act II extremist)
5. Why did Gandhi suspend NCM in 1922? → Chauri Chaura violence

✔ KEY FACT

India · 3 Acts framework: Moderate (1858-1905) → Extremist (1905-1919) → Gandhian (1919-1947) ·
Cause→effect chains: Curzon partition → Swadeshi · Jallianwala → NCM · Simon → Lahore Pakistani Resolution ·
Cripps fail → Quit India

suspended NCM in 1922 (Chauri Chaura). Cause matters more than date — if you know WHY, you can derive
WHEN.

✔ KEY FACT
The Indian National Congress was founded on 28 December 1885 at Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College,
Bombay, by Allan Octavian Hume (retired British civil servant). The first session's president was W.C.
Bonnerjee.

Ch-40 Drill — Gandhian Episodes in Strict Chronological Order
The APFC paper repeatedly tests sequence rather than year — 'which came first: Champaran or Kheda?'
Memorise the left-to-right order below; the year is a bonus. Cross-reference: NCERT Themes in Indian
History (III) and ICHR timeline.
Year

Episode

Why it matters (one line)

1917

Champaran Satyagraha (Bihar)

Gandhi's FIRST Satyagraha in India — indigo peasants; tinkathia
abolished.

1918 Feb

Kheda Satyagraha (Gujarat)

Peasant tax-relief movement during crop failure; Sardar Patel
emerges.

1918 Mar

Ahmedabad Mill Strike

Industrial Satyagraha for 35% wage hike; Gandhi's first
fast-unto-death.

1919

Rowlatt Act · Jallianwala Bagh
(13 Apr) · Hunter Commission

Nationwide protest; 379+ killed at Amritsar; Gandhi renounces
Kaiser-i-Hind medal.

1920-22

cloth.

1922 Feb

22 policemen killed; Gandhi unilaterally withdraws NCM —
controversial decision.

1924

Belgaum Congress

The only Congress session Gandhi presided over (as INC President).

1928

Patel)

Successful no-tax movement; Patel earns the title 'Sardar'.

1929 Dec

Lahore Congress · Poorna

Nehru elected President; 26 Jan 1930 = first Independence Day.

Gandhi's 'Do or Die'; all Congress leaders arrested overnight.

Ch-42 APFC Lens — Indian Labour & Social-Security Statutes Timeline
Beyond freedom-struggle events, the APFC paper consistently asks about the chronological evolution
of labour laws: which Act came first, which commission recommended what, and which Convention India
ratified. Master this timeline and you handle every 'arrange in order' question on labour history.
Year

Event / Statute

Significance

1881

Factories Act 1881

First labour legislation in British India. Regulated child labour (age
7+), working hours, holidays.

1891

Factories Act 1891 (amended)

Minimum age raised to 9; women's hours regulated.

1919

India a founding member of ILO

Treaty of Versailles set up ILO; India among original 45.

1923

Workmen's Compensation Act

First major post-WWI labour welfare law.

1923

Mines Act 1923

First comprehensive mines legislation.

1926

Trade Unions Act

Gave legal status & registration to TUs. In force 1 Jun 1927.

1929

Trade Disputes Act

Predecessor to ID Act 1947.

1929-31

Royal Commission on Labour
(Whitley Commission)

Chaired by J.H. Whitley (UK); seminal report on labour conditions in
India. Recommended statutory minimum wage, social insurance, etc.

1934

Factories Act 1934

Revised based on Whitley Commission.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 182 · Verified April 2026

Year

Event / Statute

Significance

1936

Payment of Wages Act

Regulated timely payment + permissible deductions.

1942

Indian Labour Conference
(ILC) first held

Tripartite consultative body — Labour Member B.R. Ambedkar's
initiative.

1946

Industrial Employment (Standing
Orders) Act

Conditions of employment; now subsumed into IR Code 2020.

1947

ID Act 1947

In force 1-Apr-1947; replaced Trade Disputes Act 1929. Still in
operation till IR Code 2020 fully notified.

1948

Minimum Wages Act ·
Factories Act 1948 · ESI Act
1948

Post-independence foundational trio. Factories Act 1948 remains
current (as amended). ESI = first major SS law of independent India.

1952

EPF & Miscellaneous
Provisions Act · Mines Act 1952

EPF Act = cornerstone of organised-sector SS. Scheme 1952 framed
same year.

1961

Apprentices Act · Maternity Benefit Maternity: 12 weeks (raised to 26 weeks by 2017 Amendment).
Act

1965

Payment of Bonus Act

Min 8.33% / max 20% (Gajendragadkar Commission
recommendation).

1966-69

1st National Commission on
Labour (Gajendragadkar)

Recommended minimum wage, grievance machinery, SS
consolidation. Report 1969.

1970

Contract Labour (Regulation &
Abolition) Act

Registration of principal employers + licensing of contractors.

1971

Family Pension Scheme 1971

Predecessor of EPS 1995; provided survivor benefits only.

1972

Payment of Gratuity Act

Originally max ₹3.5L; raised to ₹10L (2010), ₹20L (2018).

1976

EDLI Scheme (under EPF Act
S.6C)

Life insurance for EPF members; ₹7L cap permanent since Oct 2024.

1979

Inter-State Migrant Workmen Act
(ISMW)

Registration of employers hiring migrants + licence to contractors.

1986

Child & Adolescent Labour
(Prohibition & Regulation) Act

Fully bans <14 years in factories / hazardous; 2016 amendment
added 14-18 ban in hazardous.

1995

Employees' Pension Scheme
1995 (S.6A)

Replaced FPS 1971 on 16 Nov 1995. Pension + widow + child
benefits.

1996

BOCW Act + BOCW Cess Act

Welfare of construction workers; 1-2% cess on project cost.

nd

1999-2002

2 National Commission on
Labour (Ravindra Varma)

Recommended consolidating 44 labour laws into 4 codes. Direct
antecedent of the 2019-2020 labour codes.

2008

Unorganised Workers' Social
Security Act

Based on Arjun Sengupta (NCEUS) report; first statutory framework
for informal-sector SS.

2013

PFRDA Act 2013

Pension Fund Regulatory & Development Authority — statutory basis
of NPS.

2019

Code on Wages 2019

Merged 4 wage-related Acts. First Labour Code to get Presidential
assent (8 Aug 2019).

2020

IR Code · SS Code · OSH Code
(all 28 Sep 2020)

Three codes assented same day. SS Code consolidates 9 SS Acts; IR
Code merges ID+Trade Unions+Standing Orders; OSH merges 13
OSH-related Acts.

2024

EPS 14-Jun-2024 amendment

Table B to 42 yrs · Table D to months · Damages uniform 1%/month.

2025

SS Code 21-Nov-2025
notification

Major provisions enforced (Gazette S.O. 5319(E)). CPPS rolled out 1
Jan 2025.

✔ KEY FACT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 183 · Verified April 2026

Two commissions that shaped Indian labour law: (1) Royal Commission on Labour (Whitley, 1929-31)
— pre-independence blueprint for factories, mines, minimum wage, SS. (2) 2nd National Commission on
Labour (Ravindra Varma, 1999-2002) — recommended consolidating 44 Acts into 4 codes — which finally
happened in 2019-2020. The 1st NCL (Gajendragadkar, 1966-69) recommended minimum wage, grievance
machinery, and the bonus formula.

1881 Fact · 1923 WC/Mines · 1926 TU · 1947 ID · 1948 ESI/MW · 1952 EPF · 1976 EDLI · 1995
EPS · 2020 SS-Code · 2025 SS-Notify
QUICK RECALL

Royal Commission: Whitley, 1929-31
1st NCL Chair: Gajendragadkar (1966-69)
2nd NCL Chair: Ravindra Varma (1999-2002)
Oldest SS Act in force: ESI Act 1948
4 Labour Codes years: Wages 2019 · IR/SS/OSH 2020
SS Code in force: 21 Nov 2025

Concept

Meaning

Fraudulent emails/sites to steal credentials

Malware that encrypts data and demands payment

Distributed Denial of Service — overwhelms server with traffic

Encryption

Converts data to ciphertext using key — AES, RSA

Fingerprint, iris, face — used in Aadhaar, EPFO

Ch-46 APFC Lens — Occupational Health, Diseases & Industrial Safety
APFC papers test applied science — specifically occupational diseases (ESI Act Third Schedule) and
industrial hygiene. Not pure chemistry or physics. The 2025 PYQ Qs 54-58 (photochemical smog, amines,
phenol = carbolic acid) fit this pattern. Memorise the disease-agent mapping below — it covers every
occupational health question.

✔ KEY FACT
ESI Act Third Schedule lists 29 occupational diseases; employer must notify ESIC on diagnosis. Such cases
attract 100% disability pension under ESI + compensation under ECA 1923 (read with Schedule I). Under the
SS Code, this is now unified under the Employees' Compensation chapter + ESI.

Chapter 46 — Science & Technology PYQ Drill — EO/AO 2025
A. Defence / Space / AI Technology — New Question Types
Topic

Correct Fact

Wrong Answers to Avoid

AWACS

Airborne Warning and Control System — an
aircraft-mounted radar system for detecting and
tracking targets

Not 'Arial Warfare', not 'Airborne
Warfare and Critical Solutions'

EM Drone Jamming
— frequency

Uses Microwave frequency to disrupt GPS and
communication systems of drones

Not infrared, not visible light —
microwave is the right band for
GPS/comm disruption (GHz range)

GPS — location
method

Trilateration — measures distances from multiple
satellites and finds the intersection point

Not 'Triangulation' (which measures
angles). Not 'Dilation method'. Not
measurement from one satellite.

ChatGPT / LLMs

ChatGPT is based on Large Language Model
(LLM) — a type of generative AI trained on massive
text data

Not 'predictive AI', not 'high-level
scientific computer language', not just
'chatting software'

GPU vs CPU for AI

GPU (Graphics Processing Unit) is preferred for
training and deploying complex AI models —
massively parallel architecture

CPU handles general computing; GPU
handles matrix operations in AI

SSD vs HDD

SSD: (1) Spinning speed = ZERO (no moving parts);
(2) Can be used as virtual memory; (3) Consumes
LESS energy than HDD (not more)

Trap: SSD does NOT consume more
energy than HDD — it uses LESS. SSD
CANNOT replace the processor.

B. Chemistry — Common-Name Traps + Photochemical Smog
Question Type

Correct Answer

Trap

Carbolic acid

Phenol (C₆H₅OH)

Not alcohol, not ether, not acetone

Primary commercial use
of Amines

Synthesis of medicines and fibres (nylon, drugs,
dyes)

Not explosives (that's nitro
compounds), not batteries, not stem
cells

Primary reactants of
photochemical smog

Nitric Oxide (NO) + Unburnt Hydrocarbons —
they react in sunlight to form ozone and PAN
(peroxyacetyl nitrate)

Ozone is a PRODUCT of
photochemical smog, NOT a
reactant. CO is a primary pollutant
but not the reactant pair for
photochem smog. Sulphur
hexafluoride and soot = industrial, not
photochem smog.

Permafrost thawing —
primary environmental
concern

Release of Methane and Carbon dioxide —
frozen organic matter decomposes, releasing stored
greenhouse gases

Not UV radiation (ozone hole issue);
not invasive species (secondary
effect); not just indigenous
displacement (social, not primary env)

Water hardness —
determined by

Total concentration of Ca²⁺ and Mg²⁺ ions in the
water

Not pH value, not molar ratio of D₂O
to H₂O, not sulphuric acid reference

C. Biology — Pleural Cavity and Fatty Acids

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 196 · Verified April 2026

Topic

Correct Fact

Exam Trap

Pleural cavity
substance

Liquid (pleural fluid) — fills the thin space between
the two pleural layers surrounding the lungs

NOT dry air, not humid air — it is a thin
layer of LIQUID that reduces friction
during breathing

Fatty acids —
structure

Most fatty acids have a carboxyl group (−COOH) at
one end and a methyl group (−CH₃) at the other
end — this is the basic chain structure

Trap: Saturated fatty acids have NO
double bonds — not 'even number' or
'odd number'. Monounsaturated = ONE
double bond. Polyunsaturated = more
than one.

SSL encryption

Secure Socket Layer encryption — the protocol
underlying HTTPS that encrypts browser-server
communication

Not 'Superior Safety Level', not
'Server-to-Server Linked'

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Photochemical Smog — The Reaction Chain:
Step 1: NO + Unburnt Hydrocarbons + Sunlight → NO₂ + Ozone (O₃)
Step 2: Ozone + Hydrocarbons → PAN (peroxyacetyl nitrate) + other oxidants
Ozone and PAN are the products that cause eye irritation and crop damage. The reactants are NO and
unburnt hydrocarbons from vehicle exhaust.

QUICK RECALL
AWACS: Airborne Warning and Control System
Drone jamming freq: Microwave frequency
GPS method: Trilateration (not triangulation)
ChatGPT basis: Large Language Model (LLM)
AI training hardware: GPU (not CPU)
SSD spinning speed: Zero — no moving parts
SSD energy use: LESS than HDD (not more)
Carbolic acid: Phenol
Amines commercial use: Synthesis of medicines and fibres
Photochem smog: reactants: Nitric Oxide + Unburnt Hydrocarbons
Photochem smog: product: Ozone + PAN (ozone is NOT a reactant)
Permafrost thawing: Releases Methane and CO₂
Water hardness: Ca²⁺ and Mg²⁺ ion concentration
Pleural cavity: Liquid (pleural fluid)
Saturated fatty acids: NO double bonds (not even, not odd)
SSL: Secure Socket Layer encryption

Chapter 44 — Human Body · Plant Biology · Chemistry Essentials
These topics appear regularly in APFC science questions — body organ layers, plant root types, acid-base
chemistry, vitamins, and soap vs detergent distinctions.
✔ KEY FACT

STUDY FILTER — Bio + Chemistry Essentials | Tier 2 (3-4 marks)
MEMORIZE: Eye layers (outer→inner): Sclera → Choroid → Retina (SCR) · Fat-soluble vitamins: A, D, E, K
(ADEK) · Others = water-soluble · Deficiency map: A=Night blindness · B1=Beri-Beri · B3=Pellagra · C=Scurvy ·
D=Rickets/Osteomalacia · K=clotting · Plant roots: Banyan=Prop · Mangrove=Pneumatophore · Maize=Stilt ·
Acids by source: Tartaric=Tamarind · Formic=Ant · Lactic=Curd · Citric=Citrus · Acetic=Vinegar ·
Oxalic=Tomato · Soap = Na/K salt of fatty acid (Sodium Benzoate = preservative, NOT soap) · Dry ice = Solid
CO₂ (sublimates) · Heavy water = D₂O (nuclear moderator)
SKIP: Detailed anatomy beyond outer/middle/inner layers · Memorising every vitamin's chemical name (Retinol,
Thiamine etc.) — just the LETTER + deficiency disease · Detailed plant biology beyond root types · Periodic table
by memory · Specific organic compound preparations
TRAP: Vitamin C is NOT fat-soluble (it's water-soluble). Banyan = PROP roots (NOT just aerial). Sodium Benzoate
sounds like soap but is a PRESERVATIVE (E211). Laughing gas = N₂O (NOT NO₂, which is toxic).

A. Structure of the Human Eye — Layers (inside out)
Layer

Position

Function

Key Exam Fact

Sclera

Outermost (white of eye)

Protects the eyeball

White and tough. Anterior = Cornea
(transparent part of sclera).

Choroid

Middle layer

Blood supply to retina; absorbs
stray light (pigmented)

Dark-coloured. Anteriorly forms the
Iris and Ciliary body.

Retina

Innermost layer (back of
eye)

Photoreceptors: Rods (dim
light/BW) and Cones (colour,
bright)

Image formed here — inverted and
real. Fovea = sharpest vision. Blind
spot = where optic nerve exits.

✔ KEY FACT
Eye structure mnemonic: SCR — Sclera · Choroid · Retina (outer to inner). The lens is inside the eye, NOT
a layer — it focuses light onto the Retina. The aqueous humour (front) and vitreous humour (back) fill the eye
chambers.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Fat-soluble vitamins = A, D, E, K (mnemonic: ADEK). All others (B-group, C) are water-soluble. Trap: Vitamin
C is NOT fat-soluble. Rickets = children (Vitamin D deficiency); Osteomalacia = same deficiency in adults.
Scurvy = Vitamin C; Beri-Beri = B1; Pellagra = B3.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 198 · Verified April 2026

C. Plant Root Types — Prop Roots, Pneumatophores, etc.
Root Type

Plant Example

Function / Characteristic

Prop roots (pillar roots)

Banyan tree (Ficus
benghalensis)

Grow downward from branches to soil — act as extra trunks
giving structural support. Distinguishes banyan from other fig
trees. India's national tree.

Pneumatophores
(breathing roots)

Mangroves (Rhizophora,
Avicennia)

Grow vertically upward from soil — expose to air for gas
exchange in waterlogged soil.

Stilt roots

Maize, Sugarcane,
Pandanus

Grow downward obliquely from lower nodes — provide
mechanical support.

Adventitious roots

Grasses, Monstera

Arise from non-root parts (stem, leaf) — not regular root
system.

Tap root

Dicots (carrot, radish)

Single main root with lateral branches — stores food. Radish =
swollen tap root.

Fibrous root

Monocots (grasses,
wheat, rice)

No main root — network of roots from stem base.

Parasitic roots
(Haustoria)

Cuscuta (dodder),
Loranthus

Penetrate host tissue to absorb nutrients.

✔ KEY FACT
Banyan tree = Prop roots (NOT aerial roots — prop roots ARE a type of aerial root, but the specific term is
prop/pillar roots). Mangroves = Pneumatophores (breathing roots pointing upward). Maize = Stilt roots
(oblique downward support).

D. Soap vs Detergent & Common Acid Sources
Item

Chemical Nature

Key Fact

Soap

Sodium or Potassium salt of
long-chain fatty acids

Sodium stearate, sodium palmitate are soaps. Soap does
NOT work in hard water (forms scum with Ca²⁺/Mg²⁺).

Detergent

Synthetic surfactant
(sulphonate/sulphate salts)

Works in hard water. NOT a soap. Biodegradable
detergents preferred.

Sodium Benzoate

Sodium salt of benzoic acid — NOT a
soap

Used as FOOD PRESERVATIVE (E211). Trap: sounds like
soap but is NOT.

Tartaric acid

Naturally occurring dicarboxylic acid
(C₄H₆O₆)

Found in tamarind and grapes. Used in baking powder.

Citric acid

Organic acid found in citrus fruits

Lemons, oranges. Mnemonic: Citrus = Citric acid.

Acetic acid

CH₃COOH — carboxylic acid

Found in vinegar (3-5% solution). Glacial acetic acid =
pure.

Formic acid

HCOOH — simplest carboxylic acid

Found in ant sting and stinging nettle. Mnemonic:
FORMica = ant.

Oxalic acid

C₂H₂O₄ — dicarboxylic acid

Found in tomato and spinach. Poisonous in excess.

Lactic acid

CH₃CH(OH)COOH

In curd (fermentation by Lactobacillus). Causes muscle
fatigue.

E. Common Oxidation States & Chemistry One-Liners
Element / Compound

Key Fact

Iron (Fe) oxidation states

Fe²⁺ (ferrous, in haemoglobin) and Fe³⁺ (ferric). Ferric iron = rust (Fe₂O₃). Fe²⁺ in
FeSO₄ (green vitriol).

Chromium oxidation states

Cr³⁺ (stable, in stainless steel) and Cr⁶⁺ (toxic, carcinogenic — in chromate ions).

Hydrogen peroxide (H₂O₂)

Both oxidising AND reducing agent. Bleaches hair. 'Volume strength' = amount of
O₂ released.

Bleaching powder

Ca(OCl)Cl — calcium hypochlorite + calcium chloride. Active ingredient: Cl₂/OCl⁻.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 199 · Verified April 2026

Element / Compound

Key Fact

Dry ice

Solid CO₂ — sublimates (no liquid phase at atmospheric pressure). NOT water ice.

Heavy water (D₂O)

Deuterium oxide — used as moderator in nuclear reactors (not ordinary water).

Laughing gas

Nitrous oxide (N₂O) — used as anaesthetic, NOT NO₂ (toxic nitrogen dioxide).

Plaster of Paris

CaSO₄·½H₂O (hemihydrate of calcium sulphate). Sets by absorbing water.

Fat-soluble vitamins: A, D, E, K (ADEK)
Beri-Beri: Vitamin B1 (Thiamine) deficiency
Scurvy: Vitamin C deficiency — bleeding gums
Rickets: Vitamin D deficiency — children
Banyan roots: Prop roots (pillar roots) — downward from branches
Mangrove roots: Pneumatophores — upward breathing roots
Maize roots: Stilt roots — oblique support
Sodium Benzoate: Food preservative, NOT a soap
Tartaric acid in: Tamarind and grapes
Formic acid in: Ant sting
Lactic acid in: Curd (Lactobacillus fermentation)
Dry ice: Solid CO₂ — sublimes at atmospheric pressure
Heavy water: D₂O — nuclear reactor moderator
Laughing gas: N₂O (nitrous oxide), NOT NO₂

Chapter 48 — Key SC & HC Judgments — APFC-Relevant
Landmark rulings that shape EPFO and labour law. Each row below is enough for one MCQ. Memorise
year, court, and the single-line holding.
Case

Year

Court

Holding / Significance

Sunil Kumar vs EPFO

4 Nov 2022

SC

Allowed higher pension option for members who
contributed EPF on actual salary above ₹15,000.
Reshaped EPS-95 completely.

RC Gupta vs RPFC

Oct 2016

SC

Allowed contribution to EPS on actual salary
(precursor to Sunil Kumar).

Surya Roshni vs EPFO

28 Feb 2019

SC

Universally paid, ordinarily paid, regular allowances
are part of Basic Wages for PF. Burden of proof on
employer.

Bridge & Roof vs UoI

1963

SC

Defined 'Basic Wages' strictly; laid foundation for
allowance inclusion tests.

Bangalore Water Supply &
Sewerage Board vs A. Rajappa

1978

SC (7-judge)

Expansive definition of 'industry' — includes Govt
departments, hospitals, charities, universities.

Telangana HC (EPFO)

2022

Telangana HC

Deadline for higher pension option to be extended;
contributed to July 2023 deadline.

Bombay HC — Kiran Rajaram
Jadhav vs EPFO

2026

Bombay HC

Employee cannot be denied higher pension solely due
to missing employer records; EPFO set-aside order.

Hindustan Times Ltd vs Union

1998

SC

Regular wages test for allowances — a precursor to
Surya Roshni.

RPFC (II) WB vs
Vivekananda Vidyamandir

28 Feb 2019

SC

Held alongside Surya Roshni — 'basic wages'
includes all allowances paid universally,
necessarily and ordinarily to all employees
generally. Triggered wave of S.7A notices; EPFO later
issued 14-Feb-2020 & subsequent guidelines curbing
'roving' enquiries.

D. Chandirasegar & Ors. vs
UoI (BHEL Trichy retirees)

Sep 2025

Madras HC
(Madurai
Bench)

Quashed EPFO circular dated 18-Jan-2025; held that
Section 17 exemption from the PF Scheme does NOT
extend to the Pension Scheme (EPS). Joint-option
applications by 31-Jan-2025 must be accepted. 86
retirees' petitions allowed.

✔ KEY FACT
Bangalore Water Supply (1978) gave a 7-judge bench ruling that vastly expanded the meaning of 'industry'
under the Industrial Disputes Act. Even universities, charities, clubs, and Government departments fall under the
definition. Still good law.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Sunil Kumar (2022) + RC Gupta (2016) are the two pillars of higher-pension jurisprudence. Surya Roshni
(2019) is the pillar for basic-wages jurisprudence. Know these three by name and year.

MNEMONIC
R.C. Gupta 2016 → Sunil Kumar 2022 → Deadline Jul 2023 → 99% done Nov 2025
QUICK RECALL
Higher pension ruling: Sunil Kumar — 4 Nov 2022 SC
Basic Wages ruling: Surya Roshni — 28 Feb 2019 SC

Ch-48 Drill — 15-Case Single-Page Reference
Beyond the core 10 listed above, five more rulings recur in APFC papers. Memorise case name · year ·
court · one-line holding. Sources cross-referenced: SCC / AIR / indiankanoon.org.
Case

Year

Court

Holding

Surendra Kumar Verma vs
CGIT

1980

SC

240-day rule — a workman who has worked 240 days in the
preceding 12 months is in 'continuous service' (ID Act / Gratuity).

Workmen of Dimakuchi Tea
Estate vs Management

1958

SC

An individual dispute becomes 'industrial dispute' only when
espoused by a substantial body of workmen. (Later diluted
by S.2A for termination cases.)

Maneka Gandhi vs Union of
India

1978

SC

Expansive reading of Article 21 — procedure must be 'just, fair
and reasonable'. Foundation of every subsequent APFC-relevant
Article-21 claim.

D.S. Nakara vs Union of India

1983

SC

Pension is deferred wage, not a bounty. Arbitrary cut-off dates
for pension revisions violate Article 14 — cited in higher-pension
petitions.

Regional Provident Fund
Commissioner vs Shiv Kumar
Joshi

14 Dec
1999

SC

EPFO is a 'service provider' under Consumer Protection Act —
members are 'consumers' under S.2(1)(d) and can approach
Consumer Forum for deficiency (e.g. delayed settlement). District
Forum earlier granted 18% interest on delayed PF settlement; SC
upheld.

MNEMONIC
240 days · espousal · Art-21 procedure · deferred wage · consumer forum

Key Fact

Trap to Avoid

D. Schemes & Economic Initiatives — EO/AO 2025 Tested Items

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 206 · Verified April 2026

Scheme / Initiative

Key Number / Detail

Trap

ELI (Employment Linked
Incentive) Scheme

Outlay: ₹99,446 crore (~₹1 lakh crore) over 2
years · Cabinet approved 1 Jul 2025;
operational from 1 Aug 2025 · Part A:
first-timers (up to ₹15,000, one-month EPF
wages) · Part B: employers (up to ₹3,000/month
per new hire) · Target: 3.5 crore new formal jobs
(Aug 2025 – Jul 2027) · Implemented through
EPFO

Do NOT confuse with PLI (Production
Linked Incentive). ELI is about
employment creation, PLI is about
production output. ELI is NOW LIVE —
not just announced.

EPFO Auto-Settlement —
first launched

First introduced during COVID-19 pandemic
(2020) for non-refundable advance claims ·
ceiling was ₹50,000 initially

Trap: question may ask 'when was
auto-settlement FIRST introduced?'
Answer = COVID era (2020), not 2024
(that was the ₹1 lakh hike).

EPFO Auto-Settlement
ceiling progression

₹50,000 (2020, COVID launch) → ₹1 lakh (236th
CBT, Nov 2024) → ₹5 lakh (24 Jun 2025)

3-step progression. Know all three
amounts AND the dates. The 2026
exam window expects ₹5 lakh as
current ceiling.

PM POSHAN (Mid-Day
Meal)

Covers Classes I–VIII in govt & govt-aided
schools

Trap: was earlier only I–V, then
extended. Current = I–VIII. Do NOT
write I–X or pre-primary.

BioE3 Policy

Biomanufacturing policy; target: bio-economy to
4.25% of GDP by 2030 (announced 2024)

Trap: do NOT confuse the 4.25% GDP
target with the MSME or
manufacturing sector targets.

Zero Defect Zero Effect
(ZED)

Quality + environmental compliance initiative for
MSMEs. Administered by Ministry of MSME
through Quality Council of India.

Trap: Skill Development or Heavy
Industries — answer is MSME. ZED =
'zero defect + zero environmental
effect'. PLI is for large manufacturers
under various sector ministries.

✔ KEY FACT
ELI vs PLI quick rule: ELI = Employment (worker gets incentive/subsidy). PLI = Production (manufacturer gets
incentive on incremental output). Both are large schemes but tested separately in exams.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
EPFO auto-settlement timeline trap: The concept started in COVID 2020. The ceiling was ₹50K then. 236th
CBT (Nov 2024) raised it to ₹1L. Then 24-Jun-2025 raised it to ₹5L. A question asking 'first introduced?' needs
COVID-2020 as the answer, NOT 2024.

Chapter 49 — Government Schemes — Full Drill (HIGH HIT)
5-7 marks per paper from this section. The trick is NOT to memorise 30+ schemes alphabetically, but to
group them by purpose. Each scheme = (Launch year, Ministry, Target group, Key benefit,
Special feature). That's the question template.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Government Schemes | Tier 1 (5-7 marks)
MEMORIZE — For each scheme, 4 things: Launch year · Ministry · Target group · Headline number · The
Three ₹3,000 Pensions (2019): PM-SYM (workers) / PM-KMY (farmers) / PM-LVMA (traders) · The Three 2015
Bank-linked (9 May): PMJJBY (₹2L life @ ₹436) / PMSBY (₹2L accident @ ₹20) / APY (₹1-5K pension) · MUDRA:
Shishu (≤50K) · Kishor (50K-5L) · Tarun (5L-10L) · Tarun Plus (5-20L, 2024) · PM-KISAN: ₹6,000/yr in 3
instalments · Ayushman Bharat: ₹5L health cover; Vay Vandana 2024 = ALL 70+ regardless of income · ELI
Aug 2025: ₹15K to first-timers, ₹3K/month to employers, ₹99,446 Cr
SKIP: Every minor scheme launched · Specific budget allocation in crores for every scheme · Pre-2014 schemes
(mostly subsumed/renamed) · State-specific scheme variants
TRAP — Scheme-Ministry pairings: PMEGP = MSME (NOT Skill Dev), PMFME = Food Processing (NOT MSME),
Stand-Up India = DFS (NOT MSME), Vishwakarma = MSME, Aspirational Districts = NITI Aayog, e-Shram =
Labour.

✔ KEY FACT
The Three '₹3,000 Pension at 60' Schemes — Memory Trick:
All three launched in 2019 for unorganised workers. Distinguished by TARGET:
PM-SYM (Feb 2019) → Unorganised WORKERS (Shram Yogi)
PM-KMY (Sept 2019) → FARMERS (Kisan)
PM-LVMA (July 2019) → TRADERS (Laghu Vyapari)
All three: 18-40 entry · 50:50 contribution · ₹3,000/month pension at 60.
The Three '2015 Bank-Linked' Schemes — Memory Trick (9 May 2015):
PMJJBY → Life cover (₹2L for ₹436/yr)
PMSBY → Accident cover (₹2L for ₹20/yr)
APY → Pension (₹1K-5K/month at 60)

Chapter 53 — 25-Point Last-Night Cheat-Sheet
The 25 highest-probability facts — the final revision list the night before the exam. Cross-check you know
each one cold.
FORMULA / KEY POINTS
1. EPF Act: Act 19 of 1952 · 4 March 1952
2. EPS: in force 19 November 1995 · Sec 6A EPF Act
3. EDLI: 1976 · Sec 6C EPF Act
4. Employee 12% · Employer 12% (3.67+8.33) · CG 1.16%
5. EPS wage ceiling ₹15,000 · EPS max employer ₹1,250
6. EPS formula = (PS × Svc) ÷ 70 · +2 bonus if Svc > 20 · Min ₹1,000
7. EDLI max ₹7L · Min ₹2.5L · bonus ₹1.75L (35 × wage)
8. EPF rate 2025-26 = 8.25% (3rd yr; 239th CBT)
9. 7Q = 12% p.a. simple (unchanged)
10. 14B damages = 1%/month uniform from 15 June 2024 (old slab 5-25%)
11. 14AB — cognizable & non-bailable (deducted but not deposited)
12. Basic Wages: Surya Roshni SC 28 Feb 2019 — universal allowances IN
13. Higher Pension: Sunil Kumar SC 4 Nov 2022 · deadline 11 Jul 2023 · ~99% done Nov 2025
14. SS Code: Assent 28 Sep 2020 · in force 21 Nov 2025 · 9 Acts
15. FTE Gratuity = 1 year under SS Code Sec 53 (was 5 years)
16. IR Code Chapter VB threshold = 300+ workers (was 100 under IDA)
17. Strike notice 60 days for all industries under IR Code (was 14 days PU only)
18. Factories: Canteen 250 · Crèche 30 women · Welfare 500 · Safety 1000
19. ESI = 0.75 + 3.25 = 4% · Wage cap ₹21K · Funeral ₹15K
20. Gratuity max ₹20 L · Formula (15/26) × last wage × years
21. Bonus min 8.33% · max 20% · ceiling ₹21K · calc cap ₹7K / min wage
22. CPPS live 1 Jan 2025 · 122 ROs · ~70 lakh pensioners
23. APFC 2025: 74 posts · Advt 52/2025 · Exam 30 Nov 2025
24. CAG: Art 148 · 6 yrs or 65 yrs whichever earlier · EPFO audit Sec 5A(6)
25. UNESCO India sites = 42 · latest = Moidams, Assam (2024)

✔ KEY FACT
You are ready. Sleep well, arrive early, fill the OMR carefully, attempt with discipline. If you know these 25
points, your floor is a decent shortlist. Everything else is bonus.

Chapter 53 — Numbers & Rules Precision Patch — EO/AO 2025
EO/AO 2025 (Nov 30, 2025) tested several precision facts not captured in the main Numbers Bible above.
This patch adds must-know numbers and rules that tripped candidates.
Rule / Concept

Precise Fact

Trap / Note

IDA Sec 22 — PU Strike
Notice

Notice must be given 6 WEEKS in advance
AND strike cannot begin within 14 days of
giving notice (cooling period). Applies to
BOTH workmen (before strike) AND employers
(before lockout) in PU services.

Exam may ask: 'What is the notice
period?' → 14 days (correct MCQ
answer). Tea Plantation IS a PU
service (confirmed APFC 2025 Q90).
Pyrites Mining is NOT. 6-week notice
= PUS only; not all industries.

EC Act — One Eye = ?

Loss of sight in one eye = permanent
PARTIAL disablement (Part II, Schedule I of EC
Act 1923).

Trap: candidates mark 'total'. TOTAL =
both eyes or limbs that render worker
permanently unable to do ANY work.
One eye = 40% of compensation for
total disablement.

AS-1 Fundamental
Accounting Assumptions

Only THREE: Going Concern · Accrual ·
Consistency.

Matching is NOT a fundamental AS-1
assumption (it is a principle/concept, not
an assumption). This is the #1
accounting trap in every EPFO paper.

Capital Account —
Debit/Credit

Capital account has a CREDIT balance
(permanent credit). It increases by Credit and
decreases by Debit.

Trap: candidates say 'debit balance'.
Capital is a liability of the business to the
owner → credit normal balance.

Core Inflation

Excludes BOTH food AND energy prices.
Headline inflation = CPI including food +
energy.

Trap: some options say 'excludes food
only' or 'excludes fuel only'. Both food
AND energy are excluded from core
inflation.

RTI Act — CIC
Appointment Committee

PM (chair) + Leader of Opposition in Lok
Sabha + a Cabinet Minister nominated by PM.

Trap: options include CJI (Chief Justice).
CJI is NOT on the appointment
committee. No reappointment for CIC or
ICs. CEC appointment also involves PM
but NOT CJI.

GST Council — PM as
member?

NO. GST Council: Union Finance Minister
(Chair) + MOS Finance + State Finance
Ministers.

PM is NOT a member. President is NOT a
member. Centre has 1/3 weightage;
States together have 2/3.

ELI Scheme outlay

₹1 lakh crore (₹1 trillion) over 5 years;
Budget 2024-25 announcement.

3 components: A (first-time formal
sector workers), B (manufacturing), C
(employer incentive for new hires). ELI ≠
PLI (Production Linked Incentive).

Operating Profit formula

Operating Profit = Net Profit + non-operating Trap: candidates add non-op incomes
expenses − non-operating incomes.
and subtract non-op expenses
(backwards). Non-op expenses like
interest are added back to isolate
operating performance.

Photochemical Smog
REACTANTS

REACTANTS = NO (nitric oxide) +
Hydrocarbons. These react in sunlight to
PRODUCE ozone + PAN + aldehydes.

Trap: options include ozone as a
reactant. Ozone is a PRODUCT of the
photochemical reaction, NOT a starting
reactant.

Carbolic Acid = ?

Carbolic acid = Phenol (C₆H₅OH). Also known
as hydroxybenzene.

Trap: options include formic acid, acetic
acid. Carbolic acid ≠ any carboxylic acid.
It is an aromatic alcohol.

Services sector GVA
share (India)

~55% of India's Gross Value Added.

Agriculture ~18%, Industry ~27%,
Services ~55%. Services include IT,
banking, transport, govt, trade.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 224 · Verified April 2026

Rule / Concept

Precise Fact

Trap / Note

IFCI founding year

IFCI (Industrial Finance Corporation of India)
established 1948.

Trap: options say 1950 or 1955. IFCI =
first DFI in India, set up in 1948. IDBI
came later (1964).

Fiscal Health Index 2025

Released by NITI Aayog; covers 18 states; 5
sub-indices (revenue, expenditure, fiscal
prudence, debt, GSDP).

Trap: some say RBI or Finance Ministry
publishes it. NITI Aayog released the FHI
in 2025.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
EO/AO 2025 Top-5 traps (by difficulty, from real paper):
1. AS-1 — Matching is NOT an assumption (Going Concern + Accrual + Consistency).
2. One eye = PARTIAL disablement under EC Act (not total).
3. IDA First Schedule: Tea Plantation IS a PU service; Pyrites Mining is NOT.
4. Core inflation excludes BOTH food AND energy (not just food).
5. RTI appointment committee = PM (not CJI). No reappointment for anyone.

MNEMONIC
AS-1=3 assumptions (GAC, not Matching) · One-eye=Partial · Tea=PU, Pyrites≠PU ·
Core=no-food-no-energy · RTI=PM · Capital=Credit
QUICK RECALL
AS-1 assumptions (3 only): Going Concern · Accrual · Consistency
Matching principle: NOT an AS-1 assumption
Capital account balance: CREDIT (liability to owner)
EC Act — one eye: Permanent PARTIAL disablement
Core inflation excludes: BOTH food AND energy
RTI CIC appointment chair: Prime Minister (NOT CJI)
GST Council Chair: Union Finance Minister
IDA Sec 22 cooling period: 14 days after notice
IDA Sec 22 notice window: Can give notice up to 6 weeks before
Tea Plantation re: PU: IS a PU service — Pyrites Mining is NOT (APFC 2025)
IFCI founded: 1948 — first DFI in India
ELI Scheme outlay: ₹1 lakh crore over 5 years
Photochemical smog reactants: NO + Hydrocarbons (ozone = product)
Carbolic acid: Phenol (C₆H₅OH)
Services sector GVA: ~55% of India GVA

B. ILO Conventions — India's Ratification Status
Tested in 2023 APFC (Q112): 'Which ILO convention has India NOT ratified?' India has NOT ratified
Convention No. 87 (Freedom of Association), because it conflicts with restrictions on government
employees' right to strike.
Convention No.

Subject

India Status

No. 29 (1930)

Forced Labour

Ratified ✓

No. 87 (1948)

Freedom of Association & Protection of TU Rights

NOT Ratified ✗ — Conflicts with
Govt employee restrictions

No. 98 (1949)

Right to Organise & Collective Bargaining

NOT Ratified ✗

No. 100 (1951)

Equal Remuneration

Ratified ✓

No. 111 (1958)

Discrimination (Employment & Occupation)

Ratified ✓

No. 138 (1973)

Minimum Age (child labour)

NOT Ratified ✗

No. 182 (1999)

Worst Forms of Child Labour

Ratified ✓ (2017)

⚠ EXAM ALERT
ILO Convention No. 87 = India has NOT ratified. This is the single most-tested ILO convention fact. India
has signed Conventions 29, 100, 111, 182 — but NOT 87 (Freedom of Association) or 98 (Collective Bargaining)
or 138 (Minimum Age).

Tested 2023 APFC (Q97). Certification by
Senior Advocate needed.

Governor's Ordinance power

NOT discretionary — must act on
CM's advice; covers State +
Concurrent lists only

Tested 2023 APFC (Q96). Governer CANNOT
ordinance on Union List.

✔ KEY FACT
RTGS/NEFT owned by RBI, NOT NPCI — a perennial trap. NPCI = National Payments Corporation of India
(promoted by RBI + banks). NPCI runs: UPI · IMPS · RuPay · NACH · ChequeQ · AePS. RBI directly owns: RTGS ·
NEFT · Bharat Bill Payment System (via NPCI subsidiary).

MNEMONIC
NPCI runs UPI-IMPS-RuPay-NACH | RBI owns RTGS-NEFT | G20 = 19 nations + EU | Unicorn =
$1B | Art 43 = Living Wage | AITUC-1920-CPI → INTUC-1947-INC → BMS-1955-BJP →
CITU-1970-CPI(M)
QUICK RECALL
TU federations order: AITUC 1920 · INTUC 1947 · HMS 1948 · UTUC 1949 · BMS 1955 · CITU 1970 · SEWA 1972
ILO No. 87: NOT ratified by India (Freedom of Association)

53D.4 Score Math — How to Hit 90+
Tier

Target accuracy

Marks contribution

Tier 1 (60-70 Qs)

90%+ (lose max 6-7 Qs)

55-60 correct

Tier 2 (20-25 Qs)

75%+ (lose max 5-6 Qs)

15-20 correct

Tier 3 (10-15 Qs)

50% (attempt only if confident)

5-8 correct

Quant/English/Reasoning (25-30 Qs)

80%+ with practice

20-25 correct

TOTAL TARGET

95-113 correct

✔ KEY FACT
The 80/20 Strategy for 90+:
Don't waste time on Tier 3 mastery. Get Tier 1 to 95%+ accuracy — that alone gives you ~60 correct. Tier 2 at
75% gives ~18. Quant/Reasoning at 80% gives ~22. Total = ~100 correct = 250/300 = comfortable rank.
What kills 90+:
(1) Trying to attempt everything → negative marking eats you.
(2) Spending equal time on all subjects → Tier 1 must dominate revision.
(3) Mock tests under timing pressure not done → no calibration.
(4) Last-week current affairs ignored → 3-5 sitting ducks missed.

PDF. Your job is NOT to find new material — it is to drill these specific chapters until automatic. The
references point you to exactly where to revise.
