Chapter 54 — Numerical Formula Cheat-Sheet — Every
Calculation in One Place
2-4 marks per paper come from straight-up calculations: EPS pension, EDLI, gratuity, ESI, EC Act
compensation, bonus. This single chapter is your last-day revision sheet for every formula. Memorise
these and you cannot lose a calculation question.

54.1 EPS Monthly Pension Formula
Formula

Step

Example

(Pensionable Salary × Pensionable
Service) ÷ 70

Find pensionable salary (avg last 60 months,
cap ₹15,000)

₹15,000 × 30 ÷ 70 =
₹6,428

If service > 20 yrs: add 2-year bonus
to service

Example: 25 yrs → use 27 yrs for calc

₹15,000 × 27 ÷ 70 =
₹5,785

If <58 yrs (Early Pension): reduce by
4% per year

Reduction = 4% × (58 − actual age) on full
pension

Full ₹6,000 at 55 = ₹6,000
× (1 - 0.12) = ₹5,280

If >58 (Deferred): increase by 4% per
year (max age 60)

Add 4% × (actual age - 58)

₹6,000 at 60 = ₹6,000 ×
1.08 = ₹6,480

Floor

Minimum ₹1,000/month even if formula gives
lower

Minimum service for pension

10 years; if less → Withdrawal Benefit only
(Table D)

54.2 EDLI Benefit Formula
Formula

Floor / Ceiling

Example

EDLI = 35 × Avg Wage (last 12 months,
cap ₹15K) + ₹1,75,000 bonus

Floor: ₹2,50,000
Ceiling: ₹7,00,000

Wage ₹15K → 35×15K + 1.75L =
₹6,75,000 (within cap)

If wage < ₹15K

Use actual avg wage

Wage ₹10K → 35×10K + 1.75L =
₹5,25,000

If formula < ₹2,50,000

Pay floor ₹2,50,000 (minimum)

If formula > ₹7,00,000

Cap at ₹7,00,000 (max)

2025 amendment

₹50,000 minimum for workers
with up to 6-month coverage gap

Contribution

0.50% of wages by EMPLOYER
ONLY (capped at ₹15K wage →
max ₹75/month)

Wage ₹20K capped → 35×15K +
1.75L = ₹6,75,000

Admin charges = NIL (since Apr
2017)

54.3 Gratuity Formula (Payment of Gratuity Act 1972)
Type

Formula

Example

Covered under Act (most
workers)

(Last drawn salary × 15 × Completed
years) ÷ 26

₹30K × 15 × 10 ÷ 26 =
₹1,73,077

NOT covered under Act
(voluntary)

(Avg salary last 10 months × 15 × Years) ÷ 30

₹30K × 15 × 10 ÷ 30 =
₹1,50,000

Seasonal employees

(Wages × 7 × Years) ÷ 26

FTE workers (SS Code 2020)

Same formula but eligible after 1 year (not 5)

Maximum ceiling

₹20 lakh (tax-exempt under Sec 10(10)
Income Tax)

Above ₹20L = taxable

54.4 ESI Contribution & Benefit
Item

Rate / Formula

Example

Wage ceiling

₹21,000/month (₹25,000 for disability)

Employee contribution

0.75% of wages

₹20K wage → ₹150/month

Employer contribution

3.25% of wages

₹20K wage → ₹650/month

Total contribution

4% of wages

₹20K wage → ₹800/month

Sickness benefit

70% of avg daily wage × 91 days/year

Maternity benefit

100% of avg daily wage × 26 weeks

Disablement
(Temporary)

90% × wage × duration of disability

Disablement
(Permanent)

90% × wage × monthly for life (work-related only)

Dependent benefit
(death)

90% × wage × monthly to widow/children/parents

Funeral expense

₹15,000 lump sum

54.5 EC Act 1923 — Compensation Formulas
Situation

Formula

Floor

Death

50% × Monthly Wage × Relevant Factor (RF)

Min ₹1,20,000

Permanent Total Disablement
(PTD)

60% × Monthly Wage × Relevant Factor

Min ₹1,40,000

Permanent Partial
Disablement (PPD)

(% of disablement per Schedule I, Part II) × PTD
amount

% varies — e.g., loss of 1
eye = 40%

Temporary Disablement

25% × Monthly Wage × Period (max 5 yrs)

First 3 days no benefit

Wage cap for calculation

₹15,000/month (used if actual wage higher)

EC Act 1923 — Key Relevant Factor (RF) Values (Schedule IV):
Age 16: 228.54 (max RF)
Age 25: 219.95
Age 30: 207.98
Age 35: 197.06
Age 40: 184.17
Age 45: 169.44
Age 48: 174.63 (NOTE: 174.63 is the value at 48 per the schedule; the schedule increases age by age — verify
exact value)
Age 50: 159.80
Age 60: 121.79
Age 65: 99.37 (min)
Worked Example (Dec 2025 Q88): Worker age 48, fatal accident, wages ₹12,000/month. Compensation =
50% × 12,000 × 159.80 = ₹9,58,800. (The Q used RF 159.80 — verify which age. Some tables show 174.63 at
age 48; depends on which schedule version.)

54.6 Payment of Bonus Calculation
Aspect

Rule

Example

Eligibility wage limit

≤ ₹21,000/month

Worker earning ₹25K = NOT
eligible

Wage for bonus calc

Up to ₹7,000/month OR Min Wage (whichever
HIGHER)

Wage ₹18K, Min Wage ₹9K → use
₹9,000

Minimum bonus

8.33% of annual wage = 1 month's salary

₹9,000 × 12 × 8.33% =
₹8,996/year

Maximum bonus

20% of annual wage

₹9,000 × 12 × 20% =
₹21,600/year

Qualifying service

30 working days in accounting year

Payment deadline

Within 8 months of close of accounting year

Newcomer grace

First 5 years — bonus only if PROFIT (no minimum
mandatory)

54.7 EPF Contribution & Interest
Item

Rate / Amount

Notes

Wage ceiling (statutory)

₹15,000/month

Above this = voluntary higher
contribution OR exempted under
Sec 17

Employee contribution

12% of wages

All goes to EPF account

Employer contribution

12% of wages, but SPLIT:
• 8.33% (cap ₹15K wage) → EPS
• 3.67% → EPF
• 0.50% → EDLI (additional, employer only)
• 0.50% → EPF Admin (additional)

Total employer outflow = 13%

EPS portion cap

8.33% × ₹15,000 = ₹1,250/month max to EPS

If wage > ₹15K, excess goes to
EPF (not EPS)

EPF interest rate (FY
2025-26)

8.25% (same as FY 2024-25; approved by CBT Mar
2026)

Section 7Q Interest
(defaulted employer)

12% per annum simple interest on delayed deposits

Chapter 54 — Population & Development
India overtook China as the world's most populous country in April 2023 (UN DESA estimate). The APFC
paper tests demographic headline numbers (TFR, sex ratio, dependency ratio) and the policy concepts of
demographic dividend and window of opportunity.

✔ KEY FACT
Demographic dividend = the growth boost when working-age (15-64) share rises faster than dependents.
India's window is approximately 2005-2055, peaking around 2040. Source: EAC-PM India Demography report.

Chapter 55 — Environment & Disaster Management
Two high-yield tracks: (i) India's climate commitments under the Paris Agreement and updated NDC
2022, and (ii) the legal architecture under the Disaster Management Act 2005. Source: MoEFCC, UNFCCC,
NDMA.

Paris Agreement & India's Updated NDC
Milestone

Year

Detail

Paris Agreement
(COP-21)

2015

Hold global warming well below 2°C, pursue 1.5°C — legally
binding, 196 parties. India ratified 2 Oct 2016.

India's first NDC

2015 (pre-COP21)

Emissions intensity of GDP ↓33-35% by 2030 (vs 2005); 40% non-fossil
installed capacity by 2030; additional carbon sink 2.5-3 bn tCO₂e via
forest/tree cover.

'Panchamrit'
commitments

COP-26 (Glasgow,
2021)

500 GW non-fossil capacity · 50% energy from renewables · reduce 1
bn tCO₂ by 2030 · emissions intensity ↓45% · Net Zero by 2070.

Updated NDC submitted

Aug 2022 (UNFCCC)

Emissions intensity ↓45% by 2030 (vs 2005) · about 50% cumulative
non-fossil installed capacity by 2030.

50% non-fossil milestone June 2025

Achieved 5 years ahead of 2030 deadline — announced by MNRE.
(PIB release, June 2025.)

COP-28

Nov-Dec 2023
(Dubai)

First 'Global Stocktake'; call to 'transition away from fossil fuels'.

COP-29

Nov 2024 (Baku)

New climate finance goal: $300 bn/yr by 2035 from developed to
developing countries (a trebling from $100 bn).

COP-30

10-21 Nov 2025
(Belém, Brazil)

Concluded 22 Nov with agreement to triple adaptation finance for
developing countries. Fossil-fuel phase-out roadmap remained
contested.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Do NOT confuse the first NDC (2015: 33-35%, 40%) with the updated NDC (Aug 2022: 45%, 50%).
Aug-2022 figures are the current commitment. India reached the 50% non-fossil installed-capacity target in
June 2025 — five years early.

Chapter 57 — General English & Comprehension
UPSC papers typically include one short reading-comprehension passage plus a cluster of grammar /
vocabulary items. APFC 2023 and 2025 papers followed the same pattern. Scoring here is about
time-management — never spend more than ~1 min per item.

Grammar — Quick Reference
Topic

Rule (one-line)

Typical APFC example

Subject-verb
agreement

Singular subject → singular verb; 'neither/either…
nor/or' — verb agrees with the nearer subject.

'Neither the manager nor the clerks are
present.'

Tenses — present
perfect

Use with 'since', 'for', 'just', 'already', 'yet'.

'She has worked here since 2019.'

Tenses — past perfect

Earlier of two past actions.

'The train had left before we reached.'

Prepositions — fixed

Different from, prefer X to Y, superior to,
compare with.

'This policy is superior to the earlier
one.'

Articles

'A' before consonant sound, 'an' before vowel sound;
'the' for specific/unique.

'It is a one-time offer' (one starts /w/).

Modals

'Can' = ability; 'could' = past/polite; 'may' =
permission/possibility; 'might' = less probable.

'The scheme may be revised.' (polite)

Conditionals

Type-1 (real future): If + present, will + verb; Type-2
(unreal): If + past, would + verb.

'If the rate were 9%, corpus would
double faster.'

Active ↔ Passive

Object of active becomes subject of passive; 'by +
agent'.

'The Board issued the circular' → 'The
circular was issued by the Board.'

Reported speech

Back-shift tenses; change pronouns and time
expressions.

'He said, "I will come tomorrow"' → 'He
said he would come the next day.'

Direct ↔ Indirect
questions

Drop the question-word-first inversion; use
statement order.

'He asked where I lived' (not 'where
did I live').

Parallelism

Items in a series must have the same grammatical
form.

'She enjoys reading, writing and to
cook.' — wrong; → 'and cooking'.

Dangling modifiers

A participial phrase must modify the grammatical
subject.

'Walking to office, the rain started.' —
wrong.

Vocabulary — Exam-Relevant Words
Word

Meaning (one-line)

Near-synonym / opposite

Adjudicate

To decide a dispute judicially.

≈ rule, judge · ≠ ignore

Mitigate

To make less severe.

≈ alleviate · ≠ aggravate

Prudent

Showing careful judgment.

≈ wise · ≠ reckless

Ratify

To confirm formally.

≈ endorse · ≠ reject

Circumvent

To avoid (rules / obstacles) by going around.

≈ bypass · ≠ comply

Promulgate

To formally put a law / ordinance into effect.

≈ enact · ≠ repeal

Exemplary

Serving as a desirable model.

≈ model · ≠ shoddy

Subsume

To include within a larger category.

≈ encompass · ≠ exclude

Emolument

Remuneration, salary.

≈ compensation, pay

Gratuity

Payment as recognition of service (statutory under Act
1972).

≈ bonus, tip

Advanced Vocabulary — APFC 2020/2023/2025 Tested Words

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 243 · Verified April 2026

Word

Meaning

Mnemonic / Usage

Egregious

Outstandingly bad, shocking.

egr = extra + grievous → extremely grievous.
'Egregious violation of rules.'

Apostate

Person who abandons a religion, belief, or cause.

apo = away + stat (stand) → stood away from
belief. Antonym: devotee.

Corporeity

The state of having a physical body; bodily
existence.

corpus = body. 'Corporeity of a legal entity' —
rare; can appear as meaning trap.

Impugned

Disputed or called into question (especially
someone's integrity).

'His decision was impugned in court.' ≈
challenged, contested.

Erstwhile

Former, previous.

'The erstwhile minister.' = the former minister.
≠ current.

Cordial

Warm and friendly.

'A cordial relationship between the parties.' ≠
hostile.

Benevolent

Well-meaning, kind, generous.

bene = good + volent = wish. 'A benevolent
employer.' ≈ magnanimous.

Acrimonious

Bitter and angry in tone.

'Acrimonious dispute.' ≠ cordial. Antonym:
amicable.

Perfidious

Deceitful, untrustworthy.

'Perfidious conduct' = betrayal of trust. ≈
treacherous.

Sagacious

Having practical wisdom, shrewd.

'A sagacious administrator.' ≈ prudent, astute.

Onerous

Involving heavy obligation or burden.

'Onerous compliance requirements.' ≈
burdensome. ≠ easy.

Predatory

Seeking to exploit, aggressively pursuing gain.

'Predatory lending practices.' ≈ exploitative.

Pecuniary

Relating to money.

'Pecuniary benefit.' ≈ monetary, financial.

Remuneration

Payment for work or services.

≈ salary, emolument. Tested: 'adequate
remuneration' = Art 43 DPSP.

Ephemeral

Lasting for a very short time.

'Ephemeral gains.' ≈ transient, fleeting. ≠
permanent.

Error Spotting — Common APFC Traps
Wrong Sentence

Corrected Version

Rule

He is one of those who works hard.

He is one of those who work hard.

'who' refers to 'those' (plural) →
plural verb.

Neither of them are ready.

Neither of them is ready.

'neither' = singular pronoun →
singular verb.

He prefers tea than coffee.

He prefers tea to coffee.

prefer X to Y (not 'than').

She is more wiser than him.

She is wiser than him.

Double comparative error —
'more' + '-er' is wrong.

Each of the employees were paid.

Each of the employees was paid.

'each' = singular → singular verb.

He could not help to laugh.

He could not help laughing.

'could not help' → gerund (-ing)
form.

Being tired, the meeting was
cancelled.

Being tired, he cancelled the
meeting.

Dangling modifier: 'Being tired'
must refer to 'he'.

It is I whom am responsible.

It is I who am responsible.

Subject form after 'who'; 'whom' is
object.

✔ KEY FACT
RC strategy: (1) Skim the passage once (20 sec). (2) Read the questions. (3) Scan back for exact-phrase
matches. (4) For inference questions, pick the option that is closest to what is stated — not what could be true.
Never bring outside knowledge.

QUICK RECALL
Prefer X to Y: NOT 'prefer X than Y'
Different FROM: British English also allows 'to'; APFC = 'from'
Neither…nor verb: Agrees with the NEARER subject
Parallelism: Same grammatical form in a series
Dangling modifier: Phrase must modify grammatical subject
'each' takes singular: Each of the workers WAS paid
'could not help': Followed by gerund: help laughing NOT help to laugh
RC rule of thumb: 1 minute / question; scan, don't read twice
Egregious: Outstandingly bad → 'egregious violation'
Erstwhile: Former/previous → 'erstwhile minister'
Corporeity: Having a physical body → rare APFC trap word
Impugned: Disputed/called into question in court
Onerous: Burdensome → 'onerous compliance'
Pecuniary: Relating to money → 'pecuniary benefit'

Chapter 58 — Computer Applications & Digital India
APFC papers test two layers: (i) basic computer literacy (files, networks, security, MS Office), and (ii)
Digital India platforms relevant to EPFO — UMANG, DigiLocker, UAN, ECR 2.0, Aadhaar-based claims.
Source: MeitY, EPFO circulars, digitalindia.gov.in.

Computer Fundamentals — One-Liners
Term

Meaning

CPU

Central Processing Unit — brain of the computer (ALU + CU + registers).

RAM vs ROM

RAM: volatile working memory. ROM: non-volatile boot memory.

HDD vs SSD

HDD: magnetic disk (slower, cheaper). SSD: flash memory (faster).

OS

Operating System — manages hardware / applications (Windows, Linux, macOS).

LAN / WAN

LAN: Local (office). WAN: Wide-area (internet).

IP address

Unique numeric label for a device on a network (IPv4 32-bit / IPv6 128-bit).

Cloud computing

On-demand delivery of compute / storage over internet (IaaS / PaaS / SaaS).

Database

Organised collection of data. RDBMS = tables + SQL (Oracle, MySQL).

Malware

Umbrella term: virus, worm, trojan, ransomware, spyware.

Firewall

Network-security filter between trusted internal and untrusted external networks.

Phishing

Fraudulent attempt to obtain sensitive information via fake emails/sites.

Two-factor authentication

Password + OTP / biometric — core for EPFO portal access.

App

Typical Use

reconciliation; pivot for defaulter lists.

Presentations.

For CBT / Regional Committee briefings.

Email + calendar.

save; Alt+Tab switch; Ctrl+F find.

Save 30 sec / task.

Computer Terms — APFC Trap List
Term

Full Form / Meaning

Exam Trap

SPOOL

Simultaneous Peripheral
Operations On-Line

Not a storage device — it's the process of sending data to a queue
(e.g., print spooling). Trap: 'what does SPOOL stand for?' →
Simultaneous Peripheral Operations On-Line.

HTML comment

''

HTML comments use not // or /* */. Cannot be nested. Visible in
page source but not in browser.

SQL

Structured Query Language

DDL (CREATE, DROP, ALTER) vs DML (SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE,
DELETE). DML = Data Manipulation; DDL = Data Definition.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 247 · Verified April 2026

Term

Full Form / Meaning

Exam Trap

LaTeX

Document typesetting system
for complex
formulas/documents

NOT a programming language. Used for academic papers, not
office docs. Commands start with backslash: \begin{document}.

BIOS

Basic Input/Output System

Firmware, NOT software. Resides on motherboard chip. Controls
boot sequence. Replaced by UEFI in modern systems.

Cache memory

Small, fast memory between
CPU and RAM

Faster than RAM, slower than registers. L1 (fastest, smallest) → L2
→ L3 (slowest, largest).

URL

Uniform Resource Locator

Web address format: protocol://domain/path. HTTP = port 80;
HTTPS = port 443.

GUI

Graphical User Interface

Opposite of CLI (Command Line Interface). Windows/Mac = GUI;
MS-DOS = CLI.

ROM types

PROM, EPROM, EEPROM

PROM = write once; EPROM = erasable by UV light; EEPROM =
electrically erasable (Flash memory).

Hyperlink in
email

Created by anchor (<a
href='url'>) tag in HTML email

Anchor tag creates clickable links. Plain text emails cannot have
formatted hyperlinks.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
UMANG vs DigiLocker vs e-Sign is a classic trap: UMANG = unified services app (interact); DigiLocker = cloud
storage for documents (store); e-Sign = Aadhaar-OTP digital signature (sign). All three are distinct projects
under Digital India — don't mix them.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 248 · Verified April 2026

PART XV

Indian Geography
Mental Model First · India as a North-to-South Stairway

Chapter 58 — How to Think About Indian Geography
Indian Geography is intimidating because it feels endless — rivers, mountains, soils, crops,
minerals, census numbers… But there is a hidden order. Picture India as a stairway going from North
to South. Each step has a name, a role, and a story. Learn the stairway, and every fact slots into a step.

58A.1 India as a 6-Step Stairway (North → South)
Step

Region

Born from

What it gives India

Step 1 —
Roof

Himalayas (Himadri ·
Himachal · Shivalik)

Indian plate crashing into Eurasian
(~50 mn yrs ago) — still rising

Source of perennial rivers (Indus, Ganga,
Brahmaputra) · Natural border ·
Monsoon barrier

Step 2 —

→ Bengal)

over millions of years

Most fertile zone → 'food bowl' of India ·
Alluvial soil · Wheat, rice, sugarcane

Step 3 —

(Deccan + Central
Highlands)

Oldest landmass; once part of
Gondwana supercontinent

Mineral wealth (coal, iron, bauxite) ·

(except Narmada, Tapi)

Gujarat)

Saline/sandy soil · Wind energy · Sparse

58A.2 The 'Why' Behind Major Rivers (so they aren't just names)
River system

Where born

Where it goes

Why it matters

Himalayan rivers
(Indus, Ganga,
Brahmaputra)

Glaciers in
Himalayas

Indus → Arabian Sea; Ganga +
Brahmaputra → Bay of Bengal (via
Sundarbans)

PERENNIAL (year-round flow) —
because glaciers melt + monsoon
both feed them. This is why North
Indian agriculture is
irrigation-dependent year-round.

Peninsular
East-flowing
(Mahanadi, Godavari,
Krishna, Kaveri)

Western Ghats
(high)

Bay of Bengal — form large
DELTAS (rich rice country)

SEASONAL (rain-fed) — flow
heavily in monsoon, weak in dry
months. Deltas = fertile but
flood-prone.

Peninsular
West-flowing
(Narmada, Tapi)

Central India

Arabian Sea — form ESTUARIES
(not deltas)

Trap: Narmada & Tapi flow WEST
through rift valleys (cracks in the
plate). Estuary not delta because
rift valleys don't allow silt to
spread.

✔ KEY FACT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 249 · Verified April 2026

The 'East vs West' River Rule (mnemonic):
Peninsular rivers go EAST except two westerners: NAR-TAP (Narmada + Tapi).
Why? They flow through RIFT VALLEYS (geological cracks tilted west).
The 'Delta vs Estuary' Rule:
Eastern rivers → DELTAS (slow flat land, silt spreads).
Western rivers (Narmada, Tapi) → ESTUARIES (fast through rift valley, no spreading).
The Perennial vs Seasonal Rule:
Himalayan rivers → PERENNIAL (glacier + rain).
Peninsular rivers → SEASONAL (rain only).

58A.3 The Monsoon — Why It Matters and How It Works
Monsoon

When

Direction

Rains over

Result

South-West
(main)

June September

From Arabian Sea + Bay of
Bengal → toward land

~75% of India (most
of country)

Kharif crops (rice, jowar,
bajra, cotton). Floods.
Power generation.

North-East
(return)

October December

From land → toward sea
(reverses)

Tamil Nadu coast +
South Andhra (rain
shadow of SW)

Tamil Nadu's main rain
season. Cyclones in Bay
of Bengal.

✔ KEY FACT
Why Tamil Nadu gets rain when no one else does: The SW monsoon (June-Sep) is blocked by the Western
Ghats — eastern slopes are in the rain shadow. So TN waits. In October-December, the wind REVERSES (NE
monsoon) — now TN's coast catches the bay-fetched rain.
Highest rainfall in India: Mawsynram (Meghalaya) — ~11,872 mm/yr. Why? Khasi Hills funnel the
moisture-laden Bay of Bengal winds upward — they cool and dump water. The same funnel hits nearby
Cherrapunji.
Driest: Western Rajasthan (Thar) — under Aravalli rain shadow, dry westerlies, hot summers.

58A.4 Why APFC Cares (the connection)
EPFO operates 135+ Regional Offices across all 28 states + 8 UTs. Geography questions test whether you
can: (a) place a state on the map (rivers, capital, neighbouring states), (b) match a city to its river or
coast (where industry concentrates → where EPFO offices are), (c) recall mineral/crop dominance (which
states have heavy industry → which RO has the highest establishment count).

58A.5 Self-Check Before Moving On
✔ KEY FACT
Cover the right. Answer aloud. All 5 clean before moving on.
1. Name the 6 stairway steps from N to S → Himalayas → Plains → Plateau → Thar → Coasts → Islands
2. Which 2 peninsular rivers flow WEST? → Narmada and Tapi (NAR-TAP) — through rift valleys
3. Why are Himalayan rivers perennial? → Glacier melt + monsoon both feed them
4. When does Tamil Nadu's main rain come? → NE monsoon, Oct-Dec (rain shadow during SW)
5. Highest rainfall place? → Mawsynram, Meghalaya (Khasi Hills funnel)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 250 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 59 — Physical Geography — Reference Detail
This chapter is for REFERENCE. Use it after you have internalised the 6-step stairway above. Don't try
to memorise every cell — skim, spot-check, and come back during revision.
✔ KEY FACT
STUDY FILTER — Physical Geography | Tier 2 (2-3 marks)
MEMORIZE: 6 physiographic divisions (Himalayas / Plains / Plateau / Thar / Coasts / Islands) · 3 Himalayan
ranges N→S: Himadri (Greater) · Himachal (Lesser) · Shivalik (Outer) · Highest Indian peak = Kanchenjunga
(8,586m) · NAR-TAP rule: Narmada + Tapi = ONLY peninsular rivers flowing WEST (rift valleys) · Himalayan
rivers perennial (glacier+rain); Peninsular SEASONAL · Mawsynram = highest rain (~11,872 mm) · Tropic of
Cancer (23.5°N) through 8 states · Standard Meridian 82°30'E (Mirzapur, UP)
SKIP: Memorising every tributary of every river · Specific elevation of every peak beyond Kanchenjunga ·
Detailed glacier list · Specific monsoon date arrival in each city · All 100+ rivers · Coastal islands list (Andaman
has 572 islands — just know it's the largest island group)
TRAP: Mt. Everest is in NEPAL (not India). India's highest = Kanchenjunga. Narmada + Tapi flow WEST (not
East). Mawsynram (NOT Cherrapunji) is highest rain — both in Meghalaya, very close.

India spans ~3.287 million sq km (7th largest country). Mainland extends from 8°4' N to 37°6' N and
68°7' E to 97°25' E. The Tropic of Cancer (23°30' N) divides the country roughly into a tropical south
and sub-tropical north. Standard meridian: 82°30' E (Mirzapur, UP) — IST is 5½ hrs ahead of GMT.

Six Physiographic Divisions
Division

Key features

Exam-flash

1. Himalayan Mountains

Three parallel ranges — Himadri (Great Himalayas, incl. Mt.
Everest in Nepal, Kanchenjunga 8,586 m = highest peak in
India), Himachal (Lesser Himalayas, Pir Panjal, Dhauladhar, hill
stations) and Shivaliks (Outer, youngest, foothills).

Himadri · Himachal ·
Shivalik — N→S

2. Northern Plains

Alluvial plain built by Indus, Ganga, Brahmaputra. Divided into
Bhabar → Terai → Bhangar → Khadar. Most fertile + most
densely populated.

Bhabar-Terai-Bhangar-Kh
adar

3. Peninsular Plateau

Oldest landmass — triangular, made of Central Highlands (N of
Narmada) and Deccan Plateau (S of Narmada). Bounded by
Vindhya-Satpura-Western-Eastern Ghats.

Oldest part of Indian
landmass

4. Indian Desert

Thar Desert (Rajasthan, parts of Gujarat). Rainfall <150
mm/year. Ephemeral river Luni is the only significant drainage.

Rainfall <150 mm/yr

5. Coastal Plains

Western Coast — narrow, from Gujarat to Kerala; Konkan (N),
Kannada, Malabar (S). Eastern Coast — broad, from Bengal to
TN; Northern Circars + Coromandel.

East wider · West
narrower

6. Islands

Andaman & Nicobar — Bay of Bengal; volcanic origin (Barren
Island = only active volcano). Lakshadweep — Arabian Sea;
coral origin.

A&N volcanic ·
Lakshadweep coral

✔ KEY FACT
Highest peak in India = Kanchenjunga (8,586 m). Mt. Everest (8,848 m) and K2 / Godwin Austen (8,611 m)
lie outside Indian territory (Nepal; POK respectively). Highest peak in Peninsular India = Anamudi (2,695
m) in Kerala's Western Ghats.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Only TWO major west-flowing peninsular rivers: Narmada & Tapi. Both flow through rift valleys, cutting
across the general east-ward drainage of the Deccan. Common MCQ trap: 'Which major peninsular river is NOT
east-flowing?' → Narmada OR Tapi.

Climate & Monsoon
Season

Months

Key features

Cold weather (Winter)

Dec - Feb

Low temp in north; western disturbances bring rain/snow to N-W India.

Hot weather (Summer)

Mar - May

Loo in N plains; local storms — Kaal Baisakhi (Bengal), Mango showers
(Kerala/Karnataka).

Advancing SW
Monsoon

Jun - Sep

Main rainfall season (~75% of annual). Two branches — Arabian Sea
+ Bay of Bengal. Onset ~1 Jun at Kerala.

Retreating / NE
Monsoon

Oct - Dec

Winds reverse; brings rain chiefly to Tamil Nadu & coastal Andhra.
Cyclones common in Bay of Bengal.

✔ KEY FACT
Highest rainfall in India: Mawsynram (Meghalaya) — ~11,872 mm/yr. Cherrapunji (Sohra) is a close second at
~11,777 mm. Lowest: Leh / Western Rajasthan (<100 mm). 'October Heat' = high humidity before retreat
of SW monsoon.

PART XVI

Chapter 61 — Insurance Law Fundamentals — 7 Principles
2025 PYQ Qs 106-107 tested Contract of Indemnity vs Assurance and the doctrine of Subrogation.
Insurance law is governed by the Insurance Act 1938 (amended 2015) and regulated by IRDAI
(Hyderabad). Understand these seven principles and you cover ~95% of the insurance questions. Sources:
Insurance Act 1938; IRDAI website.

The Seven Fundamental Principles
Principle

Meaning

Exam flash

1. Utmost Good Faith
(uberrima fides)

Both parties must disclose ALL material facts affecting
risk before contract formation.

Non-disclosure voids policy;
applies to all insurance.

2. Insurable Interest

The insured must stand to suffer financial loss if the
insured event occurs.

Required at time of contract (life)
/ loss (non-life).

3. Indemnity

Insurance restores the insured to the financial position
immediately before the loss — no profit.

Applies to non-life only. Life =
ASSURANCE (pays fixed sum).

4. Subrogation

After indemnifying, insurer steps into the insured's
shoes and can recover from the third party responsible.

Non-life only; flows from
indemnity.

5. Contribution

If same risk is insured with multiple insurers, each
contributes pro rata; insured cannot claim full amount
from each.

Prevents enrichment; non-life
only.

6. Mitigation /
Loss-minimisation

Insured must act as a prudent uninsured person would,
to minimise the loss.

Duty to preserve property
post-incident.

7. Causa Proxima

The immediate / proximate cause of loss determines
coverage — not the remote cause.

Chain-of-causation test.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Life insurance is a contract of ASSURANCE, NOT indemnity. Because life has no financial measure, the
insurer pays the fixed sum assured on death (or maturity). Hence subrogation and contribution principles do
NOT apply to life. Contracts of indemnity: fire, marine, motor, health, property, engineering, crop.

Types of Insurance — APFC-relevant grid
Type

Governing law / regulator

Key point

Life (term,
endowment,
ULIP)

Insurance Act 1938 · IRDAI

Assurance; indemnity principles N/A

Fire

Insurance Act 1938 · IRDAI

Strict indemnity; principle of contribution applies

Marine

Marine Insurance Act 1963 · IRDAI

Hull, cargo, freight; oldest insurance branch

Motor (vehicle)

Motor Vehicles Act 1988 · IRDAI

Third-party cover COMPULSORY u/s 146

Health

Insurance Act 1938 · IRDAI

Sum insured × indemnity (mediclaim); distinct from ESI

Crop

Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana
(PMFBY) 2016

Farmer premium 2% kharif / 1.5% rabi / 5% horticulture

Social security

EPF Act 1952 (EDLI) · SS Code 2020

EDLI = up to ₹7 lakh (permanent Oct 2024)

✔ KEY FACT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 259 · Verified April 2026

IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India) — statutory body set up under the IRDA Act
1999, HQ Hyderabad. Chairman + max 9 members. Regulates all insurance (life + general + reinsurance)
except ESI (ESIC) and EDLI (EPFO).

MNEMONIC
U · I · I · S · C · M · P → Utmost good faith · Insurable interest · Indemnity · Subrogation ·
Contribution · Mitigation · Proxima causa
QUICK RECALL
Regulator: IRDAI (Hyderabad) — IRDA Act 1999
Life insurance nature: ASSURANCE, not indemnity
Compulsory cover: Third-party motor (MV Act 1988 S.146)
Subrogation applies: Non-life only; after indemnification
Utmost good faith: Both parties; pre-contract disclosure
Crop insurance: PMFBY 2016 — farmer pays 2% / 1.5% / 5%
Causa proxima: Proximate cause, not remote, determines cover

Mutadars (PYQ Q21 2025) were local intermediary land-revenue collectors in the Bhupathi-family

fuelled both Rampa Rebellions.

Paired with 'White Pagoda' — Jagannath Puri. Nagara style; chariot of Surya with 24 wheels + 7 horses.

⚠ EXAM ALERT

Chapter 64 — Environmental Laws — EP Act · Air · Water · NGT
Environmental law questions appear in the APFC paper under 'Social Security & Labour Welfare' and
'General Awareness'. The APFC often links environment enforcement to worker health/safety — EPFO
inspectors verify compliance with health-related statutes for coverage. Four acts dominate: the Water
Act 1974, Air Act 1981, Environment Protection Act 1986, and NGT Act 2010. Learn them in
chronological order.

Aspect

Detail
nd

Enacted under

Entry 17A of Concurrent List (after 42

Amendment, 1976)

Institutional bodies

CPCB (Central Pollution Control Board) — New Delhi; SPCB in each state

Consent mechanism

Industries discharging effluent must obtain CONSENT from SPCB — Consent to Establish
(CTE) + Consent to Operate (CTO)

Standard set by

CPCB sets national standards; SPCBs may set stricter norms

Key offence

Discharging pollutant beyond permissible limits — imprisonment 1.5 to 6 years + fine

Cess

Water (Cess) Act 1977 — levied on industries & local bodies using water; funds PCBs

Air Act 1981 — key points
Aspect

Detail

Scope

Extends to whole of India (unlike Water Act which was initially selective)

Same boards

Uses the same CPCB + SPCB structure created under Water Act 1974

Air Quality Zones

State Govt declares Air Pollution Control Areas; industries in those areas need SPCB
consent

Vehicle emissions

Empowers Govt to prescribe standards for emissions from vehicles

1987 Amendment

Included noise pollution within definition of 'air pollutant'

Key offence

Operating without SPCB consent — imprisonment 1.5 to 6 years + fine

Environment (Protection) Act 1986 — umbrella law
Aspect

Detail

Trigger

Bhopal Gas Tragedy — December 2-3, 1984; MIC (Methyl Isocyanate) leaked from
Union Carbide plant; ~3,500-5,000 deaths immediately; largest industrial disaster

Enacted under

Art 253 (power to implement international treaties) + Art 51(c) (directive principle)

Nature

Umbrella legislation — does NOT replace Water Act or Air Act; fills gaps and gives
Central Govt overriding powers

Powers of Central Govt

Issue directions to any person/officer/authority; prescribe environmental standards;
restrict/prohibit industrial activities; inspect premises & equipment

Key rules under EP Act

Hazardous Waste Rules 2016; Biomedical Waste Rules 2016; E-Waste Rules 2022; EIA
(Environmental Impact Assessment) Notification

EIA

Mandatory prior environmental clearance from MoEFCC for major
industrial/infrastructure projects

Offences

Contravention → imprisonment up to 5 years + ₹1 lakh/day fine; continuing offence:
additional ₹5,000/day

Administered by

Ministry of Environment, Forest & Climate Change (MoEFCC)

National Green Tribunal (NGT) Act 2010
Aspect

Detail

Nature

Statutory body — created by an Act of Parliament (NOT constitutional, NOT under EP
Act 1986)

Established

2010 under the NGT Act 2010; began functioning 2011

Composition

Chairperson (retired Supreme Court judge) + Judicial Members + Expert Members
(scientists/technocrats); 5-year term

Jurisdiction

Civil cases relating to environment & forest laws — Water Act, Air Act, EP Act, Forest
(Conservation) Act, Biological Diversity Act etc.

NOT under NGT

Wildlife Protection Act 1972, IPC, Land Acquisition Act — these are outside NGT's
jurisdiction

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 265 · Verified April 2026

Aspect

Detail

Appeal

NGT orders appealable only to Supreme Court of India

HQ + Benches

Principal Bench: New Delhi; Zonal Benches: Kolkata · Chennai · Pune · Bhopal

Time limits

Application must be filed within 6 months of cause of action (Court may condone delay
for good cause)

Precautionary & PPP

Applies precautionary principle and polluter-pays principle

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Three common MCQ traps: (1) NGT is NOT established under EP Act 1986 — it has its own NGT Act 2010. (2)
NGT is NOT a constitutional body — it is a statutory tribunal. (3) Water Act was enacted in 1974 BEFORE the Air
Act (1981) and EP Act (1986) — chronological order: Water → Air → EP → NGT.

✔ KEY FACT
EPFO connection: Workers in polluting industries (chemical, pharmaceutical, mining, textile) are covered by
EPF if the factory has 20+ workers. EPFO enforcement officers verify factory records — they may encounter PCB
consent requirements and EIA clearances as part of compliance verification. Factories violating EP Act can be
shut down, affecting EPF compliance.

MNEMONIC
W-A-E-N → Water 1974 · Air 1981 · EP Act 1986 (post-Bhopal) · NGT 2010 | NGT = Statutory,
NOT constitutional
QUICK RECALL
Water Act year: 1974 (PCBs created)
Air Act year: 1981 (same PCBs used)
EP Act year: 1986 (triggered by Bhopal 1984)
NGT Act year: 2010 (statutory, NOT under EP Act)
Bhopal tragedy: Dec 2-3, 1984; MIC gas; Union Carbide
NGT Appeal: Supreme Court only
EIA: Prior clearance from MoEFCC for large projects
CPCB location: New Delhi; state boards = SPCBs
Noise pollution: Added to Air Act by 1987 amendment

PART XVII

Aptitude Toolkit — Quant · Reasoning · CA
APFC-scoped: only what the PYQ pattern actually tests

Chapter 64 — Quantitative Aptitude Essentials
2025 APFC paper had ~10 quant questions (Qs 66-75). The topics are narrow: percentages, ratios,
time-work, time-speed-distance, profit-loss-discount, averages, probability, and basic logarithms. Each
formula below maps to at least one observed PYQ.

64.1 Percentages · Profit · Discount
Percent / Profit-Loss formulas
% of x = (p / 100) × x
% change = ((new – old) / old) × 100
Profit % = (SP – CP) / CP × 100 | Loss % when SP < CP
Marked-up % = (MP – CP) / CP × 100
Discount % = (MP – SP) / MP × 100
Successive discounts: d12 = d1 + d2 – (d1·d2/100)

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q75 2025): Merchant offers 15% discount then further 10% on the reduced price. Net
discount = 15 + 10 – (15·10/100) = 23.5% — not 25%. This is more favourable than a flat 5% for the
merchant (Reason is correct, Assertion is correct, Reason IS explanation → option (a)).

64.2 Simple & Compound Interest
Interest formulas
SI = P · R · T / 100
Amount at SI = P + SI = P · (1 + RT/100)
CI (annual) = P · (1 + R/100)T – P
Amount at CI = P · (1 + R/100)T
CI – SI (2 years) = P · (R/100)2
CI – SI (3 years) = P · (R/100)2 · (3 + R/100)

64.3 Ratio · Proportion · Partnership
Ratio / partnership
Ratio a : b ↔ a/b ; proportion a : b :: c : d ⇒ ad = bc
Partnership profit share ∝ Capital × Time
Mixture (alligation): cheaper/dearer ratio = (dearer-mean) / (mean-cheaper)

✔ KEY FACT

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 267 · Verified April 2026

Worked example (PYQ Q70 2025): A, B, C invest in ratio 2:3:5, but earn the same amount of profit. If profit is
proportional to investment × time, then time ratio = 15 : 10 : 6 (take LCM of 2,3,5 = 30, time = 30 ÷
investment). Option (c).

64.4 Time, Work & Speed, Distance
Time/Work & Speed/Distance
Work done / day = 1 / (days to finish)
Two persons together = 1/a + 1/b ; total days = ab / (a+b)
Speed = Distance / Time (1 km/hr = 5/18 m/s)
Average speed (equal distances) = 2ab / (a+b)
Relative speed: same direction |a-b|, opposite a+b
Two trains meeting (start A at t1, B at t2): meeting point ratio = time taken from meeting onwards — √(t1/t2)

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q73 2025): Train A starts at 9 AM from station A, reaches B at 2 PM. Train B starts at
11 AM from station B, reaches A at 3 PM. Ratio of distance from station A (to meeting point) : from station B =
Using Σ time ratio — straightforward intersection — answer 3:2 (option d). Method: if both run on same line
between A and B, ratio of remaining distances after meeting inverts the time-ratio.

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q71 2025): 5 persons paint 5 walls in 5 hours. How many hours will 7 persons take to
paint 7 walls? Work per person-hour = (5 walls) / (5×5 person-hr) = 1/5 wall. Total effort for 7 walls = 35
person-hr → 35/7 = 5 hours (option d).

64.5 Averages · Mean, Median & Mode
Averages
Arithmetic mean = (Σx) / n
Median (sorted): middle if n odd; mean of middle two if n even
Mode = most frequent value
Weighted mean = Σ(w·x) / Σw

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q79 2025): Scores of 16 students are 19, 4, 17, 7, 15, 2, 18, 11, 15, 17, 19, 4. Correct
the list, sort it, find median. Mean = (Σscores)/16; for 16 items median = average of 8th and 9th values.
Difference | mean - median | ≈ 0·5 (option c).

64.6 Probability & Counting
Probability / Counting
Probability P(E) = favourable / total outcomes
P(A ∪ B) = P(A) + P(B) – P(A ∩ B)
P(A ∩ B) independent = P(A) · P(B)
Two dice top faces add to 7 or 10 = P(7) + P(10) = 6/36 + 3/36 = 9/36 = 1/4
n-permutation nPr = n! / (n-r)! ; n-combination nCr = n! / [r!(n-r)!]

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 268 · Verified April 2026

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q80 2025): Two unbiased dice tossed; probability that top faces are marked 7 OR 10?
7 ⇒ (1,6),(2,5),(3,4),(4,3),(5,2),(6,1) = 6 outcomes. 10 ⇒ (4,6),(5,5),(6,4) = 3. Total favourable = 9 out of 36. P =
9/36 = 0.25 (option c).

64.7 Logarithms — the one rule that matters
Logarithm essentials
Number of digits in N = ⌊log10 N⌋ + 1
log 6 = log(2·3) = log 2 + log 3 ≈ 0.3010 + 0.4771 = 0.7781
log (an) = n · log a

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q69 2025): How many digits in 625? log 625 = 25 · log 6 = 25 · 0.7781 = 19.45. Digits
= ⌊19.45⌋ + 1 = 20 (option a).

MNEMONIC
Perc · SI/CI · Ratio · Time-Work · Speed · Avg · Prob · Log — 8 formula blocks, 10 questions
QUICK RECALL
1 km/hr → m/s: × 5/18
Successive discounts: d₁ + d₂ – d₁d₂/100
CI-SI 2-yr: P(R/100)²
Together work rate: ab/(a+b) days
Avg speed equal-dist: 2ab/(a+b)
Digits in N: ⌊log₁₀ N⌋ + 1
P(die sum = 7): 6/36 = 1/6

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 269 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 65 — Reasoning & Mental Ability
2025 PYQ had ~5 reasoning items (Qs 72, 75, 77, 78, Assertion-Reason). Only a narrow set of formats
appears — calendar, alpha-numeric series, 3-digit counting, assertion-reason. This chapter drills each.

65.1 Calendar — day-of-week problems
Calendar basics
Odd days in an ordinary year (365 days) = 1
Odd days in a leap year (366 days) = 2
Leap year: divisible by 4 (or 400 for centuries). 2024 is leap; 2100 is NOT.
Count odd days from start of year to target date → add to known day

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q72 2025): 2nd Feb 2025 was Sunday. What day was 1st Feb 2024? From 1 Feb 2024
to 2 Feb 2025 = 367 days (2024 was leap, so Jan-Feb 2024 + full Feb 2025 includes the Feb 29, 2024). Odd days
= 367 mod 7 = 3. Going backward 3 days from Sunday = Thursday (option c).

65.2 Alpha-numeric series
How to attack alpha-numeric series
Step 1: identify the pattern in numbers (AP, GP, primes, squares).
Step 2: identify the pattern in letters (skip-k alphabet, ASCII gap).
Step 3: match the TWO patterns together to fill missing term.

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q77 2025): Series C – 24, H – 19, M – ?, W – 9. Letters: C, H, M, ?, W — differences 5,
5, 5, 5 → next letter after M (+5) = R. Numbers: 24, 19, ?, 9 — differences -5, -5, -5 → missing = 14. Answer: R –
14 (option c).

65.3 Number-counting problems
Counting
3-digit numbers: hundreds digit 1-9, tens 0-9, units 0-9 → 9 × 10 × 10 = 900 total.
Middle digit smaller than first AND last: Σ over cases of (h, u) where tens < min(h, u)

✔ KEY FACT
Worked example (PYQ Q78 2025): How many 3-digit numbers have middle digit smaller than BOTH first and
last? For each pair (h, u) with h ≥1, u ≥0, let m = min(h, u). Number of valid tens = m. Sum: Σh=1..9 Σu=0..9
min(h,u) = 285 – 40 = 45 (option b). (Shortcut: if first = last, count = first; total works out to 45.)

65.4 Assertion-Reason format
Option

Assertion

Reason

Explanation?

(a)

True

True

YES — Reason is correct explanation of Assertion

(b)

True

True

NO — Reason is correct but NOT the explanation

(c)

True

False

—

(d)

False

True

—

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 270 · Verified April 2026

65.5 Syllogism — 3-rule test
Syllogism rules
Rule 1: exactly three terms (major, minor, middle); middle is NOT in the conclusion.
Rule 2: middle term must be distributed at least once.
Rule 3: from two negative premises, no conclusion is drawn.
A = universal affirmative (All); E = universal negative (No); I = particular affirmative (Some); O = particular
negative (Some are not).

65.6 Blood relations & directions — compact
Blood relations / Directions
Blood relations: build a family tree L-to-R; track gender each step; use +/- generations.
Directions: draw a cross (N-S / E-W); rotate by 90° for left / right; apply Pythagoras for displacement.

MNEMONIC
Odd days · AP/GP letters · Count min(h,u) · AEIO syllogism
QUICK RECALL
Odd days ordinary yr: 1
Odd days leap yr: 2
2024 was: Leap year
Syllogism Rule 2: Middle term distributed at least once
Negative premises: No conclusion from two
AR option (a): A+R both true AND R explains A
AR option (b): A+R both true BUT R does NOT explain A

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 271 · Verified April 2026

PART XVIII

Bare Act Essentials & Procedural Flows
30 highest-yield sections verbatim-style + 5 end-to-end procedural flows

Chapter 67 — The 30 Critical Sections (paraphrased close to
statute)
Reading entire bare Acts is neither practical nor necessary. What IS necessary is knowing the operative
phrasing of ~30 sections that examiners quote or paraphrase. This chapter gives each such section in
compressed form, with the exam-tested phrase in bold. Cross-check each against the bare Act on
indiacode.nic.in during your final revision.

A. EPF & Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952
§

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

1(3)(a)

This Act applies to every establishment which is a factory engaged in any industry specified in Schedule I
and in which twenty or more persons are employed.

1(3)(b)

Applies to any other establishment employing 20+ persons or class thereof which the Central Govt may by
notification specify.

1(5)

An establishment to which this Act applies shall continue to be governed by it notwithstanding that the
number of persons employed therein at any time falls below twenty.

2(b)

'Basic wages' means all emoluments earned by an employee while on duty or on leave or on holidays with
wages… but does not include: (i) the cash value of food concessions; (ii) any DA, HRA, overtime
allowance, bonus, commission or other similar allowance; (iii) presents made by the employer.

2(f)

'Employee' means any person employed for wages in any kind of work, manual or otherwise, in or in
connection with the work of an establishment, and includes contract labour engaged on the rolls and
apprentices other than those under the Apprentices Act, 1961 or Standing Orders.

6

The contribution paid by the employer shall be twelve per cent of the basic wages, DA and retaining
allowance, if any, payable to each employee. The employee shall make a matching contribution. Proviso:
rate is ten per cent in case of notified establishments (sick units, <20, jute/beedi/brick/coir/guar gum).

7A(1)

Central PF Commissioner may, by order, determine the amount due from any employer… after conducting
such inquiry as he deems necessary.

7A(2)

The officer conducting the inquiry under S.7A shall have the same powers as are vested in a court
under CPC 1908 for (a) enforcing attendance, (b) requiring discovery and production of documents, (c)
receiving evidence on affidavit, (d) issuing commissions for examination of witnesses.

7Q

The employer shall be liable to pay simple interest at the rate of twelve per cent per annum or at
such higher rate as may be specified, on any amount due from him, from the date on which the amount has
become so due till the date of its actual payment.

8B

Where any amount is in arrears under this Act, the authorised officer may issue… a certificate under this
section specifying the amount of arrears.

14(1)

Whoever knowingly makes any false statement or false representation … shall be punishable with
imprisonment which may extend to one year, or with fine of five thousand rupees, or with both.

14(1A)(a)

An employer who contravenes S.6 (contribution) — where default is in payment of employee's
contribution deducted from wages — shall be punishable with imprisonment of not less than one year
but may extend to three years and fine of ten thousand rupees.

14(1A)(b)

In any other case of default in S.6, imprisonment of not less than six months but may extend to three
years and fine of ₹5,000.

14(1B)

Default in payment of EPS/EDLI contributions or inspection charges — imprisonment of not less than six
months, which may extend to one year and fine up to ₹5,000.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 278 · Verified April 2026

§

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

14A(1)

Where a person committing an offence under this Act is a company, every person who at the time of the
offence was in charge of, and responsible to, the company for the conduct of the business, shall be
deemed to be guilty.

14AA

Repeat offence: imprisonment of not less than two years but may extend to five years and a fine of
not less than twenty-five thousand rupees.

14AB

Notwithstanding anything in CrPC 1973, an offence relating to default in payment of contribution by
the employer punishable under this Act shall be cognizable.

14B

Where an employer makes default in payment of any contribution, the Commissioner may recover such
damages not exceeding the amount of arrears as may be specified in the Scheme. Para 32A (amended
14-Jun-2024): damages shall be 1% per month of arrears.

17

The appropriate Govt may, by notification, exempt any establishment… from the operation of all or any
of the provisions of the Scheme, if it is of the opinion that its rules are not less favourable… subject to
conditions in Schedule III.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 279 · Verified April 2026

B. Employees' Pension Scheme 1995 (key paragraphs of the Scheme)
Para

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

3(1)

The Scheme shall apply to every employee of the establishments covered, whose pay at the
time of becoming a member does not exceed fifteen thousand rupees per month.

6

The employer shall, in respect of an employee covered by this Scheme, remit 8.33% of his pay
to the Pension Fund.

7

The Central Government shall contribute 1.16% of the pay of each employee whose pay does
not exceed ₹15,000 per month, to the Pension Fund.

10(2)

In the case of a member having rendered eligible service of twenty years or more, his
pensionable service shall be increased by adding a period of two years.

11(1)

The pensionable salary shall be the average monthly pay drawn in any manner during the
sixty months immediately preceding the date of exit from membership, subject to a ceiling of
₹15,000 (unless higher-pension option exercised).

12(2)

Monthly member's pension = (Pensionable Salary × Pensionable Service) ÷ 70.

12(5)

A member becomes eligible for monthly pension on attaining age of 58 years, provided he has
rendered eligible service of 10 years or more.

12(7)

Early pension from age 50: monthly pension shall be reduced at the rate of 4% per year short
of age 58.

16

On death of a member, widow/widower pension = fifty per cent of member's pension, for life;
children pension = 25% of widow pension, max 2 children, till age 25.

17

Permanent total disablement pension: monthly pension as per formula irrespective of length
of eligible service, provided disability occurs while in service.

Table D
(post-14-Jun-2024)

Member with less than 10 years pensionable service who opts out: withdrawal benefit =
pensionable salary × Table-D factor (now expressed in completed months of service, not
years).

C. EDLI Scheme 1976 (key paragraphs)
Para

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

22

The employer shall pay to the Insurance Fund 0.50% of the basic wages + DA + retaining allowance,
subject to the wage ceiling of ₹15,000 per month.

22A

Administrative charges payable by the employer: 0.01% of wages, subject to minimum ₹200 per month
(₹25 for non-functional establishments).

24

On death of a member while in service, the nominee / legal heir shall be paid an amount equal to average
monthly wages of the preceding 12 months × 30, PLUS 50% of average EPF balance (max
₹1,75,000 bonus), subject to a minimum of ₹2.5 lakh and maximum of ₹7 lakh (permanent extension
Oct 2024).

D. Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 — the high-yield sections
§

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

2(k)

'Industrial dispute' means any dispute or difference between employers and employers, or between
employers and workmen, or between workmen and workmen, which is connected with the
employment or non-employment or the terms of employment or with the conditions of labour, of any
person.

2(l)

'Lock-out' means the temporary closing of a place of employment, or the suspension of work, or the refusal
by an employer to continue to employ any number of persons employed by him.

2(q)

'Strike' means a cessation of work by a body of persons employed in any industry acting in
combination, or a concerted refusal, or a refusal under a common understanding, of any number of persons
who are or have been so employed to continue to work or to accept employment.

2(s)

'Workman' means any person employed in any industry to do any manual, unskilled, skilled, technical,
operational, clerical or supervisory work for hire or reward… but does not include any such person who
is employed mainly in a managerial or administrative capacity, or who, being employed in a
supervisory capacity, draws wages exceeding ten thousand rupees per month.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 280 · Verified April 2026

§

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

2A

Where any employer discharges, dismisses, retrenches or otherwise terminates the services of an
individual workman, such dispute or difference shall be deemed to be an industrial dispute
notwithstanding that no other workman nor any union of workmen is a party.

10

Where the appropriate Govt is of opinion that any industrial dispute exists or is apprehended, it may at any
time, by order in writing, refer the dispute to a Board, Court, Labour Court or Tribunal.

12

The Conciliation Officer shall, for the purpose of bringing about a settlement of the dispute, do all
such things as he thinks fit… and if no settlement is arrived at, send a full report to the appropriate
Govt.

22

No person employed in a public utility service shall go on strike in breach of contract — (a) without giving
notice of strike within six weeks before striking; (b) within fourteen days of giving such notice; (c)
before the expiry of the date of strike specified.

25B

'Continuous service' — A workman shall be said to be in continuous service for one year if he has, during a
period of twelve calendar months preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made,
actually worked for not less than two hundred and forty days.

25F

No workman employed in any industry who has been in continuous service for not less than one year shall
be retrenched until (a) the workman has been given one month's notice in writing indicating the reasons
for retrenchment or has been paid wages in lieu of such notice; (b) the workman has been paid, at the time
of retrenchment, compensation equivalent to fifteen days' average pay for every completed year
of continuous service; (c) notice has been served on the appropriate Govt.

25G

Where workmen of a particular category are retrenched, the employer shall ordinarily retrench the
workman who was the last person to be employed in that category (LIFO).

25H

Where any workmen are retrenched and the employer proposes to take into his employ any persons, he
shall… give an opportunity to the retrenched workmen to offer themselves for re-employment.

25K / 25N

Chapter V-B: provisions regarding lay-off, retrenchment and closure apply only to industrial establishments
in which not less than three hundred workmen are employed (post IR Code 2020; earlier 100).

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 281 · Verified April 2026

E. Other high-yield sections (Gratuity · Bonus · Trade Unions · SS Code)
Statute · §

Operative phrase (paraphrased)

Gratuity Act
S.4(1)

Gratuity shall be payable to an employee on termination of his employment after he has rendered
continuous service for not less than five years — except on death / disablement.

Gratuity Act
S.4(2)

For every completed year of service… employer shall pay gratuity at the rate of fifteen days'
wages based on the rate of wages last drawn. Formula: (last wage ÷ 26) × 15 × years.

Gratuity Act
S.4(3)

The amount of gratuity payable shall not exceed twenty lakh rupees (enhanced from ₹10L by
2018 Amendment; CG may notify higher limit).

Gratuity Act
S.4(6)

Gratuity may be wholly or partially forfeited if services terminated for (a) willful omission causing
damage/loss; (b) riotous/disorderly conduct or any other act involving moral turpitude.

Bonus Act
S.2(13)

'Employee' means any person employed on a salary or wage not exceeding twenty-one
thousand rupees per month.

Bonus Act S.8

Every employee shall be entitled to be paid bonus if he has worked in the establishment for not
less than thirty working days in that year.

Bonus Act S.10

Minimum bonus = eight and one-third per cent of the salary/wage earned by the employee
during the accounting year or ₹100 (100 in case of employees over 15 years — 60 in case of minors),
whichever is higher.

Bonus Act S.11

If the allocable surplus exceeds the minimum bonus payable, the employer shall pay bonus
proportionate to the surplus, subject to a maximum of twenty per cent of the salary/wage.

Bonus Act S.12

Where the salary or wage of an employee exceeds seven thousand rupees per month or the
minimum wage, whichever is higher, the bonus shall be calculated as if his salary were ₹7,000
(or min wage).

Trade Unions S.4

Any seven or more members of a trade union may, by subscribing their names to the rules, apply
for registration.

Trade Unions
S.9A

A registered trade union shall have at least 10% of workers or 100 workers, whichever is less, as
its members (compulsory under 2001 amendment).

SS Code S.2(35)

'Gig worker' means a person who performs work or participates in a work arrangement and earns
from such activities outside of traditional employer-employee relationship.

SS Code S.2(61)

'Platform worker' means a person engaged in or undertaking platform work.

SS Code S.114(4)

Aggregators shall contribute to the Social Security Fund at a rate not exceeding two per cent of
the annual turnover… subject to a cap of five per cent of the amount paid or payable to gig /
platform workers.

SS Code S.141

The Central Govt shall constitute a Social Security Fund for social security and welfare of
unorganised workers, gig workers and platform workers.

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Caveat on phrasing: these excerpts are faithfully paraphrased but have been tightened for brevity. For any
high-stakes verbatim question (e.g., 'which of the following is the exact definition of workman under S.2(s)?'),
cross-check the full bare Act at indiacode.nic.in before the exam.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 282 · Verified April 2026

Chapter 68 — End-to-End Procedural Flows (Narrative
Walk-throughs)
Statutes describe powers; real procedures connect them. This chapter narrates five end-to-end flows
that examiners love to test as 'which step comes next?' or 'which section applies here?'.

68.1 Full EPF Enforcement Chain (Default → Recovery → Appeal)
Suppose Employer X has deducted employee's EPF share for six months (Jan-Jun 2025) but not deposited
it. The end-to-end flow looks like this:
1. Default detected
Inspector S.13 visit · ECR mismatch · UAN complaint · self-disclosure

2. Show-Cause Notice u/s 7A
Para 7A(1) — Commissioner issues SCN + summons (civil-court powers: S.7A(2))

3. 7A Hearing
Opportunity to produce records · examine witnesses · affidavit evidence

4. 7A Speaking Order
Determines principal amount due · reasons recorded · party-wise split

5. 7Q Interest stacked automatically
12% p.a. simple, due-date to payment-date — NO separate hearing needed

6. 14B Damages SCN (separate)
Para 32A — damages 1%/month (post-15 Jun 2024) OR slab (pre) — separate hearing under natural justice

7. Recovery Certificate u/s 8B
If unpaid, certificate to recovery officer; modes under S.8E: attachment of movables · sale · arrest · appointment of receiver · attachment of bank

8. Appeal to CGIT (S.7I)
60 days from order · 75% pre-deposit u/s 7O (Tribunal may waive/reduce)

9. Writ to HC / SLP to SC
Only on questions of law / Basic Structure / jurisdictional error

✔ KEY FACT
Parallel track — Criminal: For the same default, prosecution under S.14(1A)(a) can be launched (1-3 yrs +
₹10,000 fine). If employee share was deducted-not-deposited, S.14AB makes it cognizable — police can
register and investigate without magistrate's order.

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 283 · Verified April 2026

68.2 EPS Pension Claim Flow (Superannuation to First Pension Credit)
1. Member attains age 58
EPS contribution stops; entire employer 12% now goes to EPF

2. Employer digitally submits Form 10D
Via UAN member portal; verifies last-60-month wages

3. EPFO RO computes pensionable salary
Avg last 60 months (capped ₹15,000 unless higher-pension opted)

4. Pension formula applied
Monthly pension = (Pensionable Salary × Pensionable Service) ÷ 70

5. PPO (Pension Payment Order) issued
12-digit PPO number; gateway to all pension services

6. CPPS disburses pension pan-India
Bank-account-agnostic since 1 Jan 2025; covers ~70 lakh pensioners

7. Annual Jeevan Pramaan
Digital Life Certificate via Aadhaar/face-auth; by November each year

68.3 EDLI Claim Flow (Death of Member while in Service)
1. Member dies while in EPF-covered employment
Natural or accidental death; 12+ months continuous membership

2. Nominee / legal heir files Form 5(IF)
With death certificate, bank KYC, succession certificate if no nomination

3. Employer verifies service &amp; contribution
Digital attestation via ECR

4. RO computes benefit
30 × avg wage (capped ₹15,000) + 50% of avg EPF balance (max bonus ₹1,75,000)

5. Apply min/max floors
Minimum ₹2,50,000 · Maximum ₹7,00,000 (permanent Oct 2024)

6. Direct bank credit to nominee / LH
Tax-free under S.10(10D) of IT Act; employer NOT in payment loop

68.4 Higher-Pension Flow (Post Sunil Kumar, SC 2022)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 284 · Verified April 2026

1. Eligibility test
Member in service on 1-Sep-2014 AND contributed EPF on actual wage >₹15,000

2. Joint option with employer
Deadline after 2 extensions: 11-Jul-2023 (per SC directions in review)

3. EPFO computes arrears
Principal + 8.33% EPS share difference + interest (up to current date)

4. Arrears debited from EPF balance
Any shortfall paid by member; surplus credited back to EPF

5. Revised pensionable salary fixed
Avg last-60-month ACTUAL wages (instead of capped ₹15,000)

6. Revised pension paid prospectively
Higher monthly pension; smaller EPF lump-sum — status: ~99% processed (Nov 2025)

EPFO APFC Complete Study Notes · Page 285 · Verified April 2026

68.5 Industrial-Dispute Resolution Flow (Classical ID-Act path)
1. Dispute arises
Between employer &amp; workmen (wages, hours, lay-off, retrenchment, dismissal)

2. Conciliation u/s 12
Conciliation Officer attempts settlement within ~14 days (prescribed time can extend)

3. Success → Settlement Memo
Signed by parties; enforceable as court order u/s 18 (binding on signatories)

4. Failure → Failure Report to Govt
Conciliation Officer reports failure with reasons

5. Govt Reference u/s 10
To Labour Court (2nd Schedule) / Industrial Tribunal (2nd or 3rd Schedule) / National Tribunal (disputes of national importance)

6. Adjudication &amp; Award
Tribunal passes Award; Award published u/s 17 in Official Gazette

7. Enforcement u/s 18
Award binds parties named; enforceable as if decree of civil court

8. Judicial review
Writ to HC (Art 226) — only on grounds of jurisdictional error or Art 14 breach

⚠ EXAM ALERT
Note: This classical ID-Act flow is being superseded piecemeal by the Industrial Relations Code 2020.
Substantive provisions of the IR Code are being notified in phases. Till full IR-Code notification, questions likely
to test the ID Act 1947 flow — know it cold.

Ch-68 Drill — Legal Maxims Commonly Tested
APFC being a quasi-judicial authority, his paper can slip in Latin legal maxims. Know these 15 — each
commonly appears in 7A orders, tribunal rulings, or the APFC RT.
Maxim

Meaning

Application

Audi alteram partem

Hear the other side

Natural justice — right to be heard before adverse
action

Nemo judex in causa sua

No one can be judge in his own
cause

Rule against bias; applies to APFC conducting 7A

Actus non facit reum nisi
mens sit rea

The act does not make a person
guilty unless the mind is also
guilty

Criminal intent (mens rea) usually required; EPF S.14
strict liability subset

Res judicata

A matter adjudged (cannot be
reopened)

Once 7A/CGIT order becomes final, same dispute
cannot be re-agitated

Res sub judice

A matter under judicial
consideration

Bar on parallel proceedings — can't file fresh writ
when CGIT is seized

Stare decisis

Stand by what is decided
(binding precedent)

SC decisions bind HCs; HC decisions bind subordinate
tribunals

Ratio decidendi

The reason for the decision

Part of judgment that binds future cases; obiter dicta
is persuasive but not binding

Ultra vires

Beyond the powers

A rule/order outside statutory delegation is struck
down

Intra vires

Within the powers

Opposite — rule/order within delegated authority

Locus standi

Place of standing (right to sue)

Only a person 'aggrieved' can challenge; expanded by
PIL

Ex post facto

After the fact (retrospective)

Generally disfavoured; Art 20(1) bars retrospective
penal laws

Ignorantia juris non
excusat

Ignorance of law is no excuse

Employer cannot plead ignorance of EPF Act

Suo motu

On its own motion

CIC / tribunal can take cognisance without application

Prima facie

At first sight / on the face of it

Initial threshold — enough material to proceed (not
proof beyond doubt)

Certiorari / Mandamus /
Prohibition

Prerogative writs

Certiorari quashes; Mandamus commands; Prohibition
stops

MNEMONIC
AAP · NJCS · Res-J · Stare · Ratio · Ultra · Ignorantia · Prima-Facie → 8 most-tested maxims
